Look to Step 27 now 1-26 are effectively completed or no longer required. We are working on occlusion now.

### 25 Oct 2026 (occlusion plan after Step 36; written by Claude in a planning session, for Claude Code)
Step 36 showed that pose is not the cause of the false hides: against the true table-plane geometry (oracle) the false-hide rate is 0.0% with either pose, while the model's own depth gives 20-42% on V0/V3/V4. The model bias is row-dependent (top of frame reads 6-28 cm off, bottom 1-8 cm) and the hand at V2/441 reads farther than the panel (a close-range limit). Three global scale/offset fixes did not move the failure frames. Do not try more global per-frame scale/offset or anchor-fit variants.

Work in this order. Steps 37 and 38 are offline on existing clips and can run in parallel with Sahaj recording the far clips (Step 39).
1. **Step 37:** dense table-plane correction (offline, GPU, existing clips).
2. **Step 38:** one-off 518 input-size diagnostic (offline, GPU, existing clips).
3. **Step 39:** far-range capture sheet. ASK SAHAJ. Do not capture yourself.
4. **Step 40:** evaluate the far clips with and without the Step 37 correction, then apply the decision rule.
5. **Step 41:** trimmed hand/object labelling (replaces the full Step 32) on the far clips.
6. **Step 45 (next, set by Sahaj 25 Oct after Step 44):** make the Step 44 A+L variant (relative model, mode A, base-line reference, margin `max(0.10 m, 0.10 x Z)`) usable in the live tracker with the 0.60 x 0.33707 m panel, then Sahaj runs live tests at different distances and occluders. Do this before any further offline step. Later offline steps (per-frame miss breakdown with pixel-area scoring, more labels, 3-5 m capture, panel-crop depth) are written after the live results come in.
7. **Steps 46-49 (set by Sahaj 25 Oct evening, after the Step 45 live runs):** first Step 46 (1.0 x 0.5625 m panel, side-by-side live depth view, per-frame tag logging, review of the live runs), then Step 47 (tracking stability: smoothing, source policy, map), then Step 48 (depth edge or segmentation guidance), Step 49 only if needed. See "Sahaj's live-run notes" just before Step 46.
8. **Step 48 work order (set by Sahaj 26 Oct, NEXT):** see "Step 48 work order" just before Step 48c. Task 0 (floor map) is cancelled: write and check the 48a and 48b code (tasks 1-2) on the existing map and recordings with `--replay-run`, then report (task 3).
9. **Step 50 (set by Sahaj 26 Oct afternoon; DO THIS NEXT, before the Step 48 work order in item 8: integration first, depth refinement after):** projection array of 4 panels with yaw/scale and gesture control from the laptop camera in a separate thread. See Step 50 after Step 49. Integration first; speed and occlusion refinement after Sahaj sees the performance.

Standing decisions from this session:
- The occlusion method must generalise to any occluder (person, box, chair, thin objects), not only hands. Do not add hand-specific code unless Sahaj approves it after Step 40. If a segmentation aid is ever needed, prefer a class-agnostic one.
- Sahaj cannot print a larger tag now. Keep the 0.17 m tag. For the far clips use a config copy with a lower `tag.min_side_px` (see Step 39). Do not edit `config-logitech.json`, the baseline map or the existing calibration.
- The flat-surface assumption (tag lies flat on a large flat surface, world Z=0) is allowed for calibration and testing. For production it may be used only as a gated prior (see Step 37, "Production design").
- Judge every change on the same scored set, not on V3/26 and V2/441 alone. Always report the miss rate next to the false-hide rate: a method that never occludes gets a perfect false-hide score.
- Far-range captures now exist (25 Oct, see Step 39 Result): `runs/20261025T000122-210c1bb8` (F-occ, hand and measuring-tape occlusion, mostly about 2 m from the projection) and `runs/20261025T000913-e058a920` (F-empty, no intentional occlusion, stationary poses 1.5-3 m). Treat F-empty as the false-hide gate and F-occ as the occlusion clip. The F0-F4 names in Steps 39-41 map onto these two recordings.
- New panel size for the far clips: 0.60 m wide and 0.33707 m high (Sahaj's value, chosen to mimic 16:9). Use it only in the far config copy. Keep the old 0.20 x 0.12 m panel for the old clips so they stay comparable with Step 36.
- Target regime (stated by Sahaj, 25 Oct): operation from a few metres out to about 5-6 m, possibly with the tag on the floor so the camera looks down on it. The tag is for initial grounding (pose, metric scale, plane); landmarks must carry pose and the plane when the tag is not visible. A 0.17 m tag is only about 20 px at 5 m and will not be detected, so at that range rely on landmarks built with the tag visible, or a larger tag later. Depth margins must scale with distance (use 0.15 m, 0.20 m and 10% of depth as the reference values until labelled scoring says otherwise).
- Housekeeping: set the patch-median edge rejection (`anchor_sample` use in `DepthPrototype.render`) to default off. It raised V2 mean occluded fraction from 6.8% to 12.4% and moved neither failure frame. Keep the code and tests. Log it in section 6.

### Where we are (23 Oct, after Steps 18-21; 74 tests pass; everything below is default off)

- Done: Steps 1-4, 6, 9, 9b, 11-21. Not done: Steps 5, 7, 8, 10 (Step 10 is replaced by Step 16). Hardware gate is closed except as listed below.
- **Step 18 (dead-window trace):** the correct keypoint is usually present (25-46 of about 95 landmarks have one within 3 px), but 83-93% of those correct pairs fail the Hamming gate of 60 because correct-pair distance is about 88-109 bits. Tracker IDs are mostly wrong even before the windows (for example 0 of 2.2 correct before dead window 2). No window has a landmark pose at its first frame: dead windows have fewer than 12 mapped inputs; the productive window lacks RANSAC inliers. Dead window 2 (3908-3918 s) also has a detector gap (25 of 74 landmarks have any keypoint in the window).
- **Step 19 (loosen the gate), pooled over the 8 Step 13 windows (default 25.7%):** Hamming 128 (no limit) 31.8%, median 1.77 px, p95 5.38 px, 2 frames over 10 px, no dead window moves. Hamming 128 plus radius 8 or 6: 48.3% and 49.3%, p95 9.1 and 12.5 px, 17 and 33 frames over 10 px, windows 1-3 at 38-97%. **Geometry-first, radius 12, Hamming 128: 66.9%, median 2.75 px, p95 11.16 px, 37 frames over 10 px, windows 1-3 at 90-99%.** A frozen-prior check passed (the reference panel moves 94-291 px inside windows 1-3 while median error is 2.75 px). Windows 4-5 (3908-3918 s) stay 0% in every setting.
- **Step 20 (re-check inherited IDs):** negative. Availability falls to 22.8% (default gate), 7.7% (Hamming 128) and 16.0% (geometry-first). Stale-ID lock-in is not supported as the cause. `revalidate_existing` stays off.
- **Step 21 (grace cap):** there is no cap. A seed older than about 0.25 s never produces a pose, because the stored pose is used as an extra start only when landmark RANSAC already has a candidate. Grace above about 0.25 s adds nothing on this clip. No new field added.
- **Cautions on the 66.9% result:** windows 1-3 overlap (start 3886.1, 3887.8, 3888.6 s), so they are one 20 s stretch measured three times. Only three distinct stretches exist in the tested windows: 3886-3906 s works, 3908-3918 s is dead, 3925-3946 s works. Everything comes from one clip, the error reference is the tag, and the false-pose proxy is about 7% of supported frames (37 of 538) for geometry-first.
- Reading of the evidence: descriptors are nearly useless on this setup (ORB works only as a corner detector). Geometry (project, then take the nearest keypoint) is the best lever. The open problems are the accuracy tail, the detector gap at 3908-3918 s, the missing whole-clip measurement, and the pose being invalid at the first frame of a hand-off.

### Work in this order (offline unless noted)

1. **Step 22: look at the frames of the dead stretch (3908-3918 s) and its neighbours** (diagnosis; no code change).
2. **Step 23: whole-clip sliding-window evaluation** of the top settings (default, Hamming 128, geometry-first radius 12), with per-stretch results and a false-pose rate.
3. **Step 24: tail control for geometry-first** (accept only trusted guided matches; one change per run; target p95 at most 10 px and at most about 2% of supported frames over 10 px).
4. **Step 25: hand-off continuity** (the pose is never valid at the first frame inside a window).
5. **Step 26: patch-based (NCC) refinement**, only if Step 24 cannot control the tail. Needs a rebuild from the existing build recording.
6. Then Step 7 (live performance) and Step 8 (physical acceptance); both need the camera.

**Hardware gate (23 Oct, revised):** a capture is justified only for (a) a validation clip with locked auto-exposure, auto-focus and auto-white-balance, (b) adding visible texture to the scene and rebuilding the map, if Step 22 shows the dead stretch is a featureless view, or (c) physical Step 8 trials. Step 23 must be logged before (a) or (b). Describe exactly what to capture and ask Sahaj. Do not start a capture yourself. Do not enable `mapping.multiview_descriptor_bank`, `features.id_revalidation` or `features.guided_matching.revalidate_existing` in any config.

Keep the run provenance check from the older blocks below (map SHA-256 `2e45c8ea...bbe20a3`, calibration SHA-256 `e70a5bee...cd005b751`, clip `runs/20261019T091439-6ea9d598`, tag 0.17 m). ## Previous priority block (23 Oct, after Steps 13-17; superseded, kept for history)

### Where we were (23 Oct, after Steps 6 and 13-17)

- Done: Steps 1-4, 6, 9, 9b, 11-17. Not done: Steps 5, 7, 8, 10 (Step 10 is replaced by the trimmed Step 16). 71 tests pass. Hardware gate is closed.
- Best landmark-only availability so far: 26% pooled over the Step 13 windows with guided matching (joint, guided on, Step 17 defaults: 25.7% supported, median 1.45 px, p95 6.39 px). Target is 95%. In the one productive stretch (about 3925-3946 s) guided matching reaches 71-99% (with prior grace 76-99%), and plain landmark-only guided reaches 84-98%.
- **Step 13 (tag-seeded hand-off, repaired):** five of eight windows (3886-3918 s) are 0% in all four variants, including joint with a tag-seeded prior and 2 s of valid tag lead. Time to first lost pose is 0 s: the landmark pose already fails on the first frame inside the window. So the tag seed does not prime landmark tracking. Landmark-only guided was higher than joint guided in the productive windows (84% and 95% against 71% and 88%).
- **Step 14 (dead-region diagnosis):** the dead bins (3880-3919 s, bins 388-391) are not a coverage hole (77-95 visible landmarks per bin, camera 0.03-0.12 m from a build pose, brightness 113-117, similar sharpness). They are an **identity failure**: bins 390 and 391 have 0 correct of 4,346 associations (3 of 2,475 and 0 of 1,871 within 3 px); fresh `orb_map` matches are only 11-18 per bin. Oracle hit rate at 3 px is 0.32-0.45 in dead bins and 0.60-0.71 in productive bins. Caveat: the association counts come from the landmark-only tag-withheld run (`runs/20261019T133214-ecda4960`), not from a joint run.
- **Step 15 (prior grace):** grace 0.25, 0.5 and 1.0 s give identical results, and joint guided windows improve from 71, 88, 92% to 76, 97, 99%. Identical results across three values suggest a cap (the 0.2 s frame-gap reset still applies) or an unused code path. Not yet checked.
- **Step 16 (paired accuracy, LK drift):** on the 143 frames valid in both runs, median error 1.74 to 1.48 px, p95 6.28 to 6.48 px. So the p95 concern was survivor bias, and guided matching adds 64 valid frames with p95 about 2.9 px. LK drift from correct starts (27 tracks, low power): within 3 px 96% at age 1-5, 84% at 21-50, 68% at 51+ (gate 5 px); gate 2 px gives 81% at 51+ but a worse p95. Guided-assigned matches themselves are noisy: median 6.4 px, 15% within 3 px.
- **Step 17 (parameter sweep):** defaults (radius 12 px, Hamming 60, ratio 0.75) are close to best. Radius 6: 27.7% supported but p95 10.3 px and 12 frames over 10 px. Ratio 0.6: 20.0%, p95 3.9 px, no frame over 10 px. Hamming 40 and 50 lower availability. **No setting loosened the Hamming limit above 60.** All pooled fractions include the five dead windows.
- Reading of the evidence: parameter tuning cannot reach 95%. Two untested explanations remain for the dead region: (1) the Hamming gate of 60 rejects most correct pairs, because correct-pair distance is about 91-113 (Step 9 and 9b); (2) guided matching runs only on features with no landmark ID (`landmark_id is None`), and in the dead bins features already carry wrong IDs, so guided matching never sees them.
- Reporting hygiene: Step 6 fixes are in (evidence return, startup separated, provenance with code hash). Runs made before 23 Oct have no `code_sha256`, so `same_code` fails for them by design.

2. **Step 19: loosen the guided descriptor gate and add a geometry-first option** (default off).
3. **Step 20: re-check existing IDs against the prior projection on every feature, every frame** (default off).
4. **Step 21: prior grace cap check** (small test and fix).
5. Re-run the Step 13 protocol after each change (one change per run), with the provenance check quoted.
6. Then Step 7 (live performance, needs the camera) and Step 8 (physical acceptance). A second recording with locked camera settings is needed as a validation clip before any default-on decision. Ask Sahaj first.

## Previous priority block (22-23 Oct, kept for history; its Steps 13-17 are done)

### Where we were (23 Oct, before Steps 13-17)

- Done: Steps 1-4, 9, 9b, 11, 12. Step 10 was skipped (see Step 16 for the trimmed version). Steps 5-8 are not started. 68 tests pass.
- Findings so far: the tag path works (86.9% valid). Map geometry and BA are fine and BA is now deterministic. ORB finds keypoints near the right places, but descriptors are close to random on this setup (median about 91 bits even at 0-5 degrees view change). Step 9b ruled out coverage and map-building defects. Crops show featureless surfaces and a brightness change between recordings (landmark 27802: 124 vs 220). So global descriptor matching is the weak link.
- Guided matching (Step 11) helps: cold-start landmark-only validity 17.77% to 26.61%. It stays off by default. Best landmark-only result so far is still far below the 95% target.
- **Step 12 has a protocol flaw (found 23 Oct, see Step 13).** All hand-off runs used `pose.mode = "landmark_only"` (verified in the six run `config.json` files). In `ar_projection/pose.py` the tag is used only when `pose.mode != "landmark_only"`. So the tag never seeded a prior outside the windows. The "hand-off" runs were landmark-only over the whole clip, with windows only choosing where metrics are measured. The Step 12 numbers (36% to 61.7%, and the v2 table) are therefore **landmark-only results in windows, not tag-to-landmark hand-offs**. They remain valid measurements of guided matching versus baseline in landmark-only mode, and they are **not** evidence about the intended use. The "dead regions" in v1 and the single "productive region" in v2 were selected by landmark-only validity, so they say nothing yet about the tag-seeded case.
- Possible other limits, not yet tested: the prior is dropped the moment one frame fails (`self.previous = None` in `pipeline.py`); all evidence uses one 72 s clip.

### Run provenance check (verified 23 Oct, repeat before trusting any new run)

Every run in Steps 11-12 (`20261022T183859-e386b622`, `T184029-9f0eb386`, `T185641-f093e789`, `T185808-ebb84f6e`, `T193512-07f7fa17`, `T193600-ab654a47`) used: map `data/logitech-landmark-map.npz` (SHA-256 `2e45c8ea67330a738d2a03575c508860833f2b1961d4d3ab50510c8f8bbe20a3`, file unchanged since 19 Oct), calibration SHA-256 `e70a5bee9591f6196dbdfbb12c0366e9c9ab41b559ca692e38402f7cd005b751`, tag 0.17 m ID 0, input `runs/20261019T091439-6ea9d598/input.avi` (recorded timestamps), mapping mode `localize`, LK enabled, guided matching off for the baseline runs and on for the guided runs. This is the correct combination. Experimental maps in `data/` (`*-multiview*.npz`, `*-repro-check.npz`) must not be used for tracking comparisons unless a step says so. Before every new run, check the run's `config.json` and `metadata.json` against this list and quote the check in the log entry. Also record `pose.mode` and the tag-withhold setting, because the Step 12 flaw came from `pose.mode`.

### Work in this order (all offline on the existing clip and map; no camera)

1. **Step 13: repair the hand-off protocol** (tag allowed outside windows, withheld inside; `pose.mode = "joint"`). Then rerun baseline versus guided on windows spread across the whole clip.
2. **Step 14: dead-region diagnosis** by 10 s time bin (visible landmarks, oracle hit rate, guided opportunities, tag visibility).
3. **Step 15: prior grace period** (keep the last pose only as a search seed for short failures), default off.
4. **Step 16: paired accuracy and trimmed LK drift** (replaces Step 10 as written).
5. **Step 17: parameter sweeps for guided matching, joint-fit option, default-on decision.**
6. Then Step 6 (reporting fix), then Step 8 with a physical tag-covering test. Step 5 sweeps fold into Step 17.

**Hardware gate (revised 23 Oct):** no new capture or map until Step 14 is logged. A capture is justified only if Step 14 shows a coverage hole (few map landmarks visible and few oracle hits in the failing regions), or after Steps 13-17 are done and the section 4 criteria are still not met. A second recording will also be needed later as an independent validation clip, because all results so far use one clip. For any capture or live run, lock auto-exposure, auto-focus and auto-white-balance on the camera and record the settings (the brightness difference in Step 9b suggests they may have been adapting). When the gate opens, stop and ask Sahaj. Describe exactly what to capture. Do not start a capture yourself.

Earlier notes still apply: do not enable `mapping.multiview_descriptor_bank` or `features.id_revalidation` in any config (both measured worse). The BA budget is now deterministic.

## 0. How to use this file

1. Read the CURRENT PRIORITY block above, then section 1 (rules) and section 2 (state) in full.
2. Follow the order in the CURRENT PRIORITY block. Where it is silent, find the first unchecked step in section 5. Do that step only. Do not skip ahead.
3. When a step finishes, tick its box, fill in its "Result" line, and add an entry to section 7 (progress log) and, if code structure changed, section 6 (architecture change log).
4. If a step needs the physical camera, stop and ask Sahaj to do the capture. Do not invent results.
5. If evidence contradicts this plan, record the contradiction in section 7 and propose a revised plan. Do not silently follow a plan the data has disproved.

## 1. Ground rules

- Python: always run `.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe` from the workspace root (PowerShell). Never rely on the shell prompt or on running a `.py` file directly.
- Workspace root: `C:\Users\sahaj\OneDrive\Desktop\Uni_Resources\Year_4\AMME5710\Post-Game-AR\sahaj_ar_projection`.
- The Git root is the parent `Post-Game-AR`. The working tree has large uncommitted changes. Do **not** run `git reset`, `git checkout .`, `git clean` or `git stash`. Commit only if Sahaj asks.
- Never overwrite or delete these baseline artifacts: `data/logitech-landmark-map.npz`, `logitech-camera-calibration.npz`, `config-logitech.json`, `runs/20261019T091439-6ea9d598`, `runs/logitech-comparison.json`. Copy a config to a new name for experiments. Write outputs under new names.
- Keep `config-logitech.json` as the reference configuration. Experimental flags default to **off** so existing behavior and the 40 existing tests stay unchanged.
- Run the full test suite after every code change: `.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests -v`. All tests must pass before a step counts as done.
- Change one thing per experiment. Re-run the same 1,131-frame comparison after each change. Compare availability and reference error together. More accepted poses are not success if reference error or false poses get worse.
- Distinguish diagnostics from solver inputs. The tag reference pose may be used **offline** for scoring. It must never feed the tested landmark solver.
- Do not claim a result that was not measured. Quote numbers from run artifacts with the run ID.
- Do not weaken quality gates (12 inliers, 4 cells, 3 px) as a first fix. The evidence says correct matches are missing, not that the gates are too strict.
- Keep the current tracker. Full SLAM replacement is a later decision, only if controlled experiments show a limit that small fixes cannot address.
- Do not edit this file's section 2 numbers. Add new results to section 7 instead.

## 2. State snapshot (as of 19 Oct 2026)

Project: video-see-through AR prototype. A webcam views a virtual 0.20 x 0.12 m panel fixed to a mapped workspace. AprilTag 36h11 ID 0 (0.17 m) is the anchor. Natural ORB landmarks (frozen map, 95 points) should keep tracking when the tag is hidden.

Active setup: Logitech camera index 0, backend `msmf`, 640x480, `config-logitech.json`, `logitech-camera-calibration.npz`, `data/logitech-landmark-map.npz`.

Controlled comparison on clip `runs/20261019T091439-6ea9d598` (1,131 frames, 72.4 s, original timestamps):

| Variant | Valid fraction |
|---|---:|
| tag_only | 86.91% |
| lk_periodic (best landmark variant) | 17.77% |
| orb_only | 4.07% |
| lk_orb | 4.42% |
| joint | 86.91% (tag poses only, zero landmark/joint poses) |

Target is at least 95%. `selected_landmark_variant` is null.

Key diagnostic: in the first 180 frames of the lk_periodic trial, the median frame had 26 candidate map associations but only 4 within 3 px of the tag-reference projection (8 within 5 px, 11 within 10 px). No frame had 12 consistent associations. PnP RANSAC needs 12, so it fails. Raising thresholds would not help.

Other facts:
- LK survival (about 98%) does not prove correct map identities.
- Live display rate measured 15.67 FPS (target 20). Latency p95 78 ms (target 100). Not the current priority.
- BA `x_scale="jac"` fix is already in `landmarks.py`. It is not the current problem.
- 40 automated tests pass.

## 3. Primary hypothesis (to test, not to assume)

Wrong or stale `landmark_id` values lock in and are never corrected.

Code facts verified by reading the source on 19 Oct 2026 (line numbers may drift; search by name):

1. `ar_projection/pipeline.py`, in `Pipeline.process`, the loop that removes map IDs whose reprojection disagrees with the pose by more than `ransac_reprojection_threshold_px` runs **only inside `if pose.valid:`**. In landmark-only mode most frames are invalid, so the cleanup rarely runs.
2. `ar_projection/landmarks.py`, `associate()`, keeps any inherited `landmark_id` that exists in the map and is not already used. It does no geometric check. It only runs descriptor matching for features whose `landmark_id` is `None`.
3. `ar_projection/features.py`: LK flow copies `landmark_id` forward (`association_source="lk"`). ORB redetection can carry an old `landmark_id` to a new detection when descriptor and 5 px spatial checks pass (`association_source="orb_redetection"`).
4. Net effect: a bad ID, once assigned, rides LK indefinitely during LOST. Because it already has an ID, fresh descriptor matching never replaces it.
5. The map stores one descriptor per landmark (from its first accepted view), so fresh descriptor matches from other views are also weak. This is a second, separate hypothesis (Step 4).

Competing hypotheses are listed in `CONTEXT.md` section 17: appearance/view coverage, bad map geometry, scene or camera change, RANSAC limitation. Step 2 data should discriminate between them before Step 3 changes behavior.

## 4. Success criteria for the whole sequence

Landmark-only (tag withheld) on the 1,131-frame clip:
- Valid fraction at least 95%, with at least 400 reference corner samples.
- Reference corner error median under 5 px and p95 under 10 px.
- Headless throughput at least 20 FPS (shortlist only).
- No degradation of the tag-only baseline.

Then physical acceptance (tag covered), 2-minute and 5-minute runs, then optional depth occlusion. Intermediate progress is acceptable and must be recorded honestly even if the final bar is not met.

## 5. Step sequence

Each step lists: goal, tasks, files, tests, done-when, hardware need. Tick boxes as you go.

### Step 1: freeze baseline (no code change)
- [x] Goal: make every later comparison reproducible.
- Tasks:
  - Copy `config-logitech.json` to `config-exp.json` (all experiments use copies).
  - Record the code state: if Git is usable, `git status --short` and `git diff --stat` saved to `runs/baseline-code-state.txt`. Also save SHA-256 of `ar_projection/*.py` to the same file. Do not commit.

  - Re-run the baseline comparison to a **new** name to confirm it reproduces: `.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m ar_projection.experiments compare .\runs\20261019T091439-6ea9d598 --config config-exp.json --output .\runs\baseline-repro.json`
  - Compare `baseline-repro.json` with `runs/logitech-comparison.json`. Valid fractions should match within a few frames (background mapping is not used in replay, so differences should be tiny). Note any difference.
- Done when: repro numbers match, code-state file exists.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-19. `config-exp.json` created. `runs/baseline-code-state.txt` written (git status/diff + SHA-256 of `ar_projection/*.py`). All 40 tests pass. `runs/baseline-repro.json` reproduces `runs/logitech-comparison.json` with **zero** difference on every variant's `valid_pose_fraction` (tag_only 0.8691423519009726, lk_periodic 0.17771883289124668, orb_only 0.040671971706454466, lk_orb 0.04420866489832007, joint 0.8691423519009726; `selected_landmark_variant` null in both). See section 7.

### Step 2: make correspondence quality observable (instrument only; no behavior change)
- [x] Goal: learn where wrong associations come from before changing anything.
- Tasks:
  - Add an opt-in per-observation association dump. Suggested config: `logging.association_dump` (bool, default false), written to `assoc.jsonl` in the run directory, one row per frame containing a list of observations.
  - Per observation record: `track_id`, `landmark_id`, `association_source` (`lk`, `orb_redetection`, `orb_map`), descriptor distance and ratio (when freshly matched), track age, pixel, predicted map pixel under the **solver's** pose if valid (else null), RANSAC inlier flag, and whether the ID was inherited or fresh this frame.
  - Keep dump code out of the hot path when disabled.
  - Write `scripts/score_associations.py` (offline). For each frame with a valid tag reference (RMS <= 1 px), project the map point of each association with the tag-reference pose and compute pixel error. Aggregate by association source, by time window (10 s bins), by track age bucket and by landmark ID. Output JSON plus a short markdown summary.
  - Also compute, per frame, how many map points the reference pose projects inside the image (visible set, ignoring occlusion), and how many of those have an association within 3 px (recall) versus total associations (precision proxy).
- Files: `ar_projection/config.py` (new optional field), `ar_projection/features.py` / `landmarks.py` (expose distance/ratio), `ar_projection/diagnostics.py` (writer), new `scripts/score_associations.py`, `tests/test_experiments.py` (schema test).
- Tests: dump disabled leaves `frames.jsonl` byte-identical; dump enabled produces finite JSON with expected keys; scorer on a synthetic run returns known precision.
- Done when: one dump run on the clip exists and the scorer report answers: (a) which source has the worst precision, (b) whether precision is bad from frame 0 or degrades after LOST, (c) whether visible-point recall is low for some views.
- Decision after this step (record in section 7):
  - If inherited LK or orb_redetection IDs dominate wrong associations: go to Step 3.
  - If fresh `orb_map` descriptor matches are mostly wrong or recall is low in specific views: do Step 4 first.
  - If associations are mostly correct but PnP still fails: investigate RANSAC/solver (new step, add it here).
  - If errors are systematic and view-dependent even for correct IDs: suspect map geometry or camera/scene change; discuss with Sahaj.
- Hardware: none (uses the existing clip).
- Result: Done 2026-10-19/20. Implemented `logging.association_dump` (default false), `assoc.jsonl` writer, descriptor distance/ratio now returned by `match_descriptors` and recorded on fresh `orb_map` matches, and `scripts/score_associations.py`. 42/42 tests pass (40 original + 2 new: dump-disabled leaves `frames.jsonl` unchanged; dump-enabled schema + scorer catches a known planted wrong ID). Ran lk_periodic/withhold-tag with the dump on the full baseline clip (run `20261019T133214-ecda4960`) and scored it. See section 7 for the numbers and decision: **fresh `orb_map` descriptor matches are the worst performer (96% wrong) and overall recall is very low (4.5%) from frame 0 onward → go to Step 4 first**, per this step's own decision rule.

### Step 3: map-ID lifecycle experiment
- [x] Goal: test whether expiring or revalidating suspect IDs raises availability without hurting accuracy.
- Tasks:
  - Add `features.id_revalidation` config block, default `mode: "off"`. Modes:
    - `loss_clear`: while state is LOST, clear `landmark_id` on features whose source is `lk` or `orb_redetection`; keep the image track so `associate()` can re-match by descriptor.
    - `age_expire`: clear an ID that has gone N frames (or T seconds) without being confirmed by a valid pose or inlier status.
    - `descriptor_check`: for an inherited ID, compare the feature descriptor against the map descriptor; clear if Hamming distance exceeds a threshold.
  - Keep the existing valid-pose reprojection cleanup unchanged.
  - Implement in a single small function so the pipeline diff is small. Log how many IDs each mode clears per frame (`ids_cleared_by_revalidation`).
  - Run one mode at a time on the clip: `.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m ar_projection.experiments compare ... --config config-exp-<mode>.json --output .\runs\exp3-<mode>.json`, then run `score_associations.py` on the lk_periodic run.
- Tests (add to `tests/test_experiments.py` or a new file):
  - wrong inherited ID is cleared during LOST in `loss_clear`;
  - fresh descriptor matching then assigns a correct ID;
  - map IDs stay one-to-one;
  - a correct LK track keeps its ID when pose stays valid;
  - default `off` reproduces baseline behavior exactly.
- Done when: a table in section 7 compares baseline versus each mode: valid fraction, reference median/p95, ID precision, IDs cleared. Keep the best mode only if reference error does not degrade.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-20, no hardware. Implemented `features.id_revalidation` (`off`/`loss_clear`/`age_expire`/`descriptor_check`, default `off`) as a single small function `revalidate_ids()` (`ar_projection/landmarks.py`) called from `pipeline.py` right before `associate()`; added `FeatureObservation.frames_since_confirmed` (reset to 0 by the existing valid-pose reprojection check, incremented by LK/carried by redetection) to support `age_expire`. 6 new tests cover each mode, the "never touches a fresh orb_map id" invariant, and that `off` leaves `ids_cleared_by_revalidation`=0 (53/53 tests pass). Ran all 3 modes plus `off` on the lk_periodic/withhold-tag clip (same baseline map, same 1,131-frame clip as Steps 1-2) and scored each — **all three modes make both availability and precision worse than doing nothing**, in rough proportion to how aggressively they clear ids. See section 7 for the full table and interpretation. Decision: do not enable `features.id_revalidation` in any config; keep the baseline's behavior of never clearing inherited ids on this map/recording.

### Step 4: map coverage and descriptor representation (only if Step 2 or 3 says so)
- [x] Goal: improve fresh matching from other views.
- Tasks (choose based on Step 2 evidence):
  - Analyze failures by camera view: projected visible landmarks, image-cell coverage, per-point support and baseline.
  - Try a small bounded multi-view descriptor bank per landmark (for example up to 3 descriptors from well-separated views). Requires a map schema change: bump the map format version, keep the loader able to read the old format, never overwrite the baseline map.
  - Or re-map along the operating path (needs hardware): `--mapping-mode build --map .\data\logitech-landmark-map-experiment.npz --save-map --record-input`, then point a config copy at the new map.
  - More keyframes or points are not automatically better. Test a spatially distributed, well-conditioned subset.
- Done when: comparison on the same clip shows the effect of one change at a time.
- Hardware: only for re-mapping or new test clips. Ask Sahaj first.
- Result: Done 2026-10-19/20, no hardware used. (a) Analyzed the existing frozen map/dump offline: per-point `support` is almost uniform (54/63 active landmarks at the floor of 3 confirming views; corr(support,precision)≈-0.01 — too narrow a range to explain anything), `rms` has a weak negative correlation with precision (r≈-0.29), and 32/95 landmarks are visible under the reference pose at some point but were **never** associated once in the whole clip. (b) Implemented a bounded multi-view descriptor bank (map schema extension, additive/optional npz keys, `mapping.multiview_descriptor_bank` default 0 — see section 6; 45/45 tests pass). (c) Rebuilt a 169-landmark experimental map by replaying the original build recording `runs/20261019T090841-72509636` (found via code search, no new capture needed) with the bank set to 2; discovered along the way that `MapBuilder`'s bundle-adjustment refinement uses a **wall-clock** time budget (`mapping.refinement_time_budget_s`), so re-running the exact same build-mode replay is not reproducible under machine load (a second rebuild attempt hit `time_budget_exceeded` and failed to produce a map at all — recorded as a contradiction, not silently retried into a cherry-picked result). Worked around this by deriving an exact-match control map from the successful 169-landmark build (same points/ids/descriptors/support/rms, extra-descriptor fields stripped), so the comparison below isolates only the bank. (d) Ran both maps through the lk_periodic/withhold-tag dump on the full 1,131-frame clip and scored them. **Result: the naive bank makes things worse, not better** — see section 7 for numbers and root-cause diagnosis (Lowe's ratio test, run against the enlarged flat candidate pool, rejects far more correct matches because the gap between best and second-best shrinks as the pool triples). Decision: do not adopt the bank as implemented; a per-landmark best-distance aggregation before the ratio test is the likely fix, but that is a new design decision — stopped here to check in rather than implementing it unasked.

### Step 9: oracle diagnostic (detector repeatability vs descriptor distinctiveness vs coverage)
- [x] Goal: find out which link in the chain "landmark is visible, ORB detects it, descriptor matches the map" breaks. This decides whether the problem is the detector, the matcher, the map coverage, or something else.
- Priority: **first.** Offline only. No behavior change to the tracker.
- Tasks:
  - Write `scripts/oracle_diagnostic.py`. Input: the recorded clip with its `frames.jsonl`, the existing map, and a run produced with `logging.association_dump` plus the per-frame detected ORB keypoints and descriptors. If the current dump does not store all detected keypoints and descriptors per frame, add an opt-in dump (`logging.keypoint_dump`, default false) or recompute them offline by running the same `FeatureTracker.detect` on the recorded frames with the same config and masks. State which you did.
  - Use only frames with a valid tag reference (RMS <= 1 px), the same filter as `scripts/score_associations.py`.
  - For each such frame and each map landmark, project it with the tag-reference pose. Keep landmarks that land inside the image with at least a 10 px border and are not inside the tag mask.
  - For each kept landmark, find the nearest detected ORB keypoint within 3 px (also record 5 px and 10 px). Record:
    1. **Detector hit:** whether a keypoint exists within the radius (repeatability).
    2. **Hamming distance** between that keypoint's descriptor and the landmark's stored descriptor (the "true pair" distance).
    3. **Distractor distances:** the distance from the same keypoint to the other landmarks' descriptors (best other, and the ratio best/second-best as the matcher would see it). Also the best-distance for random non-matching keypoints in the same frame, for a baseline distribution.
    4. **Would the current matcher accept it:** apply the exact `match_descriptors` rules (ratio 0.75, max distance 60, mutual, one-to-one) to the true pair, and record which rule rejects it when it fails.
    5. **Keypoint selection:** whether the keypoint survived the grid quota and the 500 cap, or only existed in the raw candidate set before selection. If the raw candidate set is unavailable, say so.
  - Aggregate. Report each of these, with counts, as JSON plus a short markdown summary:
    - detector hit rate overall, by 10 s time bin, by image cell (4x6 grid), by landmark ID, by scale/octave if available, and by camera-to-landmark distance and viewing angle change relative to the keyframe that supplied the stored descriptor;
    - true-pair Hamming distance distribution (median, p90) versus the distractor distribution and versus the 60 limit;
    - fraction of true pairs the matcher would accept, and the breakdown of rejection reasons (distance limit, ratio, mutual, one-to-one);
    - the same quantities restricted to landmarks that were "visible but never associated" (the 32 landmarks noted in Step 4).
  - Also report map-geometry consistency as a side check: for true pairs with a keypoint within 10 px, the pixel offset between keypoint and projected landmark, by view. A systematic view-dependent offset suggests map geometry or calibration error rather than matching error.
- Files: new `scripts/oracle_diagnostic.py`; optionally a small opt-in dump in `ar_projection/diagnostics.py` and `config.py`; tests in `tests/test_experiments.py` (synthetic scene with a known landmark, known keypoint and known descriptor distance, checks the hit and acceptance logic).
- Done when: the markdown report exists under `runs/` with the run ID, the clip ID and the map hash, and section 7 has an entry that fills in the decision table below.
- Decision table (record which row applies, quote the number):

  | Observation | Meaning | Next |
  |---|---|---|
  | Detector hit rate low (for example under 50%) for visible landmarks | ORB does not re-detect the mapped points. Detection settings or the grid quota drop them. | Test detection changes (FAST threshold, per-cell quota, keeping mapped-point regions, more levels) offline using this script as the metric. A new map would not help. |
  | Hit rate high, true-pair distance small (well under 60 and well below the distractors), but matcher still rejects many | Matcher rules reject correct pairs (ratio, mutual, one-to-one, ambiguity with repeated texture). | Look at the dominant rejection reason. Consider guided matching (Step 11) which removes the ambiguity. Tune one rule at a time in Step 5. |
  | Hit rate high, true-pair distance large for specific views, small for others | Stored single-view descriptor does not generalize across those views: coverage problem. | This is the case where new, deliberately varied capture is justified. Stop and ask Sahaj. Also compare against the multi-view bank result from Step 4. |
  | Hit rate high, distances small, but the keypoint-to-projection pixel offset is large and view-dependent | Map geometry, calibration or scene drift. | Investigate geometry before any recapture: held-out reprojection, focus/crop change, scene changes. |
  | Hit rate high, distances small, offsets small, matcher accepts most | The true matches exist and are accepted; the problem is elsewhere (distractor volume, ID lifecycle, solver). | Re-check the Step 2 precision numbers for a scoring error; then continue to Step 10. |

- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-20, offline only. Wrote `scripts/oracle_diagnostic.py`: recomputes ORB detection/compute offline (no `logging.keypoint_dump` added — stated choice, avoids touching the pipeline at all) on the same clip/config/map as the existing lk_periodic dump, using the same RMS<=1px tag-reference filter as `score_associations.py`. 4 new synthetic tests (known keypoint distances, known descriptor Hamming distances, a constructed "distractor closer than true pair" case) added to `tests/test_experiments.py`; 57/57 tests pass. Ran on the full clip (966 scored frames, 88,338 kept-landmark occurrences). **Decision table row 3 applies: coverage is the cause.** See section 7 for the full numbers and the filled decision table. Per the hardware gate above, stopping here to ask Sahaj rather than proceeding to Steps 10-12 or starting a capture.

### Step 9b: descriptor validity and coverage check (offline, before any capture)
- [x] Goal: decide whether the large true-pair distance from Step 9 is (A) a map-building defect (wrong or corrupted stored descriptor), (B) a measurement artifact (wrong "nearest keypoint" pairing, near-duplicate landmarks), or (C) a genuine view-coverage problem. Only (C) justifies a new capture.
- Why this step exists: Step 9 reported median true-pair Hamming distance 101 and best-other-landmark distance median 54. Random 256-bit descriptors differ by about 128 bits, and the best of about 94 random distances would be near 110. So 101 looks like "almost random", and 54 for the best distractor is much lower than random. Row 3 of the Step 9 decision table needs distance **small in some views and large in others**; the Step 9 report gives only overall numbers. The "true pair" was the nearest keypoint within 10 px, which may not be the same physical point (only 48.4% of occurrences have a keypoint within 3 px).
- Priority: **next.** Offline only. No tracker behavior change. Reuse `scripts/oracle_diagnostic.py` where possible, and write new scripts under `scripts/`. Do not overwrite `runs/oracle-diagnostic.json`; write new output files.
- Tasks (run all four; report each separately):
  1. **Tight-radius true pairs.** Recompute the Step 9 true-pair distance using only keypoints within **3 px** of the projected landmark (also report 2 px and 5 px). Report median, p10, p90, and the fraction under 30, 40 and 60 bits. Compare with the 10 px result. If the 3 px distances are much smaller, the Step 9 median was inflated by wrong pairings and the conclusion changes.
  2. **Descriptor self-consistency on the build recording** (decisive for hypothesis A). Use the map-building run `runs/20261019T090841-72509636` (the recording the 95-landmark map was built from) and the map `data/logitech-landmark-map.npz`. For each landmark, find the keyframe observations that supported it (the stored descriptor came from the first accepted view). In that **same keyframe image**, compute the ORB descriptor at the observed pixel and compare it with the stored descriptor. Then do the same for the other supporting keyframes. Report the distribution of Hamming distance for (i) the source view, which should be about 0 to 10 bits if the descriptor is stored correctly, and (ii) the other supporting views. If the source-view distance is not near zero for most landmarks, **map building stores the wrong descriptor** (check `MapBuilder` for track-identity mix-ups, descriptor taken from a different keypoint than the triangulated one, or reuse of a mutated array). In that case fix the defect and rebuild the map **from the existing build recording, no new capture**, then rerun the Step 9 numbers on the fixed map before anything else.
  3. **Distance versus viewing change** (decisive for hypothesis C). For each tight-radius true pair (task 1), compute the angle between the current camera ray to the landmark and the ray from the source keyframe, the ratio of camera-to-landmark distances (current versus source), and the in-plane rotation difference if available. Bin by these. Report median true-pair distance per bin. Coverage is the cause only if distance is small for views close to the source keyframe and rises clearly with view change. If distance is large even for views close to the source keyframe, the cause is (A) or (B), not coverage.
  4. **Distractor geometry.** For each rejection labelled `distractor_closer` in Step 9, record the **image distance between the projected true landmark and the projected distractor landmark** and their 3D distance. Report how many distractors are within 5, 10 and 20 px (or within a few cm) of the true landmark. If many are, the map contains near-duplicate or clustered landmarks on the same physical point or repeated texture. In that case consider merging near-duplicates or spatial-uniqueness pruning of the map (offline, new map file, baseline untouched) as the fix, instead of a capture. Also report the pairwise Hamming distance between stored descriptors of the 95 landmarks (median, p10, fraction under 40 bits) to see whether the map descriptors are far less diverse than random.
- Files: new `scripts/descriptor_validity_check.py` (tasks 1, 2, 3, 4 may be separate scripts or one script with sub-commands); tests in `tests/test_experiments.py` with a synthetic scene: a stored descriptor equal to the keyframe descriptor must give distance 0; a descriptor from a wrong keypoint must give a large distance; binning must place a known view change in the right bin; a planted near-duplicate landmark must be flagged.
- Done when: a report under `runs/` (JSON and markdown, with clip ID, build run ID and map SHA-256) and a section 7 entry that applies the decision table below.
- Decision table (record which row applies, quote the numbers):

  | Observation | Meaning | Next |
  |---|---|---|
  | Source-view self-consistency distance is not near zero for many landmarks (task 2) | (A) Map-building defect: wrong stored descriptor. | Fix `MapBuilder`, add a regression test, rebuild the map from the existing build recording (deterministic BA budget first), rerun Step 9 numbers. **No capture.** |
  | 3 px true-pair distances are much smaller than the 10 px result (task 1), and matcher acceptance rises sharply when only 3 px pairs are counted | (B) Step 9 was inflated by wrong nearest-keypoint pairings. | Re-interpret Step 9 with 3 px pairs. Revisit the matcher or guided matching (Step 11). **No capture.** |
  | Many `distractor_closer` landmarks lie within about 10 px or a few cm of the true landmark, or stored descriptors are far less diverse than random (task 4) | (B) Near-duplicate or clustered landmarks, repeated texture. | Offline map pruning or merging, spatial-uniqueness filter, then guided matching (Step 11). **No capture** unless pruning fails. |
  | Self-consistency is good, 3 px pairs are valid, and true-pair distance rises clearly with view change from the source keyframe while staying small near it (task 3) | (C) Genuine view-coverage problem. | **Hardware gate opens.** Stop and ask Sahaj. Describe the capture (below). |
  | Self-consistency is good, but true-pair distance is large even for views close to the source keyframe | Descriptor not repeatable even at nearly the same view: look at ORB settings (scale, orientation, blur, exposure), image preprocessing differences between the build and test recordings, camera auto-exposure or focus changes. | Investigate offline first (compare build and test frames of the same landmark). Ask Sahaj about camera settings. |

- If the gate opens, the capture recommendation to give Sahaj (adjust to the task 3 findings): same camera, focus, tag and scene; a wide translation arc plus moves closer and farther; varied angle and tilt; static background only (no hands or moving objects); many well-separated keyframes rather than clustered ones; deterministic BA budget in place first; save as a new map file and never overwrite `data/logitech-landmark-map.npz`; keep the existing clip as the comparison input.
- Hardware: none for this step.
- Result: Done 2026-10-23, offline only (plus two bug fixes along the way — see below). Fixed `MapBuilder`'s wall-clock BA deadline first (now `mapping.refinement_max_evaluations`, a deterministic residual-evaluation cap — see section 6), then verified a build-mode replay of `runs/20261019T090841-72509636` now reproduces `data/logitech-landmark-map.npz` bit-for-bit (same 95 ids, points, descriptors). Wrote `scripts/descriptor_validity_check.py` covering all 4 tasks, reusing `scripts/oracle_diagnostic.py`'s `collect_rows()`. Also fixed a real performance bug found while running this (`classify_pair` was recomputing a whole-frame backward descriptor match per landmark-hit instead of once per frame — tens of thousands of redundant calls) and a real correctness bug (the first provenance-recovery attempt drove `FeatureTracker`/`MapBuilder` by hand instead of the real `Pipeline`, and got 85 landmarks instead of 95 — switched to driving the actual `Pipeline` class, which now reproduces the frozen map exactly). 9 new synthetic tests across all 4 tasks; 62/62 pass.
- Runs (IDs / output files): `runs/step9b-report.json`, clip `runs/20261019T091439-6ea9d598` (966 scored frames, same RMS<=1px filter as Steps 2/9), build recording `runs/20261019T090841-72509636` (95 landmarks, 18 keyframes, exact match to the frozen map).
- Numbers (quote from `runs/step9b-report.json`):
  - **Task 1 (tight radius):** true-pair distance barely changes with a tighter pairing radius — 2px: median 96/p90 137/matcher-accepts 2.19% (n=31,338); 3px: 97/138/1.81% (n=42,751); 10px (Step 9's radius): 101/139/1.21% (n=72,172). Tightening the radius does **not** reveal small distances hiding under Step 9's loose 10px pairing.
  - **Task 2 (self-consistency on the build recording):** source view (the view whose descriptor is actually stored): median **0.0**, p90 **0.0** (n=84/95 landmarks had a keypoint within 2px; 1 was missing its own descriptor comparison trivially since distance-to-self is definitionally 0). Other supporting views **within the same build session**: median **58.0**, p90 125.0 (n=161). 58 observations (of 303 total feature_observations, matching the build's own reported count) had no ORB keypoint within 2px of the recorded pixel at all.
  - **Task 3 (distance vs. viewing-angle change from the source keyframe):** median true-pair distance by angle bin — 0-5°: 91 (n=1,159); 5-15°: 93 (n=19,315); 15-30°: 97 (n=9,135); 30+°: 102 (n=13,142). Flat, not rising — already near-random even in the 0-5° bin.
  - **Task 4 (distractor geometry):** of 66,720 `distractor_closer` occurrences, only 12.3% have the distractor within 2cm of the true landmark, 31.0% within 5cm, 40.2% within 10cm (median separation 30cm, p10 1.7cm) — most distractors are **not** spatially close. Stored descriptor pairwise Hamming distance across the 95 landmarks: median 114, p10 79, only 0.6% under 40 bits — the map's own descriptors are about as diverse as random, not unusually clustered.
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): this cleanly separates the four hypotheses.
  - **(A) map-building defect: ruled out.** Task 2's source-view self-consistency is exactly 0 — `MapBuilder` stores precisely the descriptor a fresh ORB computation gives at the recorded pixel in the recorded keyframe. No bug in track-identity bookkeeping or descriptor assignment.
  - **(B) measurement artifact: ruled out as the primary driver.** Task 1 shows tightening the pairing radius from 10px to 2px changes essentially nothing (101→96 median, 1.2%→2.2% acceptance) — Step 9's 10px pairing was not inflating the distance. Task 4 shows near-duplicate/clustered landmarks explain only a minority of `distractor_closer` cases (12-40% depending on threshold, median separation 30cm) and the map's own descriptors are about as diverse as random — not a map built from confusable near-duplicates.
  - **(C) genuine view-coverage problem: ruled out.** This is the key new finding. Task 3's whole premise — distance small near the source keyframe, rising with view change — does not hold: the median distance is already ~91 bits (close to the ~128-bit random expectation) in the *smallest* angle bin (0-5°) and only creeps up modestly from there (91→102 across the full range). A coverage fix (more/wider keyframes) targets exactly the failure mode this data says isn't happening.
  - **What the data does show:** a genuine gradient by *where* the comparison happens, not by viewing angle. Same pixel, same image (task 2 source view): 0 bits. Same landmark, a different keyframe *within the same build session* (task 2 other views, still governed by the build's own `min_baseline_m`/`min_parallax_deg` gates, i.e. genuinely small viewpoint changes): 58 bits — already notably degraded, but well under random. Same landmark, the separate **test clip recorded in a different session** (task 1/3, any viewing angle): ~91-139 bits — close to or at random. This points at the last decision-table row: ORB descriptors here are not even robust to the small viewpoint changes *within* one recording session (58 bits is already a lot of drift for "nearly the same view"), and whatever differs *between* the build and test recording sessions (lighting, exposure, focus, or just accumulated small scene/camera differences) makes it much worse again. Both the build-session drift and the cross-session gap point at ORB/image-conditions robustness, not at how many or how spread-out the keyframes are.
- Decision table row applied: **last row** — "Self-consistency is good, but true-pair distance is large even for views close to the source keyframe → descriptor not repeatable even at nearly the same view: look at ORB settings (scale, orientation, blur, exposure), image preprocessing differences between the build and test recordings, camera auto-exposure or focus changes." The hardware gate does **not** open — row 4 (coverage) is explicitly contradicted by task 3.
- Decision / next step: do not capture new data. Investigate offline first, as the table says: compare an actual build-session frame and a test-clip frame of the *same* landmark side by side (crop and look — brightness, blur, focus) before writing any more code. Ask Sahaj: were the build recording and the 1,131-frame test clip taken in the same lighting, with the same camera auto-exposure/focus behavior, and roughly the same time apart? If the two recordings differ in those respects, that is the leading explanation for both Step 9's and this step's numbers — cheaper to fix (retake a same-conditions test clip, or re-tune exposure/focus to be fixed rather than auto) than a new deliberately-varied capture, and it does not touch any tracker code.

### Step 10: LK drift diagnostic
- [ ] Goal: find out whether LK holds a feature on the same physical point, separately from whether the initial ID was right.
- Priority: second, after Step 9 is logged. Offline only.
- Tasks:
  - Using the association dump from the lk_periodic withhold-tag run (`runs/20261019T133214-ecda4960`) and the tag-reference poses, select tracks whose map ID was **correct at assignment** (within 3 px of the reference projection on the frame the ID was first set). These are the only tracks where drift can be measured without confounding by a wrong seed. State how many tracks qualify. If very few qualify, say that the test has low power.
  - For each such track, plot or tabulate the pixel error against the reference projection versus track age (frames and seconds since assignment). Report median and p90 by age bucket and the fraction still within 3 px.
  - Repeat with the LK forward/backward gate set to 2 px and 1 px (config copy, one value per run, same clip, same map) and report survival count, drift versus age, and the number of correct tracks retained. Do not change the default.
  - Also report the origin split: how many wrong inherited IDs trace back to a wrong fresh `orb_map` match (wrong at assignment) versus a correct start that drifted. This directly answers whether LK or ORB is the primary source of wrong associations.
- Files: `scripts/lk_drift_diagnostic.py`; config copies `config-exp-fb2.json`, `config-exp-fb1.json`; tests with a synthetic drifting track.
- Done when: section 7 has the origin split and the drift-versus-age table.
- Decision: if drift is small, LK is fine and Step 11 (guided matching) is the priority. If drift is large, tighten the forward/backward gate, or add an appearance check on the tracked patch, before Step 11.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: _(fill in)_

### Step 11: pose-prior guided matching (default off)
- [x] Goal: when a recent valid pose exists, associate map points by projecting them and searching a small window, instead of global descriptor matching. This is the standard approach and removes most ambiguity.
- Priority: third. Start only after Steps 9 and 10 are logged. Use their results to choose the search radius and acceptance rule.
- Design sketch (adjust to the Step 9 and 10 findings):
  - New function (suggested `guided_associate()` in `ar_projection/landmarks.py`), called from `pipeline.py` next to `associate()` when `features.guided_matching.enabled` is true and a prior pose is available (previous valid pose, within a configurable maximum age; consider a short motion extrapolation, but record it).
  - For each map landmark, project with the prior pose; skip landmarks outside the image or in the tag mask. Take detected keypoints within a radius `r` (start at 8-15 px, scale with pose age) of the projection. Accept the one with the smallest Hamming distance if it is under a limit and clearly better than the second candidate in the window. One-to-one.
  - Give guided matches their own `association_source` value (suggest `guided`) so the scorer separates them.
  - Allow guided candidates to join the joint fit when the tag is visible (new option, off by default), so landmarks can support the tag. Keep the standalone 12-inlier gate for the landmark-only path.
  - Keep the existing global matching as the fallback when there is no prior.
  - No stale-prior trap: if the guided result fails the pose gates, drop the prior as the existing code does. Do not extend the prior past `max_age`.
- Tests: guided match picks the correct keypoint in a synthetic window; ignores a closer-in-pixels wrong keypoint with a worse descriptor; respects one-to-one; default off leaves existing outputs identical (the existing 53 tests, plus a byte-identical `frames.jsonl` check).
- Evaluate with Step 12 (hand-off protocol). The old cold-start withhold-tag run will not show the benefit, because it never has a prior.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-23, no hardware, chosen by Sahaj ahead of Step 10 (noted as a deviation from the document's stated order — Step 10 is not yet logged). Implemented `features.guided_matching` (`enabled`/`search_radius_px`/`max_hamming_distance`/`ratio_test`, default off), `guided_matches()` in `ar_projection/landmarks.py`, integrated into `associate()`: it runs first on `landmark_id is None` features when a prior pose is available (reusing the pipeline's existing `self.previous`/`max_prediction_gap_s` prior-pose bookkeeping rather than inventing a new age mechanism — no stale-prior trap needed since that's already how `self.previous` gets cleared), leaving any features it can't match to fall through to the unchanged global matching. New `association_source="guided"`. 4 new tests (synthetic: prefers correct descriptor over a closer-but-wrong pixel; respects the search radius; one-to-one per feature and per landmark; default-off leaves `associate()`'s output byte-identical regardless of whether a prior is passed in). 66/66 tests pass. Deliberately **not** implemented: the "(new option, off by default) allow guided candidates to join the joint fit" nuance from the design sketch — guided associations currently join whichever pose fit is active exactly like `lk`/`orb_redetection`/`orb_map` always have, with no new restriction; flagged as deferred, not silently skipped.
  Ran the existing lk_periodic/withhold-tag comparison (guided off vs on, same baseline 95-landmark map, same 1,131-frame clip) as a sanity check, explicitly caveated per this step's own note that cold-start withhold-tag rarely has a prior. Even so: `off` 17.77% valid (`runs/20261022T183859-e386b622`, matches the known baseline exactly), `on` **26.61% valid** (`runs/20261022T184029-9f0eb386`, +301 vs 201 valid frames). Reference error: median 1.98px→1.91px (unchanged/slightly better), p95 6.39px→8.71px (worse but still under the 10px target), sample count 712→1064. Guided matching fired an average of 1.6 associations/frame across the clip (1,825 total), confirming it's genuinely active, not a no-op. This is a real, measured improvement despite the unfavorable test setup — Step 12's hand-off protocol (tag visible then hidden, so a real prior exists going into each hidden window) should show a larger effect.

### Step 12: hand-off test protocol (tag visible, then hidden in windows)
- [x] Goal: measure the intended behavior. In real use the tag is visible first, then occluded. The existing `--withhold-tag` hides the tag from frame 0, so the tracker never gets a pose to start from and must relocalize cold.
- **CAUTION (added 23 Oct): the Step 12 runs did not test this goal.** They ran with `pose.mode = "landmark_only"`, so the tag never entered the solver outside the windows (`pose.py`: the tag is used only when mode is not `landmark_only`). See Step 13 for the repaired protocol. The window tooling (`withhold_tag_windows`, `window_metrics`, `hand_off`) is fine and is reused.
- Priority: fourth. Can be built in parallel with Step 11 since it is needed to evaluate it.
- Tasks:
  - Add replay support for withholding the tag only inside given frame or time windows (suggested `--withhold-tag-windows start:end,...` or a config list). Outside the windows the tag is available to the solver as normal and seeds the pose and prior.
  - Define windows on the existing clip using frames where the tag reference is valid (so error can be measured inside the windows). Suggested: several 5 s, 10 s and 20 s windows spread through the clip. Record the exact windows in the report.
  - Metrics, inside windows only: valid fraction, time to first lost pose, longest continuous supported run, reference corner median/p95 (only with a reference), and recovery after the window ends. Do not count the withheld tag as evidence (see Step 6). Do not count frozen or predicted poses.
  - Run baseline (no guided matching) and Step 11 guided matching on the same windows.
- Files: `ar_projection/cli.py`, `ar_projection/experiments.py`, `ar_projection/pipeline.py` (small), `tests/test_experiments.py`.
- Done when: a table in section 7 compares baseline versus guided matching per window length.
- Hardware: none. A later physical tag-covering test (Step 8) repeats this on live data.
- Result: Done 2026-10-23, no hardware. Added `withhold_tag_windows` to `pipeline.run()` (`[(start_s,end_s),...]`; withheld inside any window in addition to the existing `withhold_tag` flag — one-line change, default `None` leaves every existing caller byte-identical) and `--withhold-tag-windows start:end,...` to `ar_projection/cli.py`. Added `window_metrics()` (valid fraction, time to first lost pose, longest continuous supported run, reference corner median/p95, recovery after the window closes — all restricted to a window, using `raw_pose.valid` so frozen/predicted poses are never counted since the pipeline never carries those forward) and `hand_off()` (replays a clip twice, guided matching off then on, over the same windows) plus a `handoff` CLI subcommand in `ar_projection/experiments.py`. 4 new tests (a real synthetic-recording replay confirming withholding only happens inside the configured window; `window_metrics` against hand-built rows with known valid-fraction/lost-time/longest-run/recovery). 68/68 tests pass. See section 7 for the full comparison table and interpretation.

### Step 13: repair the hand-off protocol (tag-seeded prior, landmark-only inside windows)
- [x] Goal: measure what the system is meant to do: tag visible (seeds pose and prior), then hidden, with landmarks carrying the pose through the hidden window.
- Priority: **first.** Offline.
- Why: Step 12 ran with `pose.mode = "landmark_only"`. The tag never seeded anything. Its windows measured landmark-only cold tracking only.
- Tasks:
  - Make `hand_off()` (`ar_projection/experiments.py`) run the replays with `pose.mode = "joint"` (config copy, for example `config-exp-handoff.json`), so tag observations are used outside the windows and withheld inside them. Confirm in the log that inside a window, actual pose sources can only be `landmark` or `none`, and that outside they include `tag`/`joint`.

Use 5, 10 and 20 s windows, several of each, each preceded by at least 2 s of valid tag tracking. Record all windows in the report.
  - Run baseline (guided off) and guided (guided on) on the same windows. Also rerun the old `landmark_only` mode on the same windows as a control so the effect of the tag seed is visible.

Use only poses whose source is landmark inside the window. Do not count frozen or predicted poses.

- Done when: section 7 has a table comparing baseline versus guided, tag-seeded versus landmark-only, per window length, with the provenance check quoted. State plainly whether the Step 12 conclusions hold.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-23. `hand_off()` now runs `joint` and `landmark_only` (control), each guided off/on; `pick_windows()` chooses windows by tag-reference validity from a tag_only run (`runs/20261022T194955-42300b59`), 8 windows (20/10/5 s, 80% tag-valid, 2 s valid lead); `window_sources()` confirms sources. Test extended (outside window source is tag/joint, inside never). 68/68 pass. Output `runs/step13-handoff.json`. Provenance: all four runs localize, map `data/logitech-landmark-map.npz`, pose.mode and guided flag as labelled (checked in each `config.json`). Sources inside windows: only `landmark`/`none`; outside (joint): `tag`/`none`.
  Valid fraction inside windows (windows 1-5 start 3886-3918 s, all 0% in all four arms; the table lists the three windows that have signal): 20 s `3925:3945`: joint base 51.8%, joint guided 70.9%, LM-only base 60.8%, LM-only guided 84.1%. 5 s `3932:3937`: 42.9 / 88.3 / 50.6 / 94.8%. 10 s `3937:3946`: 94.6 / 91.9 / 89.3 / 98.0%. Reference median ~1.4-2.0 px everywhere; p95 3.8-10.4 px (worst: joint guided 5 s, 10.38).

(2) The tag seed did NOT help: joint is no better than landmark_only (slightly worse), and the five windows before 3925 s give 0% even with a fresh tag prior, so the dead region is real and not a prior issue -> Step 14. (3) Joint-mode arms are worse than landmark-only, unexplained; do not assume the tag prior is harmless. Step 12 conclusions (guided helps) hold; its "dead region" reading is confirmed, not an artifact.
  Next: Step 14.

### Step 14: dead-region diagnosis by time bin
- [x] Goal: find out why large parts of the clip give no landmark poses at all (v1 windows 1-4 gave 0% for both variants; v2 found only about 22 s of 72 s productive).
- Priority: second. Offline. Start after Step 13 is logged, or in parallel if the work is separate.

  - whether the tag is visible and the reference valid;
  - the camera position and viewing direction relative to the mapped keyframes (for a coverage comparison);
  - image brightness and a sharpness measure (variance of Laplacian) per bin, as an exposure and motion blur check.
- Decision table (record the row that applies):
  - Few visible landmarks in failing bins, many in productive bins: **coverage hole**. A capture or re-map of that region is justified. Ask Sahaj.
  - Similar visible counts but low hit rate or high distance only in failing bins: view or image-quality problem (blur, exposure). Investigate frames, ask about camera settings.
  - No difference in any of these: the failure is in association or solver behavior. Look at Step 15.
- Done when: a per-bin table is in section 7 and a decision row is recorded.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-23, offline. New `scripts/dead_region_diagnosis.py` (reuses `oracle_diagnostic.collect_rows` and `score_associations.load_run`; no tracker change). Output `runs/step14-dead-region.json`. Bins are absolute `t//10`. No new test added for this script (reporting only).

  | 389 | 96% | 94 | 35% | 105 | 0% | 161/2496 | 17 | 0.027 | 113 | 193 |
  | 390 | 93% | 77 | 32% | 113 | 0% | 0/1899 | 16 | 0.034 | 116 | 233 |
  | 391 | 64% | 95 | 33% | 101 | 0% | 0/1391 | 18 | 0.059 | 115 | 155 |

Detector hit rate is lower in bins 388-391 (32-45%) than in 392-394 (60-71%), and sharpness is lowest in 391 only. True-pair distance is about random everywhere (96-113). The clearest separator is association correctness: LK-carried IDs are 0% correct in 390-391 and 7-21% in 387-389, versus 62-67% in 393-394, and productive bins have many more fresh `orb_map` seeds (306-370 vs 11-28). Dead bins therefore hold wrong inherited IDs that never get replaced.

Mixed between row 2 (lower hit rate in failing bins, cause not pinned down; blur explains at most bin 391) and row 3 (association behaviour: wrong IDs lock in, few fresh seeds). ### Step 15: prior grace period (default off)
- [x] Goal: stop one failed frame from destroying the prior. Today `pipeline.py` sets `self.previous = None` on any invalid pose, so guided matching runs only while every frame succeeds.
- Priority: third.
- Design: add `pose.prior_grace_s` (default 0 = current behavior). When a frame fails and a valid pose exists within the grace time, keep that pose **only as the seed for guided matching and the solver's initial guess**. It must never be rendered, counted as a valid pose, or used as a pose estimate. After the grace time, drop it exactly as now. Log `prior_age_s` and `prior_used` per frame.
- Tests: default 0 leaves `frames.jsonl` unchanged; with grace > 0 a short gap keeps the seed, a long gap drops it; the seed never appears as a valid pose; speed gates still apply.
- Evaluate with the Step 13 protocol: grace 0, 0.25 s, 0.5 s, 1 s. One change per run. Report valid fraction, reference error and false-pose rate.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-23. Added `pose.prior_grace_s` (default 0; `config.py`, allowed zero), `Pipeline.prior_time`, and per-frame diagnostics `prior_age_s`/`prior_used` (`pipeline.py`); `pose.py` seed and speed-gate windows use `max(max_prediction_gap_s, prior_grace_s)`. On failure the pose is kept only in `self.previous` (seed for guided matching and the solver), never rendered or counted; the solver gets the prior's age as dt. The existing 0.2 s frame-to-frame gap reset still applies. New test `test_prior_grace_keeps_seed_through_short_gaps_only_and_never_renders_it` (grace 0, 0.05, 0.1); 69/69 pass. Sweep script `scripts/prior_grace_sweep.py`, output `runs/step15-grace.json`.
  Protocol: Step 13 windows, `pose.mode=joint`, guided on, map/calibration/clip as in Step 13 (checked in each run `config.json`: joint, localize, guided true, prior_grace_s as labelled). Grace 0 is `runs/step13-handoff.json` joint/guided. Cells are valid fraction / ref median px / ref p95 px; the five windows before 3925 s stay 0% in every setting.
  | window | grace 0 | 0.25 s | 0.5 s | 1.0 s |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 20 s 3925-3945 | 0.709/1.43/4.69 | 0.757/2.01/9.35 | same | same |
  | 5 s 3932-3937 | 0.883/1.49/10.38 | 0.974/2.85/15.5 | same | same |
  | 10 s 3937-3946 | 0.919/1.69/6.93 | 0.987/2.2/6.18 | same | same |
  Findings: grace raises availability by 4.8, 9.1 and 6.8 points, but the median error rises in two windows (1.43 to 2.01, 1.49 to 2.85) and p95 roughly doubles in two (4.69 to 9.35, 10.38 to 15.5). So the extra poses are lower quality (possibly a seed that drifts rather than a correct re-acquisition). 0.25, 0.5 and 1.0 s gave identical results to the last digit; I did not investigate why, so a grace over 0.25 s has no measured effect here (a seed older than that evidently never produces a pose, or the speed gate rejects it). The five dead windows are unchanged, so a prior is not what is missing there. False-pose rate was not measured (no independent truth beyond the tag reference).
  Decision: keep default 0. Do not enable on this evidence; the accuracy cost is unexplained. Re-check in Step 16 with paired accuracy on common frames and error by association source.

### Step 16: paired accuracy and trimmed LK drift
- [x] Goal: settle the p95 accuracy question and the LK question without the full Step 10.
- Priority: fourth. Replaces Step 10 as written (Step 10 stays as the full-design reference).
- Tasks:
  - **Paired p95:** compare reference error only on frames valid in both baseline and guided runs of the same windows. Report median and p95 on that common set, and separately on frames valid in only one run. This removes the survivor-bias question from the Step 12 log.
  - **By source:** split reference error by association source (`guided`, `lk`, `orb_redetection`, `orb_map`) to see which source drives the p95 tail.
  - **LK drift on guided-assigned tracks:** take tracks whose ID was assigned by guided matching and was within 3 px of the reference at assignment. Report error versus age and the fraction still within 3 px. Repeat with the LK forward/backward gate at 5, 2 and 1 px (config copies, one value per run). Do not change the default.
- Decision: if the tail comes from long-lived LK-carried IDs, add a periodic geometric re-check against the current pose (not only when the pose is valid) or tighten the gate. If it comes from guided matches themselves, tighten the guided acceptance rule (Step 17).
- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-23, offline, no tracker code change. New `scripts/step16_paired.py` (output `runs/step16-paired.json`; no unit test, reporting only). Four replays of the existing clip, Step 13 windows, `pose.mode=joint`, `logging.association_dump=true`, prior_grace 0, localize on `data/logitech-landmark-map.npz` (checked in each `config.json`): `baseline_fb5` (guided off), `guided_fb5`, `guided_fb2`, `guided_fb1` (LK forward/backward gate 5/2/1 px; default 5 unchanged). Error here is the per-frame MEAN of the four panel-corner errors against the tag reference, over supported frames inside the windows (frames counted once even if windows overlap), so it is not the per-corner metric used in Steps 12-15 and p95 values are not directly comparable to those.
  Supported frames in windows: baseline 156, guided_fb5 207, guided_fb2 214, guided_fb1 200.
  Paired p95 (baseline vs guided_fb5): on the 143 common frames median 1.74 vs 1.48 px, p95 6.28 vs 6.48 px (flat). Frames valid only in baseline (13): median 1.87, p95 2.38. Frames valid only in guided (64): median 1.29, p95 2.92.
  By source, observations vs reference (all / RANSAC inliers). guided_fb5: guided 1006 obs, median 6.4 px, 15% within 3 px, inliers (n=146) median 3.07, p95 7.46; LK 9133 obs, 28% within 3 px, inliers (n=3065) median 2.12, p95 8.78; orb_redetection inliers p95 8.53; orb_map 1290 obs, 1% within 3 px, inliers (n=20) p95 8.1. Baseline: LK inliers median 1.95, p95 9.19.
  LK drift on guided-assigned tracks (assigned within 3 px of the reference), fraction still within 3 px by frames since assignment (1-5 / 6-20 / 21-50 / 51+): gate 5 px 96/85/84/68% (27 tracks), 2 px 96/93/78/81% (30 tracks), 1 px 90/78/67/83% (26 tracks). Error p95 at 51+ frames: 8.0 / 13.8 / 13.9 px.
  Findings: (1) The p95 worsening seen in Steps 12-15 does not come from the guided poses: on the common frames p95 is unchanged (6.28 vs 6.48), and the extra frames guided adds are not harder (median 1.29, p95 2.92). My Step 12 log's survivor-bias wording is only half right: guided adds frames, but they are good ones; the earlier p95 rise is more likely the per-corner metric and which frames were pooled. (2) Individual guided matches are mediocre: only 15% within 3 px and inlier median 3.1-3.8 px against about 2 px for LK, so guided is the weakest inlier source, and with gate 2 its inlier p95 is 11.5. That is the candidate for tightening in Step 17. (3) LK drift: about 96% correct at assignment, 68-83% after 51+ frames. The gate gives no clear signal (tracks surviving longer under tighter gates show larger tails). Only 26-30 qualifying tracks per setting, so low power; I do not claim a gate effect. (4) Gate 2 gave the most supported frames (214) but the differences between gates (200-214) are small and unrepeated.
  Decision: no change to the LK default; the answer to the Step 16 question is tighten the guided acceptance rule (Step 17), not the LK gate or a periodic re-check.

The default is a reasonable middle: a smaller radius (6) gets the most frames but the worst tail (12 frames over 10 px); looser ratio (0.9) or tighter Hamming limits cost frames without a clear accuracy gain; ratio 0.6 is the best on accuracy (p95 3.91, no frame over 10 px) for 22% fewer supported frames. This matches Step 16, where the weak guided matches were the issue: the ratio test is the lever that removes them. Caveat: single clip, single run per setting, overlapping windows, 150-220 frames each, so differences of a few frames or tenths of a pixel are not distinguishable from noise.
  Deferred option (guided joins the joint fit when the tag is visible): not added. In joint mode `PoseSolver` already feeds every landmark association, guided included, into the same PnP RANSAC and joint refinement as the tag, so the behavior the sketch asks for is the existing one; there is nothing to switch. Not done: the optional NCC patch score (descriptor distance was not shown to be the limit once the ratio test is tight) and sweeps of combined settings.
  Recommendation: leave `features.guided_matching.enabled` false by default. If it is turned on for the physical Step 8 acceptance test, use the shipped 12 px / 60 / 0.75, or `ratio_test` 0.6 if p95 matters more than availability. No configuration reaches the section 4 criteria: in-window supported tracking is about 70-99% in the three live windows and 0% in the five dead ones, so the 95% target is not met on this clip, and the dead region (Step 14: wrong inherited IDs, low detector hit rate) is the remaining gap. A second independent clip with locked exposure, focus and white balance is needed before relying on any of these numbers; ask Sahaj when ready.

Also try radius 6 and 8 px together with the loose limit, because fewer candidates compensate for weak descriptors.
