- Result: Done 2026-10-23, offline. Added `features.guided_matching.geometry_first` (default false; `config.py`, with a `load_config` default) and a branch in `guided_matches()` (`landmarks.py`): candidates in the window are ranked by pixel distance; candidates within 1 px of the nearest are tied and the lowest Hamming wins; a pick is dropped if its distance exceeds `max_hamming_distance` or, with 3+ candidates, is 32 bits worse than the window median (the 1 px and 32 bit values are constants with a `ponytail:` comment, not config). The ratio test is not applied in this mode. One-to-one per feature and landmark is unchanged. New test `test_guided_matches_loose_limit_and_geometry_first` (tight limit rejects, loose accepts, descriptor ranking by default, nearest pixel wins in geometry-first, descriptor breaks a pixel tie). 72/72 pass; default off, so existing outputs are unchanged. Not done: the optional NCC patch score (the instruction says to do it only if the descriptor-free options fail, and one of them did not). Sweep `scripts/step19_sweep.py`, output `runs/step19-sweep.json`.

One change per run against the Step 17 default row (12 px / 60 / 0.75: 207 supported frames of 804, 25.7%, median 1.45, p95 6.39, 3 frames over 10 px). (3) Geometry-first with radius 12 is the strongest lever so far: 66.9% pooled, windows 1-3 at 90-99% (they were 0% in every earlier variant), at a cost of median 2.75, p95 11.16 and 37 frames over 10 px. (5) Windows 4-5 (3908-3918 s, the dead window with the detector gap from Step 18) stay at 0% in every setting, so the loose gate does not fix that stretch; Step 18 showed few keypoints there. ### Step 20: re-check existing IDs against the prior projection on every feature, every frame (default off)
- [x] Goal: stop wrong inherited IDs from blocking guided matching.
- Priority: third. Offline. Start after Step 18 is logged.
- Background: guided matching runs only on features with `landmark_id is None`. Wrong IDs carried by LK or ORB redetection stay until a valid pose removes them (cleanup runs only when the pose is valid). Step 3 `id_revalidation` made things worse, but it ran before guided matching existed and had no pose prior. Do not reuse its code path blindly.
- Design: new option `features.guided_matching.revalidate_existing` (default off). When a prior pose (seed) exists, for each feature with an inherited ID, project the landmark with the prior and compare with the feature pixel. If the disagreement exceeds a radius (start at 12 px, same as the search radius), clear the ID and let guided matching try that feature (and others) in the same frame. Keep the one-to-one rule. Log `ids_cleared_by_guided_revalidation` per frame. Never use the tag reference pose for this; only the seed pose the tracker itself holds.
- Tests: a wrong inherited ID is cleared and replaced by a guided match in a synthetic frame; a correct inherited ID stays; default off leaves output unchanged; one-to-one holds.
- Evaluate with the Step 13 protocol, one change per run, with and without the Step 19 loose gate if Step 19 helped.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-23, offline. **Negative result: revalidation lowers availability in every combination tested.** Added `features.guided_matching.revalidate_existing` (default false; `config.py`) in `associate()` (`landmarks.py`): when guided matching is enabled, a prior pose is held and the option is on, every feature whose ID was inherited (source `lk` or `orb_redetection`, with a descriptor) is cleared if its landmark projects more than `search_radius_px` from the feature under the tracker's own prior pose (never the tag reference); cleared features are then free for the guided pass in the same frame, one-to-one preserved. Per-frame count in `map_associations.ids_cleared_by_guided_revalidation`. New test `test_guided_revalidation_clears_wrong_inherited_id_and_rematches_it` (wrong ID cleared and rematched, correct ID kept, one-to-one, default off leaves it). 73/73 pass. Sweep `scripts/step20_sweep.py`, output `runs/step20-sweep.json`; Step 13 protocol, joint, guided on, prior_grace 0, same map/clip/windows, each run `config.json` checked.
  | setting | supported | fraction | median px | p95 px | over 10 px | per-window valid (8 windows) | IDs cleared (whole run) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Step 17 default (no revalidation) | 207 | 25.7% | 1.45 | 6.39 | 3 | see Step 17 | 0 |
  | revalidate, 12 px / 60 / 0.75 | 183 | 22.8% | 1.74 | 5.10 | 0 | 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, .62, .56, .93 | 139 |
  | Step 19 Hamming 128 (no revalidation) | 256 | 31.8% | 1.77 | 5.38 | 2 | 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, .89, .96, .99 | 0 |
  | revalidate, Hamming 128 | 62 | 7.7% | 2.99 | 6.10 | 0 | 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, .20, .29, 0 | 144 |
  | Step 19 geometry-first (no revalidation) | 538 | 66.9% | 2.75 | 11.16 | 37 | .90, .98, .99, 0, 0, .89, .90, .88 | 0 |
  | revalidate, geometry-first | 129 | 16.0% | 2.57 | 10.69 | 12 | .06, 0, 0, 0, 0, .36, .21, .81 | 343 |
  Findings: only 139-343 IDs were cleared over about 1,100 frames, yet availability fell by 3 points (default gate), 24 points (Hamming 128) and 51 points (geometry-first) against the matching runs without revalidation. With the default gate the tail improves (p95 5.10, no frame over 10 px) because the poses that survive are the safer ones. None of the five dead windows moves off 0% (geometry-first window 1 gets 6%, a loss from 90%). This matches Step 3 (clearing inherited IDs is worse), now with a pose prior in place. I did not establish the mechanism. Plausible, untested reasons: the 12 px radius is tight against a seed that is already a frame stale, so correct IDs are cleared too; and cleared features are re-assigned by guided matching using the same prior, which can reassign them wrongly. Do not turn this on.
  Decision: keep `revalidate_existing` off. Stale-ID lock-in as the cause of the dead region is not supported by this fix; Step 18's wrong-ID counts may reflect a poor prior or LK start rather than something that clearing repairs. The best lever found remains Step 19 geometry-first (windows 1-3), with windows 4-5 still at 0% from a detector gap.

### Step 21: prior grace cap check
- [x] Goal: explain why prior grace 0.25, 0.5 and 1.0 s gave identical results in Step 15.
- Priority: fourth (small).
- Tasks: read `pipeline.py` and `pose.py` to see how the 0.2 s frame-gap reset (`max_prediction_gap_s`) and the speed gates interact with `prior_grace_s`. Count, per window, the frames where the seed was kept and used (`prior_used`, `prior_age_s`). If the 0.2 s rule makes values above 0.2 s identical, either say so in the log or make the grace time actually effective (a separate seed-expiry field), default off. Add a test where grace 0.1 and 0.5 give different seed use over a 0.3 s failure.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-23, offline, no behavior change. **The grace time is not capped; the three values are identical in outcome because seeds older than 0.25 s never produce a pose.** Evidence from the three Step 15 runs (`runs/step15-grace.json`, 804 in-window frames each): frames where a seed was held and used: grace 0.25 s 273 (max age 0.343 s, 6 frames older than 0.2 s), 0.5 s 284 (max 0.563 s, 17 older than 0.2 s), 1.0 s 308 (max 1.047 s, 41 older than 0.2 s); valid frames in the windows: 255 in all three. So the longer values do keep and use the seed longer (it is not an unused code path), but the extra frames do not turn into poses. Why: the 0.2 s frame-to-frame reset (`max_prediction_gap_s`) fires on the gap between consecutive frames, not on seed age, and fired once in the whole run, so it is not the cap. In `pose.py` the stored pose is appended as an extra start only `if seeds` is already non-empty, i.e. only when landmark RANSAC has already produced a candidate; the seed alone never creates a pose. Frames where the seed was older than 0.25 s fail landmark RANSAC anyway (in-window gate reasons for grace 1.0: `PnP RANSAC failed` 309, `insufficient RANSAC inliers` 172, no gate failure 255). The seed also feeds guided matching, but that did not change the outcome either. So no separate seed-expiry field is needed and none was added.
  One imprecision found: the seed's age is read before the keep/drop decision, so a seed is used for one frame beyond the grace time (grace 0.1 s with frames 0.05 s apart is used on 3 failed frames, ages 0.05, 0.10, 0.15). Left as is and covered by the new test `test_prior_grace_values_differ_over_a_long_failure` (grace 0.1 gives 3 uses, 0.5 gives 6 uses over 0.3 s of failures, no valid pose in either). 74/74 pass.
  Consequence for Step 15: the gain it measured (valid fraction +5 to +9 points) comes from grace up to about 0.25 s; longer values add nothing on this clip. Grace stays default off (accuracy cost in Step 15).

### Step 24: tail control for geometry-first guided matching (default off)
- [x] Goal: keep the availability gain of geometry-first (66.9% pooled) while cutting the false-pose tail (p95 11.16 px, 37 frames over 10 px).
- Priority: third. Offline. Start after Step 23 is logged.
- Candidate changes (one per run, Step 23 windows and metrics, geometry-first radius 12, Hamming 128 as the base):
  - a second-best margin: accept the nearest keypoint only if the second nearest in the window is at least a set ratio farther (or has a clearly worse descriptor distance);
  - a mutual check: each keypoint may be claimed by only one landmark, resolved by smallest pixel offset (one-to-one already exists; test a stricter tie rule);
  - a consistency pass after the landmark pose is estimated: drop guided matches whose reprojection error under that pose exceeds a limit and refit once (this uses the tracker's own pose, never the tag reference);
  - a pose acceptance gate on the guided-only path: minimum inliers or inlier fraction above the 12 and 4-cell rules, applied only when most inliers are guided matches;
  - an upper bound on frame-to-frame pose change for landmark-only poses (the 3 m/s and 300 deg/s gates are generous for a handheld camera at this range).
- Report for each: pooled and per-stretch valid fraction, per-corner median and p95, share of supported frames over 10 px, and effect on the 3 stretches that work. Pick the setting that meets p95 at most 10 px and at most about 2% of supported frames over 10 px with the smallest loss of availability, or say that none does.
- Tests: each new rule has a synthetic test; defaults leave `frames.jsonl` byte-identical.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-23, offline. **A pixel-margin rule meets the target: `geometry_margin` 1.5 gives 75.5% supported with p95 7.80 px and 1.9% of supported frames over 10 px.** Implemented one new rule: `features.guided_matching.geometry_margin` (default 1.0 = off; `config.py`, `landmarks.py`): in geometry-first mode a pick is dropped if another candidate in the window is closer than `geometry_margin` times its pixel distance (pixel margin only; a descriptor tie-break is not implemented, marked with a `ponytail:` comment). New test `test_geometry_margin_drops_ambiguous_picks_only`; 75/75 pass; default off leaves outputs unchanged. The other candidates were run with existing config knobs instead of new code (`min_landmark_inliers`, `max_landmark_rms_px`, `ransac_reprojection_threshold_px`, the two speed gates). Not implemented: the stricter keypoint tie rule (one-to-one already holds) and a post-pose consistency refit (the 2 px RANSAC threshold is its closest existing form). Scripts `scripts/step24_tail.py` (runs `runs/step24-tail.json` and, with `--combined`, `runs/step24-combined.json`).
  Provenance: Step 23 windows (`runs/step23-windows.json`: 5 non-overlapping windows, 629 frames), `pose.mode=joint`, guided on, geometry-first, radius 12, Hamming 128, prior_grace 0, revalidate_existing false, localize on `data/logitech-landmark-map.npz`, clip `20261019T091439-6ea9d598`; each run `config.json` checked. Base row = Step 23 (c): 68% supported, median 3.42, p95 13.75, 9% over 10 px. One change per run (the last two rows combine two and are a follow-up check):
  | change | supported | median px | p95 px | over 10 px | stretch 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 supported |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | base (c) | 68% | 3.42 | 13.75 | 9% | 75 / 0 / 80 / 91% |
  | margin 1.5 | **75.5%** | 3.53 | **7.80** | **1.9%** | 85 / 0 / 84 / 97% |
  | margin 2.0 | 70.6% | 3.68 | 10.06 | 5.2% | 78 / 0 / 81 / 99% |
  | margin 3.0 | 63.4% | 4.40 | 11.23 | 8.3% | 75 / 0 / 35 / 100% |
  | min_landmark_inliers 20 | 61.5% | 2.48 | 4.66 | 0.8% | 63 / 0 / 83 / 97% |
  | max_landmark_rms 1.5 px | 8.9% | 6.63 | 40.0 | 44.6% | 7 / 16 / 11 / 7% |
  | RANSAC threshold 2 px | 34.2% | 3.89 | 10.22 | 6.0% | 35 / 0 / 7 / 95% |
  | speed gates 1 m/s, 90 deg/s | 83.3% | 2.67 | 10.36 | 5.5% | 84 / **79** / 80 / 90% |
  | margin 1.5 + speed gates | 78.9% | 3.25 | 12.18 | 8.1% | 92 / 0 / 78 / 97% |
  | margin 1.5 + min inliers 20 | 50.9% | 2.72 | 5.32 | 0% | 63 / 0 / 0 / 99% |
  Findings: (1) Margin 1.5 is the setting that meets p95 at most 10 px and at most about 2% over 10 px with no loss of availability (it is higher than the base, 75.5% against 68%; I did not establish why, a plausible but untested reason is that ambiguous picks used to poison the prior that later frames search from). Margin 2.0 sits on the limit (p95 10.06, 5.2%), margin 3.0 is worse, so the effect is not monotonic and rests on single runs. (2) `min_landmark_inliers` 20 gives the cleanest tail (p95 4.66, 0.8%) at 61.5%, a tail-first alternative. (3) The tighter speed gates give the highest availability (83.3%) and are the only setting that moves the dead stretch 1 (3908-3918 s) off 0% (79%), but the tail is marginal (p95 10.36, 5.5%); so the dead stretch can be reached by rejecting jumps, a lead worth following (not yet explained). Combining margin 1.5 with the speed gates loses the tail benefit (p95 12.18, 8.1%) and stretch 1 again returns to 0%; combining it with 20 inliers loses stretch 2. A 1.5 px landmark RMS limit or a 2 px RANSAC threshold removes most poses. (4) Caveat: 5 windows, 45 s, one clip, single runs, no repeat to measure run-to-run spread; the per-stretch tails use 76-80 frames.
  Recommended setting for further work and any physical test (still default off): geometry-first, radius 12, Hamming 128, `geometry_margin` 1.5. The 95% target is still far away (stretch 1 is 0% and the whole clip is 75%).

### Step 25: hand-off continuity
- [x] Goal: stop the panel from disappearing at the moment the tag is hidden. Step 13 and Step 18 show the landmark pose is invalid on the first frame of every window, even with a tag-seeded prior.
- Priority: fourth. Offline. Start after Step 24 has a candidate.
- Tasks: (1) measure how long the landmark path needs after the tag is hidden before it gives its first valid pose (time to first valid landmark pose, from Step 23 data). (2) If it is short (under about 0.3 s), consider a labelled fallback: keep rendering the last tag-based pose for that short time as an explicit "bridge" state, shown and logged as such, never counted as valid tracking and never longer than a configured limit. Default off. (3) While the tag is still valid, add landmark associations in the background (guided matching with the tag pose as the prior) so the landmark path already holds 12 or more mapped inputs when the tag disappears; test whether that removes the first-frame failure. One change per run.
- Do not count the bridge frames as evidence-supported poses in any availability number. Report them separately.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: Done 2026-10-23, offline, **no code change**. The premise (the landmark pose is invalid on the first frame of every window) held only for the default matching of Steps 13 and 18; with geometry-first matching it does not. Data: `runs/step23-clipwide.json` and `runs/step24-tail.json` (per-window `time_to_first_valid_s`) plus the `frames.jsonl` of the Step 24 margin 1.5 run (geometry-first, radius 12, Hamming 128, `geometry_margin` 1.5, joint, prior_grace 0, Step 23 windows, `config.json` checked).
  (1) Time to first valid landmark pose after the tag is hidden, margin 1.5: 0.03 s (3877.7 s, 20 s window), 0.01 s (3899.7 s), never (3908.6 s, the dead stretch), 0.01 s (3925.4 s), 0.03 s (3941.3 s). The first frame inside the window is valid with source `landmark` in four of five windows. For comparison, the default matching (Step 23 a) gives a valid pose in only one of five windows (0.03 s) and geometry-first without the margin in four of five (0.01-0.03 s).
  (3) Background association while the tag is valid already happens: `associate()` runs every frame in joint mode and uses the held pose as the guided prior, whatever the pose source. Mapped inputs in the second before each window (median): 42.5, 20.5, 17, 26, 51; guided associations 3-8 per frame; in the first 0.5 s inside the window the median is 48, 23, 18, 23, 56.5. So the 12-input requirement is met before the tag is hidden, including in the dead window (17); there the pose fails on correctness (RANSAC finds too few consistent inliers), not on the count, and in the first 0.5 s 0 of 8 frames are valid. The dead stretch is the cause, not hand-off lag.
  (2) Bridge state: not implemented. Where the landmark path works it recovers within one to two frames (0.01-0.03 s), so a bridge would hide nothing the user can see; where it fails (3908-3918 s) it fails for the whole window (5 s), far past the 0.3 s limit, and a bridge would only show a stale panel for a fraction of that. By the step's own rule (consider a bridge only if the delay is under about 0.3 s) the case is not made, and bridge frames could not be counted as tracking anyway. Add it only if a live test shows a visible flicker at the moment of occlusion.
  Decision: Step 25 closes with no change. The remaining hand-off failure is the dead stretch at 3908-3918 s, which only the tighter speed gates (Step 24: 79% there, tail marginal) have moved. Caveat: five windows on one clip.

  - Build a new map from M1 into a new file (for example `data/logitech-landmark-map-occ.npz`); never overwrite `data/logitech-landmark-map.npz`. Record landmark count, support, RMS, extents and the SHA-256, and compare coverage (image cells, distance range) against the old map.
  - Make a config copy that points to the new map. Re-run the `compare` tool on V1 and V2 with the tag withheld, the recommended Step 24 setting (geometry-first, radius 12, Hamming 128, `geometry_margin` 1.5), and the old defaults for contrast.
  - Report, per clip: supported fraction, reference error against the tag where the tag is visible (V1), and the list of intervals where the tag is hidden (V2) with the landmark pose supported or not.
- Decision: if landmark pose is unsupported for most of V2, do not judge occlusion quality there; report that depth evaluation for V2 is limited by tracking. Use V1 (tag pose valid) as the main depth-evaluation clip and V2 as the realistic case.
- Provenance: record the map SHA-256, calibration SHA-256, the code hash, `pose.mode`, guided flags and withhold windows for every run.
- Hardware: none (uses the recorded clips).
- Result: tasks done, one open item. Map built (502 landmarks, SHA-256 `86ff9dab87d9e995...`, see section 7). V0/V1/V2/V4 landmark-only pose is usable (77-90% valid, 2-3.5px median reference error). V3 was re-recorded by Sahaj; the re-record improved landmark-only validity from 0% to 16.2% (still far below the other clips, driven by the same PnP-RANSAC-fails pattern, now partial instead of total) — **V3 remains not usable for judging the mask**, carried forward as an open item rather than solved. Guided-on vs guided-off comparison done on V1/V2: guided-on (current default) matches or beats guided-off on both (V1: 84.6% valid/2.20px vs 77.2%/2.47px; V2: 76.8%/2.43px vs 78.2%/2.67px) — no evidence to change the default. V1 confirmed as the clean main depth-evaluation clip, V2 as the realistic case, per this step's decision rule.

### Step 30: depth scale calibration and scale hold (the tag is calibration only)
- [ ] Goal: get metric scale from the tag at the start, then keep it correct when the tag is hidden by a hand.
- Priority: fourth. Offline, GPU.
- Method (proposal; change only with evidence):
  - **Calibration phase:** while the tag is visible and unoccluded, compute `s = median(Z_true / Z_pred)` over the tag-plane pixels (polygon from the tag corners, eroded by a few pixels to avoid blurred edges), using the tag pose and the known 0.17 m size to get `Z_true`. Use the median over the calibration interval (at least 30 frames).
  - **Hold phase:** keep `s` when the tag is hidden or not detected. Update only from anchors that are not occluded: mapped landmarks projected by the tracker's own pose (never the tag reference), keeping only landmarks whose predicted depth agrees with the held scale within a tolerance, using a robust median. Treat anchors on the occluder as outliers.
  - **Scale model:** scale only at first. Test scale plus offset as an ablation, and per-frame scale against a smoothed scale.
  - **Tag visible again:** compare the held scale with the new tag-derived scale and log the difference (drift check).
- Experiments (no mask yet):
  - V0: scale stability over time and across camera distances (relative std).
  - V1: tag-derived scale with and without a hand in the frame (the hand is in the image but not on the tag). This measures how much a nearby hand disturbs the model's scale.
  - V2: held scale against the scale re-measured when the tag returns (drift over each tag-hidden interval, in percent).
  - Compare tag-only, landmark-only and combined anchors. Report anchor counts per frame.
- Report: scale per frame, anchor counts, relative MAD, drift per interval, fraction of frames where the existing gate (at least 12 anchors, relative MAD at most 0.25) would pass, and the effect of a 5% and 10% scale error on the 0.02 m margin at 0.3, 0.5 and 1.0 m panel depth.
- Tests: synthetic depth map with a known scale recovers it; outlier anchors on an occluder are rejected; hold mode keeps the scale when the tag is absent; defaults leave the normal tracking output unchanged.
- Hardware: none (uses the clips).
- Result: first pass only, not complete. Added `tag_plane_scale()` (calibration phase) with 2 tests; measured stability on V0/V1 and zero-order-hold drift on V2 (see section 7) — scale is stable (~6.6-6.9% relative MAD) and even a trivial "do nothing" hold drifts only 0.01-6.2% across V2's real occlusion gaps (up to 11.5 s). Not yet done: the landmark-anchor hold-phase update/outlier-rejection logic (Step 30's second bullet — the existing live `DepthPrototype.render` already has a version of this but it was deliberately left untouched here, offline-only, since the live path is gated to Step 35), the scale+offset ablation, the V1 hand-presence split (no timing labels exist to split V1 by hand-near-tag vs not — flagged, not fabricated), and the anchor-count/gate-pass-fraction reporting. The 5%/10% margin-error arithmetic below is a bigger finding than anything else here.

### Step 31: visibility mask and composite (offline)
- [x] Goal: produce the mask and the composited image for every frame of the occlusion clips.
- Priority: fifth. Offline, GPU.
- Tasks:
  - Per frame: tracker pose, panel camera-Z per pixel (ray-plane), predicted depth times held scale, mask rule `real < panel - margin`. Margin default 0.02 m; sweep 0.01, 0.02, 0.05 m.
  - Keep invalid-depth pixels visible (current policy) and log how many there are; add an option to treat them as occluded and compare.
  - Export per frame: rectified image, visibility PNG, virtual-Z NPY, pose, K, timestamp, scale value and its validity, inference time (the existing export format).
  - Write contact sheets (PNG) for each scenario with the panel, mask, and depth side by side.
  - When the scale is unavailable: show the panel unoccluded and log "visibility unavailable". Never reuse a stale mask.
- Tests: ray-plane depth matches an analytic case; a synthetic nearer plane hides the panel and a farther plane does not; invalid depth follows the stated policy; scale failure disables the mask.
- Output: `runs/step31-masks/` plus a markdown note.
- Hardware: none.
- Result: done, with a corrected finding (see the "Step 31 correction" log entry below — an initial "confirmed correct occlusion" example turned out to be wrong on closer inspection). Mask exported for every frame of V0-V4 plus a margin sweep and invalid-depth-policy comparison on V1. Confirmed, by direct photo overlay: **false positives** with no occluder present at all (V3 frames ~20-30, V4 frames ~1270-1280, up to 73%/55% of the panel falsely hidden) and a **false negative** (V2 frame 441, hand clearly on the panel, 0% occluded). Neither a margin sweep (tested down to 0.0) nor a per-frame scale correction can fix the false negative — the raw model output itself doesn't separate the close hand from normal-distance background in that frame. See section 7 for the full breakdown and the follow-up experiments.

### Step 32: hand labels and scoring
- [ ] Goal: measure mask quality against labels made by Sahaj.
- Priority: sixth.
- Tool: write `scripts/step32_label.py` (OpenCV brush or polygon, saves PNG masks). Per labelled frame, Sahaj paints the **occluder silhouette** over the whole image and sets a flag, front or behind the panel plane, per object. Ground truth for the panel is then: panel polygon, minus the silhouette of every object flagged "front". Frames to label: about 10 per scenario for V1-V4 (about 40-50 in total), chosen by the script across the clip with extra frames at transitions. Sahaj labels; Claude Code does not.
- Metrics: visibility-mask IoU (target at least 0.85), boundary F1 at 2 px, false-hide rate on V0 and V3 (pixels hidden that should be visible), miss rate on V1/V2 (pixels visible that should be hidden), transition timing disagreement and flicker (pixels changing state between consecutive frames with no labelled change), per scenario and pooled.
- Report V1 (tag pose valid) and V2 (landmark pose, tag hidden) **separately**, so depth error is not confused with pose error.
- Output: `runs/step32-scores.json` and a table in section 7.
- Hardware: none (Sahaj's time for labelling).
- Result: _(fill in)_

### Step 33: input size, speed and latency
- [ ] Goal: choose the depth input size that passes quality and speed.
- Tasks: score IoU at 252, 392 and 518 on the same labelled frames; measure pipeline FPS and read-to-display latency with depth on (live clip replay paced at source time, and a live run if Sahaj agrees); check whether depth can run every second frame with the mask warped or held for the in-between frame, as a labelled option (never counted as a fresh measurement).
- Selection rule: highest-quality size that passes IoU, front/behind coverage, at least 20 displayed FPS and at most 100 ms p95, with at least 95% of frames having a valid scale. If none passes, say so and do not choose.
- Hardware: GPU; camera only for the live run (ask Sahaj).
- Result: _(fill in)_

### Step 34: edge and flicker fixes (only if Step 32 fails on boundary or temporal scores)
- [ ] Options, one per run: RGB-guided or bilateral refinement of the mask edge, small morphological clean-up, temporal smoothing of depth or the mask (short EMA with an explicit lag cost), hysteresis on the margin. Report the effect on IoU, boundary F1 and flicker against the Step 32 base. Do not add a segmentation model before simpler fixes are scored.
- Result: _(fill in)_

- [ ] Goal: run the mask in the live loop, with a safe fallback.
- Gate: the existing rule stays. The CLI requires a passed tracking acceptance report (matching calibration, map, tag and image size) before depth is imported. Offline scoring in Steps 30-34 does not need that report and must say so in its output. Do not weaken the live gate without Sahaj's approval.

### Step 36: depth-source comparison on the false-hide and miss frames (diagnosis only, no behavior change)
- [x] Goal: find out where the false occlusion and the hand miss come from by comparing, on the same frames, (A) the model depth, (B) the panel's virtual depth from the landmark pose, (C) the panel's virtual depth from the tag pose, and (D) an oracle scene depth built from known geometry. Three fixes (Huber loss, correct checkpoint, patch-median anchors) left V3 frame 26 (empty scene, about 87% falsely hidden) and V2 frame 441 (hand on the panel, 0% hidden) unchanged, so the cause may not be the depth values at all.
- Priority: **first.** Offline, GPU. No code change to `occlusion.py` defaults, the fit, anchors or margin.
- Sahaj has confirmed that the tag is clearly in view in V3 frame 26.
- The five quantities, per chosen frame:
  - **A. Model depth.** Output of `depth_anything_v2_metric_hypersim_vits.pth` at input size 252, in two forms: raw, and scaled by the current `DepthPrototype` fit (record `a`, `b`, anchor count, relative MAD, and which anchors were used).
  - **B. Virtual panel depth, landmark pose.** The pose the mask export actually used for that frame (`render_pose`), with the panel's ray-plane camera-Z from `panel_layer()`. Record `pose_source` (`tag`, `landmark`, `joint`) from `frames.jsonl`. If the export pose was tag-based or joint, say so; then B and C are close to the same pose and the comparison must also include a landmark-only replay (`--pose-mode landmark_only --withhold-tag`) for those frames.
  - **C. Virtual panel depth, tag pose.** The same panel geometry, projected with a tag-only pose fitted to the tag corners in that frame (the same fit as the tag reference in `withhold_tag` runs: `tag_reference_T_CW`, `tag_reference_rms_px`). Record tag side in pixels, tag RMS, and the ambiguity flag.
  - **D. Oracle scene depth.** Ray-plane camera-Z of the **table plane** (world Z = 0, assuming the tag lies flat on the table; verify first, see task 2) and of the tag plane, using the tag pose from C. This is true depth for every table pixel.
- Tasks:
  1. **Choose frames.** Always include V3 frame 26, plus V3 frames 20-30. Include V4 frame 1276 (panel at the top border) and V4 frames 1270-1280. Include the V0 frames with the highest false-occlusion fraction (V0 should read 0%; take the worst five and five random others). Include V2 frame 441 (hand miss) and V2 frame 72. Add five control frames per clip with 0% false occlusion. For every frame check that the tag was detected, tag RMS is 1 px or less, side is 40 px or more and the pose is not ambiguous. **If the tag registration is poor in a chosen frame, find the nearest frame (within 30 frames) with a good tag fit that shows the same behaviour (false-occluded fraction above 30%, or the same hand miss) and use that instead; write down the substitution and the frame distance.** If no such neighbour exists, say so and continue with the others.
  2. **Verify the table-plane assumption.** On three tag-visible frames, check that the landmark points nearest the table and the base of the tag lie close to world Z = 0, and that the tag plane and the table plane meet at the tag. If the tag is not flat on the table, or the table is not at Z = 0, stop and ask Sahaj. Do not guess the plane.
  3. **Pose agreement (B versus C).** Per frame report: camera position difference (m), rotation difference (degrees), pixel difference of the four panel corners (median and max), panel median camera-Z difference (m) and the maximum Z difference over the panel (m). Report landmark-pose supporting counts (RANSAC inliers, mapped inputs) and reprojection RMS for the landmark pose.
  4. **Depth against oracle (A versus D).** On table pixels that lie outside the panel and outside the tag, compute `model_scaled - oracle` per pixel. Use robust statistics (median, 25th and 75th percentile, 5th and 95th percentile) because objects stand on the table. Report the bias by image row band (top, middle, bottom thirds) and by column band, and fit a plane to the bias map so the top-to-bottom gradient is measured, not assumed. Repeat the same statistics with raw depth and the best single-frame affine fit to the oracle (an upper bound on what a perfect scale and offset could achieve). Report the scale and offset the oracle fit would choose and compare with the anchor fit's `a`, `b`.
  5. **Mask under four depth/pose combinations on the panel.** For each frame compute the occluded fraction of panel pixels for: (i) model depth against B; (ii) model depth against C; (iii) oracle depth against B; (iv) oracle depth against C. Use the production margin (0.02 m) and also 0.05 m. Where the oracle depth is used, only table pixels have an oracle value; for panel pixels that fall on empty space between the camera and the table, the oracle value is the table behind them and must not occlude. This case is defined in the output as the "no occluder" reference, and any combination that hides panel pixels there is a false hide.
  6. **Hand frame (V2 441).** Report median and 5th/95th percentile model depth under the hand mask (a hand mask drawn by Sahaj, or a simple polygon recorded in the log), the panel's virtual Z from C, the table depth from D in the same column, and whether the hand reads nearer than the panel in the raw output. State clearly whether the model separates the hand from the table with the correct checkpoint.
  7. **Figures (one PNG per frame, `runs/step36-depth-sources/`):**
     - Row 1: rectified photo with the panel outline from B and from C in different colours, tag outline, table-plane grid projected from C.
     - Row 2: model depth scaled, oracle depth D (table and tag only, blank elsewhere), and their difference, all on one colour scale in metres with a colour bar.
     - Row 3: virtual panel Z from B, from C, and B minus C, with a colour bar in metres.
     - Row 4: the four masks from task 5 overlaid on the photo, and a scatter plot of model depth against oracle depth on table pixels with the identity line and the fitted line.
     - A profile plot of model, oracle and panel Z along a vertical line through the panel centre.
     - Label every figure with clip, frame index, pose source, tag RMS, anchor count and fit `a`, `b`.
  8. **Summary tables** in `runs/step36-depth-sources.json` and `runs/step36-depth-sources.md`: one row per frame with every number above, then a grouped summary (false-hide frames, controls, hand frames).
- Decision table (record the row that applies, per group of frames):
  - **B and C differ** (panel Z difference above about 2 cm, or corners differ by more than 3 px): the panel's virtual depth is wrong because the landmark pose is wrong. The fix is in pose selection (for example, prefer the tag pose when it is valid and well registered, or reject landmark poses that disagree with a good tag fit), not in the depth model. Report how often this happens in each clip.
  - **B and C agree, model against oracle shows a structured bias above the margin** (plane-like or row-dependent): model bias. Next step is the dense table-plane calibration (fit scale, offset and a low-order plane on table pixels each frame, instead of sparse landmark anchors), then re-score V0.
  - **B and C agree, model against oracle is small, but the model-versus-panel mask still hides pixels:** the problem is local to the panel region (border, halo, or the model reading the panel's surroundings). Report where on the panel the hidden pixels lie, and compare with the depth edges.
  - **Oracle against C gives no false hides, oracle against B does:** pose problem confirmed.
  - **Oracle gives no false hides in either, model does:** model or fit problem confirmed.
  - **Hand frame:** if the hand reads nearer than the panel with the correct checkpoint but the mask shows 0%, the fit or scaling hides it; if the hand reads far, this is a model limit at close range. Record which.
- Tests: add a test only for a reusable helper (for example `plane_depth(T_CW, plane, camera)` returns the analytic Z for a synthetic plane and camera). Existing tests still pass in `.venv` (no torch import).
- Output: the figures, the JSON and markdown summary, the script `scripts/step36_depth_sources.py` (run in `.venv-depth`), and a section 7 log entry: what changed, runs, numbers, interpretation split into observation and hypothesis, and the decision row. Add the script and any helper to section 6.
- Provenance to quote: checkpoint file name and SHA-256, map SHA-256, calibration SHA-256, clip run IDs, input size, margin, pose source per frame, code hash.
- Do not change defaults, fix anything, or tune anything in this step. If the result is mixed, report it as mixed.
- Hardware: GPU only. No camera.
- Result: decisive. On V3/V4's false-hide frames, comparing the model's depth against the geometric oracle (table-plane, true geometry) gives **0.0% false occlusion with either pose**, while the model's own depth gives 40-42%. Same pattern on V0 (the should-always-read-0% clip): oracle 0.0%, model 20-24%. Pose (B vs C) agrees well everywhere it matters (panel Z within ~2cm median). This is row "oracle gives no false hides in either, model does: model or fit problem confirmed" from the decision table, on two independent clips. The bias has clear spatial structure (row-dependent: top-of-frame bias far larger than bottom, -6 to -28cm difference across groups), so it is not a scale/offset a per-frame fit could remove. On the hand frame (V2/441): the model's own depth places the hand at 0.84m, farther than the panel/table at ~0.71m -- "hand reads far, this is a model limit at close range" per the decision table, independently confirming the earlier diagnosis with the current production fit rather than an exploratory one. Full detail in the section 7 log entry below.

### Step 37: dense table-plane correction of the model depth (offline, GPU, existing clips)
- [x] Goal: remove the row-dependent model bias by calibrating the model depth against the known table plane every frame, then re-score false hides without hurting the hand case.
- Why: the panel stands on the table, so at its base "real minus virtual" depth is the model's table bias. Step 36 measured that bias as structured (plane-like, row-dependent) and larger than the 2 cm margin. Sparse landmark anchors cannot remove it. The oracle plane (`occlusion.plane_depth`) gives true depth for every table pixel.
- Method (change only with evidence; one change per run):
  1. Per frame, select calibration pixels: table-plane pixels with oracle depth in about 0.3-1.5 m (a config parameter; far clips may need a larger upper bound, see Step 40), away from grazing rays (reject oracle depth growth beyond a set limit or ray-to-plane angle below about 10 degrees), excluding the tag, the panel (dilated), and pixels the landmark map says are above the table (landmark height above about 3 cm projected with a small radius).
  2. Robust fit (Huber or IRLS) of `Z_true ~ a + b*raw + c*row + d*col` on those pixels. Also test two lighter variants: `a + b*raw` only, and `b*raw` + plane in (row, col). Report which terms matter.
  3. Hold coefficients with a short median over the last N frames (start at 5). Gate the fit: minimum calibration-pixel count and fraction of the image, residual MAD at or below a set limit, plausible `b`. If the gate fails, fall back to the existing landmark-anchor fit; if that fails too, show the panel unoccluded and log "visibility unavailable". Never reuse a stale mask.
  4. Apply the correction to the whole image (including object pixels), then use the existing mask rule `real < panel - margin`.
  5. Set the margin from the post-correction residual spread on table pixels (for example 3 x robust sigma), not from a guess. Report the chosen value and the sweep (0.02, 0.03, 0.05 m).
- Implementation: new function and a `DepthPrototype` option, default off. Keep the old path unchanged. Add tests: a synthetic depth map with a known plane bias is recovered; objects above the plane are excluded from the fit; gate failure falls back to the anchor fit; defaults leave existing output unchanged. Run the full suite in `.venv` (no torch) and the depth tests in `.venv-depth`.
- Evaluation (existing clips V0-V4, current map `data/logitech-landmark-map-occ.npz`, checkpoint `depth_anything_v2_metric_hypersim_vits.pth`, input size 252):
  - Pass criteria: V0 mean occluded fraction at most 1% and the 5 worst V0 frames at most 5%; V3 frames 0-60 and V4 frames 1240-1300 false hides near 0%; Step 36's 25 control frames stay near 0%; V1 and V2 occluded fractions not lower than before on frames where an occluder is in front (report both numbers).
  - Report per clip: calibration-pixel count, gate pass fraction, fitted terms, residual MAD, post-correction bias by row band, and the same group tables as Step 36 (false-hide group, V0, controls, V2/441 window).
  - Expect this to fix false hides only. The V2/441 hand miss is a close-range model limit and may not move. Record it either way.
- Production design (write as a short subsection in the log, do not implement beyond the option): the plane is a gated prior, not a requirement. Failure cases to measure and list: too few plane pixels visible, a large occluder covering most plane pixels, a non-flat surface around the tag, grazing views. Log the fraction of frames where the gate passes per clip.
- Output: `scripts/step37_plane_calibration.py`, `runs/step37-plane/`, `runs/step37-plane.json` and `.md`, section 6 and section 7 entries. Provenance: checkpoint file and SHA-256, map SHA-256, calibration SHA-256, clip run IDs, input size, margin, gate settings, code hash.
- Hardware: GPU only. No camera.
- Result: no variant meets the pass criteria. Best is `noint` (b*raw + plane in row/col, no offset): V0 mean 4.7%->2.2% (need 1%), V0 worst5 still 25-100%, V3 0-60 14.7%->4.8%, V4 1240-1300 3.6%->2.6%, false-hide group 10.2%->2.2%, controls 0%->1%, V2 431-451 window 1.8%->11.5% (new false hides), V2/441 0%->13%. `full` is unidentifiable on a flat table (raw is nearly planar in row/col, fitted b collapses to ~0.14, gate passes 1-2%); `fixedb` (b from the anchor fit, added on evidence) is no better. Residual MAD stays 2.5-4 cm, above the 2 cm margin: the bias is not a plane. Gate pass fraction per clip and all tables in runs/step37-plane.json/.md. V1/V2 frames with baseline hidden >30% (proxy; no labels): 50%->29% (V1), 53%->37% (V2) under noint, i.e. misses may rise. Observation vs hypothesis: observed that a dense plane fit helps false hides only partly and costs misses; hypothesis that the model's table bias has non-planar structure, so far-range data (Step 39/40) is the next test. Not implemented: production design beyond the default-off `DepthPrototype(plane_correction=PlaneParams(...))` option; failure cases to list from the gate pass fractions (V3 0.55 is the lowest: grazing/too few plane pixels). Housekeeping (patch-median default off) not done, baseline depends on it.

### Step 38: one-off 518 input-size diagnostic (offline, GPU, existing clips)
- [x] Goal: find out whether input size 518 (the model's native size) reduces the row bias or fixes the hand read. This is a diagnostic, not a production choice (518 costs about 95 ms per frame).
- Tasks: re-run Step 36's selection (the 79 frames) and the V2/441 window at input size 518 with nothing else changed. Report the same bias statistics (median, row bands, plane fit of the bias map), the four mask fractions, and the hand-under-polygon depth. Also run the Step 37 correction at 518 once Step 37 exists, and at 392 as a middle point.
- Decision: if 518 clearly lowers the bias or reads the hand nearer than the panel, note the speed cost and propose running the model every k-th frame with a held mask (labelled option, never a fresh measurement). If not, record "no gain" and drop it.
- Output: `runs/step38-input-size.json` and `.md`. Hardware: GPU only.
- Result: no gain from larger input. Anchor-scaled false-hide group is 10.2/7.5/13.3% at 252/392/518 and V0 31/36/26%; row bias does not shrink (518 flattens the top/bottom gradient but the median bias stays about -8 cm). Hand under the polygon reads 0.82-0.84 m vs panel 0.714 m at every size, never nearer. Step 37 noint at 392/518: false-hide group 3.2%/2.6%, V0 mean 3.1%/2.9% (252: 2.2%), V2/441 2%/9% (252: 13%), at roughly 100/180 ms per frame. Decision: record "no gain" and drop 518/392.

### Step 39: far-range capture sheet (ASK SAHAJ; do not capture yourself)
- [ ] Goal: record clips at about 1.5-2.5 m to test whether the failures come from the close-range tabletop regime, with occluders other than hands.
- Priority: Sahaj records while Steps 37-38 run.
- Setup (write into the sheet and log how it was done):
  - Keep the 0.17 m tag (a larger tag is not available). At 2 m the tag side is about 51 px, at 2.5 m about 41 px, at 3 m about 34 px. Stay at or under about 2.5 m.
  - Create a config copy (for example `config-logitech-far.json`) with `tag.min_side_px` lowered from 40 to 25. Check the allowed range in `ar_projection/config.py` first. Keep every other setting. Do not edit `config-logitech.json`.
  - Panel in the config copy: `panel.width_m` 0.60 and `panel.height_m` 0.33707 (about 180 x 101 px at 2 m with the current camera). Keep `offset_y_m` 0.15 and `tilt_x_deg` 90. At 90 degrees the corner Z values are `[0, 0, 0.33707, 0.33707]`, so the panel base stays on the surface; check this after the change. Sahaj's height is used exactly (a strict 16:9 height for 0.60 m would be 0.3375 m).
  - Lock auto-exposure, auto-focus and auto-white-balance, as in Step 28. Keep the tag fixed and flat on a large flat surface (floor or large table) for the whole session.
  - Build a new map from the far scene into a new file (for example `data/logitech-landmark-map-far.npz`). Never overwrite an existing map. Spread texture over the surface and the background.
- Clips (30-60 s each, `--record-input`, HFYU, same commands as Step 28 with the new config and map): M-far map-build; F0 empty scene (false-hide gate, camera moves slowly, a 2-3 s stationary start with the tag visible); F1 occluder in front of the panel with the tag visible; F2 occluder covers the tag and the panel; F3 occluder clearly behind the panel plane; F4 partial edge crossings, slow and fast. Use varied occluders: a person, a box, a chair, a pole, a thin or dark object, and at least one hand pass.
- Ground truth: every pass must be at least 10 cm in front of or behind the panel plane (5 cm is below the expected depth noise at 2 m). Tape-measure it and write one line per pass in a sidecar note (front or behind, approximate distance, object, rough mm:ss).
- Tag-quality check (Claude Code, after recording, before trusting any result): per far clip report tag side in pixels, tag RMS, ambiguity rate and valid-tag fraction with the new `min_side_px`. If the tag pose is poor (RMS above 1 px or ambiguity above about 10% of frames), say so: pose and the plane oracle are less reliable, and Step 40 results must be labelled accordingly.
- Output: a capture sheet and, after recording, a log of run IDs and file sizes.
- Hardware: camera, Sahaj only.

### Step 43: relative-depth model with disparity-space alignment (offline, GPU)
- [x] Goal: test whether the author's relative-depth model, aligned in disparity space, orders near and far surfaces better than the Hypersim metric model. Added in a planning session after Step 40.
- Why: the metric checkpoint is fine-tuned on synthetic indoor rooms (Hypersim, max depth 20 m), a narrow domain. The relative model is trained on much broader data and is generally preferred for ordering and edges. For occlusion the question is ordering (is this pixel nearer than the panel), which a relative model suits. Its output is disparity-like (larger means nearer), known only up to scale and shift, so it must be aligned with `d ~ s/Z + t` from reference depths.
- Warning about earlier evidence: Steps 27-31 loaded `depth_anything_v2_vits.pth` (the relative checkpoint, SHA-256 `715fade13be8f229f8a70cc02066f656f2423a59effd0579197bbf57860e1378`) into the metric class (sigmoid head scaled by `max_depth=20`). That distorts the output, so the Step 31 finding that "inverse depth fits worse than linear depth" is not valid for the relative model run correctly. Do not cite it.
- Tasks:
  1. Add a separate adapter `RelativeDepth` in `occlusion.py` (do not change `MetricDepth`): load the author's non-metric `DepthAnythingV2` class (`depth_anything_v2/dpt.py` in the checkout, encoder `vits`, features 64, out_channels `[48, 96, 192, 384]`) with the relative checkpoint already on disk. Do not download anything. Record file name and SHA-256 in every output.
  2. Alignment modes, one per run: (A) per-frame robust affine fit `d = s/Z + t` (Huber) on landmark anchors, held by a 5-frame median; (B) the same fit on table-plane pixels, with `Z` from the pose (world Z=0 plane, tag excluded, same pixel rules as Step 37); (C) local fit on a band of table pixels around the panel base line. Gate each fit on pixel count and residual; on gate failure fall back to (A), then to "visibility unavailable".
  3. Mask rule: a pixel occludes when `Z_real < Z_panel - margin`, with `Z_real = s/(d - t)` and a guard for `d - t` near zero. Margins: 0.15 m, 0.20 m and 10% of panel depth.
  4. Evaluate on the same frames as Step 36 (V3/26 and 20-30, V4/1270-1280, V0 worst and random, V2/441 window and V2/72, controls) and on the far clips (F-empty gate, F-occ with the Step 41 labels when they exist). Report: false-hide fraction, hand read under the V2/441 polygon (aligned depth against panel Z and table depth), pose source, fit residual, gate pass fraction, and ms per frame at 252 and 392.
  5. Compare against the metric baseline on identical frames and margins. Change one thing per run.
- Decision: if the hand reads nearer than the panel and F-empty hidden fraction is at most 2% at margin 0.15 m, use this model for the next steps. If not, record the result and ask Sahaj. Mention that larger relative models (Base, Large) exist but are not downloaded; ask before suggesting a download.
- Notes for later (do not implement now): for objects standing on the floor, the contact point with the floor gives a depth from the pose and plane geometry alone, which can order a standing person against the panel without trusting model depth; this needs a class-agnostic mask of the occluder.
- Output: `scripts/step43_relative.py`, `runs/step43-relative.json` and `.md`, images as in Step 42 for the worst frames, section 6 and section 7 entries.
- Hardware: GPU only. No camera.
- [x] (done; images of the worst frames not generated)
- Result: full tables in `runs/step43-relative.md`/`.json`. Relative model (SHA-256 715fade1...1378, input 252), margin 0.15 m: false-hide group 0% (metric 2.0%), V0 mean 0.2/0.1/0.0% (A/B/C; metric 0.5%), V0 worst frames 5% or below (metric 93%), F-empty 1.0/0.9/0.9% (metric 1.5%), F-occ 5.6% (metric 6.3%), V4 still about 10% for every variant (unresolved). Hand V2/441: relative A reads 0.691 m, nearer than the panel (0.714 m); B 0.736, C 0.741 (median not nearer, p5 nearer); metric 0.844. Step 41 labels at 0.15: IoU 0.952 (metric 0.892), false-hide 1.7% (7.7%), miss 36.6% (70.4%); the 3 tag/joint frames are 100% miss for both. Mode A raises V2 hidden to 4.6% (metric 0.9%) and its V2/441 window to 10% at the 10%-of-depth margin; B and C do not (0.7%). **Decision rule met by mode A** (hand nearer than panel, F-empty at most 2% at 0.15 m): propose using the relative model for the next steps; not acted on pending Sahaj. Observation vs hypothesis: observed that relative alignment plus a 0.15 m margin removes the near-clip false hides and halves the labelled miss rate; hypothesis that ordering quality, not metric scale, was the limit. ### Step 44: base-line (contact-line) depth reference with a gated threshold (offline, GPU)
- [x] Goal: test Sahaj's idea (25 Oct). The panel's base edge lies on the table, so its extension across the image is a line of known geometry between foreground and background. Use the model depth along that line as a local reference, and call a pixel an occluder only when the model reads it clearly nearer than the reference. A bias shared by the line and the panel then cancels, and false occlusions are gated by the threshold.
- **Decisions by Sahaj (25 Oct, after Step 43), these replace earlier options in this step:**
  - Depth source: the relative model (Step 43, `RelativeDepth`, SHA-256 `715fade1...1378`, input 252) with alignment mode A (landmark anchors, 5-frame median hold, gate on median relative error of 0.25 or less). Do not run the metric model in this step. Do not run alignment modes B or C: on the labels A, B and C differ by 0.5% or less, and B/C fall back to A on most far frames.
  - Margin: one rule, no sweep: `margin = max(0.10 m, 0.10 x Z_panel)`. A pixel occludes when `Z_real < Z_panel - margin`. The percentage is the performance gate that scales with distance. The 0.10 m floor stops the margin shrinking when the panel is close. Do not run a margin sweep (0.05, 0.15, 0.20, 4 x MAD) in this step.
  - Order: run Step 44 first, look at the result, then decide whether more labelling is needed. Sahaj will label more frames if asked; do not start a labelling pass yourself.
- Geometry: the line is the intersection of the panel plane with the table plane (world), extended beyond the panel, projected with the tracker pose. Sample it only where it lies inside the image. Its true depth varies along the line when the camera is oblique, so take the expected depth profile `Z_line(u)` from the pose (ray-plane) and compare it with the model along the line; do not average to one number unless the profile is nearly constant.
- Method (one change per run; default off). Two variants only, on identical frames, same margin rule:
  - **Baseline (A):** relative model, mode A alignment, no line reference. This is the Step 43 mode A result re-scored with the margin rule above, so the comparison is like for like. Do not reuse the 0.15 m numbers from Step 43.
  - **Line (A+L):** the same, plus the line reference below.
  1. Reference from the line: after the mode A alignment, compare the aligned model depth along the clean part of the line with `Z_line`. Fit a shift (and, only if the profile spans enough depth, a scale) between them. If the profile does not span enough depth, take scale from the mode A fit and fit the shift only. Because the test compares a pixel with the line, a common additive bias cancels and a scale error only multiplies a small difference.
  2. Robust statistic: occluders can only make the line read nearer, never farther. Use an upper percentile of the line depth (start at the 70th percentile of depth, i.e. the farther side), or the median of the farther half. Report the line spread after the fit (MAD) as the local noise.
  3. Mask rule: as above, with the corrected depth.
  4. Gate: require a minimum fraction of the line inside the image and clean (for example 30% of its length, at least 40 px), line MAD below a limit, and a held value from a recent good frame when a hand or object covers the line. If the gate fails, hold the last good reference for up to a set time, then fall back to the plain mode A depth (Baseline) and log it. If mode A itself is unavailable, show the panel unoccluded and log "visibility unavailable". Never reuse a stale mask.
- **Headline metric: gate pass rate on the far clips.** Plane gate B passed on only 10-15% of far frames, which is why the far clips mostly ran on mode A. Report the fraction of far frames on which the line reference passes its gate, by panel distance band (under 1.5 m, 1.5-2.5 m, over 2.5 m), and the reasons for failure (line outside image, line covered, MAD too large, profile too short). If the line passes on far frames where plane B did not, that is the main benefit.
- Known limits to report, not hide:
  - An object or the hand covering the line contaminates the reference. The upper-percentile rule and the hold reduce this; report how often the gate fails and why.
  - A hand that the model reads as far is still a miss: this method removes common bias, it does not fix a depth blind spot. Step 43 shows the relative model is the one that reads the hand nearer than the panel.
  - A false nearer blob larger than the margin (halo at an edge, flat-region gradient) still passes the gate. The line only removes common-mode bias.
  - The bias gradient inside the panel region (rows above the base line) is not corrected. Report the residual bias by row inside the panel.
  - The line may fall outside the image (panel near the image border); report how often.
  - Frames where the pose source is tag/joint and still score 100% miss in Step 43 (3 labelled frames) are not expected to change here; list them separately.
- Evaluation: Step 36 frame set (V3/26 and 20-30, V4/1270-1280, V0 worst and random, V2/441 window, V2/72, controls), F-empty and F-occ, and the 17 Step 41 labels. Report, for Baseline and A+L: false-hide fraction, miss rate on labelled front occluders (pooled and per frame; per frame matters because the panel is mostly visible and pooled IoU is misleading), gate pass fraction, line MAD per distance band, hidden fraction against panel depth, and V4 hidden fraction (still about 10% in Step 43; say whether the line changes it).
- Pass criteria (set in advance): F-empty mean hidden at most 2%, V0 mean at most 1%, and a miss rate on the labels no worse than Baseline. A+L is worth keeping if it passes these and either raises the far-frame gate pass rate above plane B's 10-15% or lowers the miss rate or false-hide rate against Baseline.
- Output: `scripts/step44_baseline.py`, `runs/step44-baseline.json` and `.md`, images for the worst frames as in Step 42, section 6 and section 7 entries. Default off; no change to production defaults.
- Hardware: GPU only. No camera.
- [x] (done)
- Result: tables in `runs/step44-baseline.md`/`.json`, images `runs/step44-images/`, scripts `scripts/step44_baseline.py`, `scripts/step44_figures.py`. Relative model, mode A, margin max(0.10 m, 0.10 Z). **Pass criteria all met by Line (A+L):** F-empty 0.8% (baseline 0.7%), V0 0.5% (0.5%), labelled miss 18.7% vs 36.9% for Baseline. **Headline: line gate passes on 74% (F-occ) and 91% (F-empty) of far frames, plus 3-5% held, against plane B's 10-15%**; line MAD 1.5-3.4 cm near, 5-9 cm far. Other effects: V2 mean hidden 9.6% -> 2.2%, V3 1.4% -> 0.2%, V4 11.9% -> 10.3% (not fixed), labelled IoU 0.953 -> 0.960, false-hide 1.8% -> 2.1%. The hand at V2/441 reads 0.726 m with the line (0.691 m baseline), not caught by either rule at this margin. Failure reasons: mostly "scale not positive" from the shift+scale fit (F-occ 363 frames), then line covered, MAD, and line outside image (0-2%). Observation vs hypothesis: observed that referencing the line removes bias on V2/V3 and halves the labelled miss rate without raising far false hides; hypothesis that the "scale not positive" failures come from a nearly flat line profile and could be avoided by always using shift-only (untested; one change per run). Caveat: 17 labels, per-frame misses on thin slivers (e.g. frame 791, where the labelled front silhouette includes the table in front of the base line); labelling convention confirmed by Sahaj (red = in front of the panel plane, blue = behind; everything nearer than the plane counts as front, including table in front of the base line), so the scorer's use of red as hidden is right and thin-sliver misses such as frame 791 are real per the labels, partly from pose error placing the panel's lower edge over foreground. Recommendation: keep A+L, default off, no production default change.

- [x] Goal: let Sahaj run the tracker live (camera, display, recording) with the 0.60 x 0.33707 m panel and the Step 44 variant A+L (relative model, mode A alignment, base-line reference, margin `max(0.10 m, 0.10 x Z_panel)`) actually masking the panel, so he can vary distance and occluders himself and see where the shortcomings are. - Where things stand (checked in the code, 25 Oct): the A+L logic exists only in `scripts/step43_relative.py` (`AlignedPrototype`, mode A) and `scripts/step44_baseline.py` (`LinePrototype`). `LinePrototype.render` is a measurement tool: it computes the aligned depth and the hidden fractions but returns the **unmasked** panel from `panel_layer(T, panel, cam)`. The live path (`ar_projection/cli.py`) can only build `MetricDepth` (Hypersim metric model, 0.02 m margin), requires `--depth-repo`, `--depth-weights` and `--tracking-report` that matches the config fingerprint, and demands `mapping.mode=localize` with `camera.undistort=true`. `config-logitech-far.json` has `mapping.mode` `build`, which cannot run depth. So today a live run cannot use Step 44.
- Do not change: tracking, pose, mapping and calibration code or defaults; `config-logitech.json`; the baseline map; the Step 43 and Step 44 scripts (they must still run and give the same numbers). New behaviour is opt-in through the new config block and flags below, and the new class is the production version of A+L.
- Tasks:
  1. **Production class** `RelativeLineOcclusion` in `ar_projection/occlusion.py` (new class; leave `MetricDepth`, `DepthPrototype` and `RelativeDepth` as they are). Same `render(packet, result, panel, landmark_map, features)` interface as `DepthPrototype`, so `pipeline.run` needs no change. Copy the logic, do not import from `scripts/`: mode A anchor fit (at least 12 anchors, Huber, gate 0.25, 5-frame median hold), then the base-line reference (shift+scale only when the line profile span is at least 0.15, otherwise shift only with scale from mode A; 30th-percentile shift; gates 40 px, 30% clean, relative MAD at most 0.10; hold the last good line fit for 30 frames; then fall back to the mode A depth). Use the same constants as Step 44. Mask rule: a pixel is hidden when `Z_real < Z_panel - max(0.10, 0.10 x Z_panel)`. The returned `(color, visible)` must come from the aligned depth (line reference when fresh or held, else mode A), not from the unmasked panel. When mode A is unavailable, draw the panel unoccluded and set `status` to "visibility unavailable"; never reuse a stale mask. Write no per-frame JSON by default (the `result.diagnostics["occlusion"]` dict is already logged in `frames.jsonl`); save depth and image files only when `--occlusion-output` is given, and do not crash if that folder already exists. Record in the stats: alignment source (anchor, line fresh, line held, line fallback), line reason on failure, fit values, panel Z median, margin at the panel median, hidden fraction, inference ms and total occlusion ms.
  2. **Per-pixel margin.** `panel_layer` takes a scalar `margin_m`. Add an optional `margin_fraction` argument (default 0, so every existing caller is unchanged) and use `margin = max(margin_m, margin_fraction * z)` per pixel inside it. Do not compute the panel twice per frame to get this.
  3. **Config block** `occlusion` in `ar_projection/config.py` with defaults: `enabled` false, `model` "relative", `repo` null, `checkpoint` null, `input_size` 252, `margin_floor_m` 0.10, `margin_fraction` 0.10, `line_reference` true, `line_hold_frames` 30, `require_tracking_acceptance` true. Defaults stay off so old configs and replays are unaffected; the config fingerprint must not change for configs without the block. In `config-logitech-far.json` only, add the block with `enabled` true, `model` "relative", `line_reference` true, and `repo` and `checkpoint` set to the same author checkout and relative checkpoint (`depth_anything_v2_vits.pth`, SHA-256 `715fade1...1378`) that `scripts/step43_relative.py` uses (`REL_CKPT`, `REPO` from `step31_masks`). Record the checkpoint file name and SHA-256 in the run's log.

### Sahaj's live-run notes (25 Oct evening, after Step 45) and Claude's reading of the runs
- Runs: `runs/20261025T081445-adcde7c8`, `runs/20261025T081546-aa877612`, `runs/20261025T081836-22c82ea7` (first session), `runs/20261025T083944-75475811` and `runs/20261025T084746-303f35ec` (later, camera held above head, tag detected out to about 3 m by tape measure).
- Sahaj's observations:
  - Occlusion is poor when the hand is held flat; a vertical hand is relatively fine. A black object behaves the same as the flat hand. **The hand was very close to the camera, not near the panel.** Sahaj's hypothesis: the edges of the flat hand are poorly defined in the depth map, so fingertip depth diffuses into the background; depth needs edge or segmentation guidance.
  - Heavy jitter of the projection, apparently from the ORB landmarks; the tag seemed barely used (mostly landmark, not joint).
  - The tag works at about 3 m when it is visible: the problem in the first session was the viewing angle of the flat tag. Holding the camera above head height made it detectable. Sahaj will test that way with the existing map for now.
  - At about 3 m the panel did not look like 1 m. **Explanation found by Claude: the panel was never 1 m.** `config-logitech-far.json` and the config saved in `runs/20261025T084746-303f35ec/config.json` still have `width_m` 0.60, `height_m` 0.33707. Step 46 changes it.
- Claude's reading of the frames.jsonl files (observation; not yet checked by Claude Code):
  - First session (camera 0.7-1.7 m from the tag origin, median about 1.4 m by the tracker's pose): pose source counts landmark 45-70%, joint 8-16%, tag 2-3%, none 11-49%; tag observations in only 14-15% of frames. Frame-to-frame panel corner motion (median of 4 corners): landmark to landmark median 2.3 px (p90 6-7.5), tag to tag 11-15 px (p90 29-48), landmark to tag switches 25-46 px. Pose loss gaps up to 9.6 s; landmark gate failures are "PnP RANSAC failed" and "insufficient RANSAC inliers"; median landmark inliers 22-26; on a sampled lost frame fresh descriptor matching gave 1 match from 467 features. Median processing 62.7 ms (about 16.8 fps). Occlusion unavailable on 27-52% of frames, mostly because pose was lost.
  - Later session (`084746`, camera above head; tracker distance from tag origin median 1.96 m, max 2.59 m): pose sources tag 177, joint 113, landmark 391, none 697 of 1378 frames (pose valid 49%). Corner motion: joint median 1.7 px (p90 2.9), tag 2.2 px (p90 6.7), landmark 3.0 px (p90 9.1), landmark to tag switch 7.4 px median (p90 13). So when the tag is seen well, tag and joint are the steadiest sources and landmark-only is the noisiest. Note: the tracker's maximum camera distance (2.59 m) is below Sahaj's 3 m tape measure; the two may be measured to different points, or there may be a scale error. Check this (Step 47, task 1).
  - Camera motion is mixed into the corner-motion numbers; jitter on a still hold has not been measured.
- Current pose smoothing (from the code, `ar_projection/rendering.py` `PoseSmoother`): a fixed-weight exponential moving average on the camera pose. Each frame: rotation moves `measurement_weight` (0.40) of the way from the previous smoothed rotation to the new one (axis-angle), translation is `0.6 x previous + 0.4 x new`. It resets on tracking loss, relocalisation or a time gap, but **not** on a pose-source switch. **It is disabled in every config in use** (`filter.enabled` false in `config-logitech-far.json`), so the live runs had no smoothing at all. A fixed-weight EMA trades jitter against lag at all speeds; it has no velocity model.
- Sahaj's question on ORB features: `features.max_features` is 500 per frame and the map has 502 landmarks (`mapping.max_landmarks` 800). The occlusion map (`data/logitech-landmark-map-occ.npz`) was built on 24 Oct from nearer viewpoints. At 2-3 m the same surfaces appear 2-4 times smaller, at the edge of ORB's 8-level, 1.2-factor pyramid (about 4.3x scale range), and new viewpoints see different surfaces. Planned answer (Step 47): test one change at a time on a fixed recorded clip: (a) a map built from the operating viewpoints and distances with the current 500 features; (b) the same with more features per frame (1500-2000) and a larger landmark cap. Report associations, inliers, loss %, jitter and ORB time for each. Do not assume more features fix it before (a) is measured.

### Step 48 pre-check (26 Oct, from `runs/20261025T092056-7a6e0212` logs; no depth maps were saved, model not re-run)
- Sahaj's reading: when the hand enters (worse when flat), far background depth is corrupted and occlusion fails; pose loss happens when the hand covers ORB points.
- Finding (`scripts/step48_fit_ridge.py`): the per-frame fit `d = t + s/Z` is not identifiable. Over 1921 frames at camera distance > 1.3 m, `corr(t, s)` = -0.996 and every fit lies on one line through `d` = 2.24 at `Z` = 1.38 m (the base-line/table depth). Run `084746` shows the same (-0.925). The base line's inverse-depth span is median 0.22 (gate 0.15), so the line fixes one point and the slope floats.
- Effect: `t, s` swing from (-15, 24) to (0, 3) with the camera still. The same model value `d` = 8 maps to Z 0.2-1.0 m (p10-p90). When `t` is very negative, all depths are pulled toward about 1.4-1.7 m: the hand is pushed back behind the panel (misses) and the far background is pulled forward (the "corrupted" far depth). Frames with `t` < -6 hide 0.7% of the panel versus 3.1% otherwise; contact sheet `runs/step48-092056/sheet1.jpg` shows a hand over the panel with 0% hidden (frames 660, 1305).
- So the hand corrupts the fit, which then corrupts both near and far. The model replay below also measures the model's own squeeze.
- Pose loss with the hand over ORB points: not checked in this pass.
- Model replay (26 Oct, CPU, relative vits at 252, every 3rd frame, 702 frames with a logged fit; scripts `scripts/step48_*.py`, which still have the cloud paths hard-coded; outputs in `runs/step48-092056/`). Hand proxy = skin-colour mask (YCrCb), so hand numbers are approximate. The logged hidden fraction reproduces (corr 0.995).
  - Fit: a per-frame Huber fit on table-plane pixels (world Z = 0, about 39k pixels, Z 0.9-1.9 m, inverse-depth span median 0.70) gives hand-on-panel hidden 0.66 (logged 0.54); frames with less than half the hand hidden drop from 42% to 28%. Background false hides rise from 0.9% to 2.0% (white specks near the panel base, from about 10% table fit error).
  - Model squeeze (Sahaj's hypothesis): confirmed, moderate. With a big hand (> 40k skin pixels), the table-fit scale `s` drops 4.78 to 3.29 (-31%), and the table residual doubles (7.8% to 15.4%). The model's far background does not get worse in ordering (sheet2: the raw model map is clean); the per-frame refit absorbs most of the squeeze.
  - Model misses (new): in the remaining failures (sheet3), the model gives the hand about the same `d` as the table behind it. This happens for flat hands lying over the table and for hands or forearms cut off at the image edge. No scale fit or depth fusion can fix pixels where the current frame's depth is already wrong.
  - Hand Z under every fit is about 1.0 m against a panel at about 1.2 m, so the margin test is close to its limit even when the fit is good.
  - Background-prior prototype (`scripts/step48_prior.py`: metric prior at 160x120, warped by pose, fused where it agrees, never fused on near blobs; per-frame fit against prior plus table): hand hidden 0.60, false hides 1.4%. Adding the "nearer than prior" gate changes little. Hiding any foreground blob (much nearer than the prior) on the panel, regardless of panel Z: hand hidden 0.76, frames under half hidden 21%, but false hides 3.1% (part of that may be arm pixels the skin mask missed).
  - Reading: the fit fix (table or prior references) is a clear gain. The temporal prior is most useful as a change detector (what is new against the static scene), not as a depth smoother. Flat and edge hands need a cue other than this model's depth: colour or appearance change against a pose-warped background image, or a segmenter.
- Plan (replaces the old Step 48 order; the old (a)-(c) guidance variants become Step 48c):
  - 48a: make the fit well-conditioned. Fit `t, s` on table-plane pixels (wide depth span) plus mapped anchors, Huber, with near outliers rejected. Log the fit's conditioning (inverse-depth span, `corr` of a bootstrap). Reject fits whose slope is not constrained.
  - 48b: static-background prior (the temporal filter). Keep a metric background depth, warped to the current pose. Each frame: fit `t, s` against the warped prior on pixels that agree with it; a pixel occludes only if it is nearer than the panel by the margin AND nearer than the prior by a margin; update the prior only at pixels that agree with it, with per-pixel confidence. Do not blend the occluder into the prior.
  - 48c: the old edge and segmentation guidance variants, after 48a/48b.

If the `--map` flag is spelled differently, or the map path comes from the config instead, use what the code accepts.

**Task 1: 48a, plane-referenced scale fit (code).**
- In `ar_projection/occlusion.py` add a reference mode `occlusion.reference`: `"line"` (current behaviour, the default so old runs reproduce) or `"plane"`.
- `"plane"`: each frame, compute the true plane depth for pixels on the tag plane (world Z = 0) inside a box around the tag, config `occlusion.plane_extent_m` = [x_min, x_max, y_min, y_max] (default [-1.0, 1.0, -0.6, 0.9]). Sub-sample (about every 2nd pixel). Add mapped-landmark anchors (`collect_anchors`) to the same fit.
- Fit `d = t + s/Z` with a robust fit (Huber IRLS, scale from MAD); then drop residuals beyond 2.5 sigma and refit. The reference is `scripts/step48_analyze.py` `robust_fit`.
- Conditioning gate: accept the fit only if the inverse-depth span of the inliers (p95-p5 over median) is at least `occlusion.plane_min_span` (default 0.30), the inlier fraction is at least 0.5, and `s` > 0. Otherwise hold the last good fit for `line_hold_frames`, then fall back to the anchor fit. Never accept the old ridge-type fits: log a bootstrap or condition-number check of `t, s`.
- Log per frame: `alignment_source` ("plane_fresh" / "plane_held" / "plane_fallback"), fit, plane pixel count, inlier fraction, span, relative residual, fit time.
- Offline check: replay `runs/20261025T092056-7a6e0212` (and its recorded input) with `"plane"` and reproduce the 26 Oct prototype numbers within reason (hand-on-panel hidden about 0.66 versus 0.54 logged; background false hides about 2%). Use `scripts/step48_analyze.py` as the reference. Those scripts have cloud paths hard-coded: change them to repo-relative paths and run them with `.venv-depth` on the GPU. Also report false hides on F-empty (`runs/20261025T000913-e058a920`) with "line" and "plane".

**Task 2: 48b, stored background map and foreground detection (code).**
- New class (for example `BackgroundPrior`) in `occlusion.py`, switched by `occlusion.background_prior.enabled` (default false). Reference implementation: `scripts/step48_prior.py`.
- Map: metric depth of the static background at 160 x 120 with a count per pixel. Each frame, warp it to the current pose (forward splat with z-buffer, using `render_T_CW`). The pose is world-referenced, so keep the map across short tracking losses.
- Update rule: where the new depth agrees with the stored depth (within `agree_tol`, default 0.15 relative), take a running average and raise the count (cap 20). Where there is no stored depth, insert the new depth only if the pixel is not in a near blob, and never store a depth nearer than the panel inside the panel footprint (so a static occluder in front of the panel is never stored as background). Where the new depth is clearly farther (> 1.15 x stored), lower the count and drop the pixel at zero.
- Foreground mask: current Z < `near_ratio` (default 0.8) x stored Z, morphological opening 7 x 7, upsampled to full resolution.
- Hide decision: hide if (Z + margin < Z_panel) OR (foreground AND Z < Z_panel + `fg_slack`). `fg_slack` default 0.1 m. Report a sweep of `fg_slack` = 0.1, 0.5 and no limit; the prototype gave hand hidden 0.67 / 0.76 and false hides 1.35% / 3.1% for 0.1 / no limit.
- The scale fit stays the 48a plane fit. Use stored-map pixels in the fit only where the plane gives fewer than `plane_min_px` pixels (config).
- Side-by-side view (key `v`): add the foreground mask and the stored map as panes.
- Log per frame: map coverage, foreground fraction, hidden-by-foreground fraction, time.
- Tests (`tests/`): identity pose warp returns the same map; a synthetic near blob is never stored; a synthetic `t, s` is recovered by the plane fit; with all new switches off, the output is unchanged from now.
- Offline check: the same 092056 replay and F-empty, with 48a alone and 48a+48b. Use the skin-colour hand proxy from `scripts/step48_analyze.py` and label those numbers as approximate.
- Not in this step: the colour comparison against a pose-warped background image. That comes after the live test, if hands cut off at the image edge still fail.

**Task 3: report to Sahaj.**
- What changed (files, config fields), test count, the offline numbers for line / plane / plane+prior on 092056 and F-empty, and the time per frame added.
- The live command for the new floor map with the best variant switched on, and the keys. Ask Sahaj to run it live with: hand (vertical, flat, at the image edge), a person walking across, and an empty scene.
- Add rows to section 6 (architecture change log) and fill in the Results below.
