**Decisions from Sahaj (do not change without asking):**
- Panels stand upright on the tag plane (the floor now; world Z = 0 is the tag plane, Z up). Array transforms are **yaw about the vertical (world Z) through the array pivot** and **uniform scale about the same pivot**. Pivot = bottom centre of the centre panel. The pivot never leaves the floor.
- Limits in the JSON config, not hard-coded: yaw step 15 deg, yaw limit +-60 deg, scale limit 2.0x maximum. Scale step 1.1x per gesture and scale minimum 0.5x (confirmed by Sahaj).
- Layout at scale 1, yaw 0 (all in the config, metres, 5 cm gaps, gap is a config value and may be raised if occlusion suffers):
  - Centre panel (game): 0.50 wide x 0.28125 tall (16:9), base on the floor, centred on the pivot.
  - Left panel (video, Sahaj calls it "2D projection"): 0.25 wide x 0.444 tall (9:16), base on the floor, inner edge 0.05 m left of the centre panel, already yawed 5 deg. Confirmed by Sahaj: the 5 deg is a yaw about its inner vertical edge, turning the panel towards the viewer (toe-in). Keep the angle and its sign as config values.
  - Right top panel (score) and right bottom panel (player stats): each 0.25 wide x 0.1875 tall (4:3), inner edges 0.05 m right of the centre panel; bottom panel base on the floor, top panel 0.05 m above it.
  - Keep the current `panel.offset_y_m` as the array's offset from the tag.
- Content (hard-coded for now, rendered to a texture of the panel's aspect ratio at about 400 px on the long side):
  - Centre: the current game. A config list of games (name, plus optional video path or placeholder). Placeholder: the game name in large text on the checkerboard.
  - Left: the existing panel-video path (`set_panel_video`/`advance_panel_video`). Thumb up plays, stop sign stops.
  - Score: "1:0" filling most of the panel.
  - Stats: two columns. Column 1 title "Person 1", then the lines "Height: //", "Weight: //", "Rank: //". Column 2 is "Person 2" with the same lines (confirmed).
- Gesture to action map (a config table, so a reversed direction is fixed in JSON, not code):
  - Swiping Left: previous game. Swiping Right: next game.
  - Turning Hand Clockwise: yaw the array one step (15 deg) so the **left** panel comes closer to the viewer (the camera side). Turning Hand Counterclockwise: one step so the **right** panel comes closer. Define the yaw sign by this effect and test it: after one clockwise step, the left panel's centre must be nearer the camera than the right panel's centre.
  - Zooming In With Full Hand: scale up one step. Zooming Out With Full Hand: scale down one step. (Map the "Two Fingers" zoom classes the same way if the model has them.)
  - Thumb Up: continue game and play the left video. Thumb Down: reset yaw and scale to the initial values. Stop Sign: stop game and stop the left video.
- Gestures come from a **second, front-facing camera (the laptop camera)**, not the AR camera, in a **separate thread**.

**Task 1 (after Task 0): array geometry (`ar_projection/rendering.py`, `geometry.py`, `config.py`).**
- New config block `array` (panels list with name/role/size/position/yaw, gap, pivot, limits, steps, yaw sign) with `enabled` (default false: the single panel stays the default, old configs load unchanged).
- `ArrayState` (yaw, scale, game index, playing) and a function that returns each panel's 4 world corners for the current state.
- Near-plane clipping: clip each panel polygon to camera Z >= 0.05 m (Sutherland-Hodgman in camera coordinates) before projecting, then intersect with the image. `visible_panel` must no longer drop a whole panel when one corner is behind the camera. Tests: a panel partly behind the camera gives a clipped polygon; fully behind gives None.
- Panels can overlap after yaw: build one per-pixel panel depth map (nearest panel wins) and one panel-id map, then composite each panel's texture with a perspective warp of its (clipped) corners.
- Per-panel corner covariance and smoothing keep working (the existing pose filter applies once; corners come from the filtered pose).

**Task 2: occlusion over the array (`occlusion.py`).**
- `panel_layer` takes the array depth map instead of one panel. The hide test is per pixel against that depth map, unchanged otherwise.
- Scale reference: if the Step 48a `"plane"` reference exists, use it (it does not depend on any panel). Otherwise keep the base line of the centre panel. Do not tune occlusion in this step; report how it behaves.

**Task 3: gesture thread (`ar_projection/gestures.py`, new).**
- Findings from the 26 Oct check (verify them): the gesture code is in `../dynamic-gesture-classification` (`webcam.py`: `load_model`, `LiveSession`; `webcam_stable.py`: `StableController`; `landmarks.py`: `MediaPipeHand`). It samples hands at 12 Hz, predicts every 0.2 s with a small GRU/LSTM/DD-Net on CPU over a 3 s window, and confirms with 4 of 5 votes (about 1 s). `run_camera` opens its own window, so do not call it; reuse the classes headless.
- Blockers to resolve first (ASK SAHAJ): (a) no trained experiment directory was found on this machine (needed by `load_model`): ask for its path; (b) `mediapipe` is not installed in `.venv-depth` (Python 3.13); the gesture README targets Python 3.14 on Windows. Ask before installing anything. `models/hand_landmarker.task` is not in the gesture folder; a copy exists at `../demo_hand_gesture_recognition/hand_landmarker.task`.
- Design: a daemon thread owns its own `cv2.VideoCapture(gesture.camera_index)` (laptop camera, config), runs hand landmarks, `LiveSession` and `StableController`, and puts **one event per new confirmation** (edge-triggered, plus a config cooldown, default 1.0 s) on a `queue.Queue`. The main loop drains the queue without blocking once per frame and applies events to `ArrayState`. No OpenCV windows or `waitKey` in the thread.
- Failure handling: if the gesture camera or model fails, log it, disable gestures and keep the AR running.
- GIL check: measure AR frame rate with the thread on and off. If it drops by more than about 10%, move the same worker into a `multiprocessing.Process` (spawn) with a `multiprocessing.Queue`; the event interface stays the same.
- Keyboard fallback (also for replay tests): `,` / `.` game previous/next, `j` / `k` yaw left/right, `=` / `-` scale up/down, `t` thumb up, `b` thumb down (reset), `x` stop. Check these do not clash with the existing keys (`q d r s o v p f`).

**Task 4: logging, tests, report.**
- `frames.jsonl` per frame: array state (yaw, scale, game index, playing), gesture events with timestamps and labels, per-panel visibility, timings (array render, occlusion, gesture queue). A separate gesture log with per-prediction label/score and the thread's own rate.
- Tests: corner geometry at known yaw/scale, limits clamp, reset, clipping, event mapping from the config table, edge-trigger and cooldown.
- Offline check: replay `runs/20261025T092056-7a6e0212` with the array enabled and keyboard events scripted (or a fixed state) to show the array renders and occludes. Report time per frame for single panel versus array.
- Report to Sahaj: files and config fields changed, test count, timings, the live command (AR camera + `--gesture-camera` or config index), and a short live test list: each gesture once, rotate to +-60 deg, scale to 2.0x close up (clipping), hand over each panel.

### 2026-10-20, Step 2, association-quality instrumentation
- What changed: added `logging.association_dump` (default false, opt-in), `assoc.jsonl` per-frame writer, descriptor distance/ratio exposed from `match_descriptors` and recorded on fresh `orb_map` matches, `tag_reference_rms_px`/`tag_reference_T_CW` added to withhold-tag diagnostics, and `scripts/score_associations.py` (offline: scores every association against the tag-reference pose on frames with `tag_reference_rms_px <= 1px`). 2 new tests added (42/42 total pass). No default-config behavior changed (confirmed by the dump-disabled-vs-enabled `frames.jsonl` equality test).
- Runs (IDs / output files): dump run `runs/20261019T133214-ecda4960` (lk_periodic variant, tag withheld, `config-exp-dump.json`, full 1,131-frame baseline clip); scorer output `runs/assoc-score-lk_periodic.json` (+ `.md`).
- Numbers (quote from `runs/assoc-score-lk_periodic.json` and ad hoc aggregation over the same dump): `scored_frames` 966/1131 (frames with a trustworthy tag reference, RMS<=1px). Overall `recall_within_3px` 0.0453, `precision_within_3px` 0.2151. Per `association_source`, n and within-3px rate: `orb_map` n=841, rate=0.0392 (worst); `lk` n=13258, rate=0.2358; `orb_redetection` n=6305, rate=0.1562. Per-frame precision at the very start of the clip: frame 0 = 1/8 (0.125), frames 1-14 range 0.16-0.32; mean precision frames 0-179 = 0.185, frames >=180 = 0.222 (no meaningful degrade-after-LOST pattern; bad from frame 0, consistent with the pre-existing diagnostic in section 3 bullet 5, ~4/26 ≈ 0.15 in the first 180 frames).
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): data, not assumption. Inherited associations (`lk`+`orb_redetection`) are 96% of volume (19,563/20,404) and are wrong 76-84% of the time — consistent with hypothesis 1 (stale IDs ride LK/redetection once assigned, section 3). But freshly-matched `orb_map` associations are *worse* than the inherited ones (96% wrong vs 76-84%), and overall recall of landmarks that are actually visible under the tag-reference pose is only 4.5%, present from frame 0. A pure ID-lifecycle fix (Step 3: clear stale IDs) can only route features back into descriptor-based re-matching — and that re-matching is itself the worst-performing path. This points primarily at hypothesis 2/the map's representation (one descriptor per landmark, built from a single first-accepted view) rather than ID staleness alone.
- Decision / next step: per this step's own decision rule ("If fresh `orb_map` descriptor matches are mostly wrong or recall is low in specific views: do Step 4 first"), go to **Step 4** (map coverage/descriptor representation) before Step 3. Step 3's ID-lifecycle experiment is not ruled out as a secondary improvement, but fixing descriptor matching first is the data-supported priority.

### 2026-10-20, Step 4, map coverage / multi-view descriptor bank
- What changed: offline analysis of the existing frozen map (no code); implemented a bounded multi-view descriptor bank as a backward-compatible, off-by-default map schema extension (`mapping.multiview_descriptor_bank`, see section 6); rebuilt an experimental 169-landmark map with the bank enabled by replaying the original build recording; ran it and an exact-match control map (same landmarks, bank stripped) through the association dump + scorer on the full baseline clip.
- Runs (IDs / output files): build replays `runs/20261019T134507-249ed477` (multiview, succeeded, 169 landmarks/21 keyframes) and `runs/20261019T134931-f88ec5b1` (control rebuild attempt, **failed** — `time_budget_exceeded`, see interpretation); maps `data/logitech-landmark-map-multiview.npz` and `data/logitech-landmark-map-multiview-control.npz` (derived, not rebuilt); dump runs `runs/20261019T140503-44fe942e` (control) and `runs/20261019T140720-9ee0cbff` (multiview); scorer outputs `runs/assoc-score-control.json`, `runs/assoc-score-multiview.json`.
- Numbers (quote from artifacts): offline map analysis — `support` mostly at floor 3 (54/63 active landmarks), corr(support,precision)=-0.011, corr(rms,precision)=-0.289, 32/95 landmarks visible-but-never-associated across the clip. Rebuild nondeterminism — original live build: 95 landmarks/23 keyframes; replay attempt 1 (bank=2): 169 landmarks/21 keyframes, refinement `elapsed_ms: 4900.9` (just under the 5000ms `refinement_time_budget_s`); replay attempt 2 (bank=0, same recording/config): refinement `outcome: "time_budget_exceeded"` at `elapsed_ms: 5003.2`, `bootstrap_reason: "keyframe capacity reached before map became ready"`, `map_ready: false` — i.e. build-mode is **not** reproducible run-to-run on this machine, unlike the bit-exact localize-mode replay from Step 1. Final comparison (control vs multiview, same 169 landmarks, only the bank differs) on `pose_source_counts` from `summary.json`: control `{"none": 910, "landmark": 221}` (19.5% valid), multiview `{"none": 1131}` (**0% valid, every frame**). Scorer: control `recall_within_3px`=0.0237, `precision_within_3px`=0.168, total fresh+inherited associations ≈20,595 over the clip; multiview `recall_within_3px`=0.0097, `precision_within_3px`=0.3793, but total associations collapsed to ≈4,190 (5x fewer). Aggregated `match_descriptors` stats across all frames: control avg 3.2 fresh matches/frame from a 169-row train pool; multiview avg **0.4** fresh matches/frame from the enlarged 507-row pool, with `ratio_rejected` rising from avg 111.1/frame to 138.8/frame.
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): two separate findings, not one. (1) Build-mode bootstrap is wall-clock-timing-dependent (`refinement_time_budget_s` is real seconds, not a frame/iteration count), so repeated rebuilds of the same recording are not guaranteed to reproduce — a genuine limitation of the current code, independent of this step's feature, noted here rather than silently retried until it looked clean. (2) The naive multi-view bank **regresses availability to zero**, not because the extra descriptors are wrong, but because flat-concatenating them into one shared training pool for Lowe's ratio test (`a.distance >= ratio_test*b.distance`, comparing best vs. second-best across ALL rows) makes the test far stricter as the pool triples in size: the gap between best and second-best shrinks simply from having 3x the candidates, so the ratio test now rejects most genuinely-correct matches as "ambiguous" that it would have accepted against the smaller single-descriptor pool. Precision looks better on the tiny surviving sample, but that is a selection artifact, not a real improvement — availability (the metric that matters per section 4) collapsed from 19.5% to 0%.
- Decision / next step: do not enable `mapping.multiview_descriptor_bank` in any shipped config as implemented (original cut). The correct fix is to aggregate each landmark's multiple descriptors to a single best-distance-to-this-landmark figure *before* running the ratio test across landmarks (not raw rows) — implemented next, see the follow-up entry below. Step 3 (ID lifecycle) remains untried and is unaffected by this result.

### 2026-10-20, Step 4 continued, grouped ratio-test fix
- What changed: added `group_match_descriptors()` (`ar_projection/features.py`) — computes full pairwise Hamming distances (via `np.unpackbits`+`cdist`, exact match to `cv2.NORM_HAMMING`'s bit-level distance), reduces to one best-distance-per-landmark before the ratio test, so a landmark's own second view can no longer masquerade as the "ambiguous second-best" competitor. `landmarks.associate()` now calls this instead of flat-concatenation when `m.extra_descriptors is not None`, unchanged otherwise. Added a focused regression test (`test_group_match_descriptors_avoids_flat_ratio_test_false_ambiguity`) that reproduces the exact failure mode by construction: a landmark with two close-but-not-identical views and a genuinely different landmark further away — the flat matcher rejects the correct match (ratio ambiguity against its own second view), the grouped matcher accepts it. 46/46 tests pass. Known simplification (flagged with a `ponytail:` comment in the code): the grouped matcher does not do a reciprocal/mutual-match pass (`cfg.features.mutual_matching` is ignored for this path).
- Runs (IDs / output files): dump run `runs/20261019T225035-4ec2d88f` (multiview map, same 169 landmarks, now via `group_match_descriptors`); scorer output `runs/assoc-score-multiview-fixed.json`.
- Numbers: `pose_source_counts` `{"none": 1051, "landmark": 80}` → 7.07% valid (up from 0%, still well below the no-bank control's 19.5%). Fresh (`orb_map`) match volume recovered and then some: avg 4.5 matches/frame (vs control's 3.2/frame, multiview-broken's 0.4/frame) — confirms the ratio-test fix works as intended. But precision dropped further than the control despite more matches: scorer `precision_within_3px`=0.1267 (control was 0.168), and by-source median error got *worse* for every source, including `lk` (median 121.3px vs control's 18.4px) and `orb_redetection` (15.7px vs control's 18.5px, about even). `mutual_rejected` is 0 for every frame (expected — that check is not implemented in the grouped path), versus control's avg 4.5/frame real rejections.
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): the grouped ratio test fixed the catastrophic collapse (0%→7%) exactly as diagnosed, confirming that diagnosis was correct. But the bank is still a net regression against the simpler single-descriptor map (19.5%→7.07% valid-pose), and the likely reason is the dropped mutual-match check: in the control, mutual/reciprocal verification was silently doing real filtering work (avg 4.5 rejections/frame); the grouped matcher has no equivalent, so more matches get through, and a larger fraction of them are wrong — which then also pollutes downstream `lk`-inherited associations once a wrong ID rides forward on a track (consistent with hypothesis 1 from section 3). This is a second, compounding negative result for the bank-as-implemented, not a contradiction of the first.
- Decision / next step: do not enable `mapping.multiview_descriptor_bank` yet. The next bounded increment would be adding a reciprocal/mutual check to `group_match_descriptors` (match train→query at the landmark level the same way, and require agreement) before trying this again — flagged to Sahaj as a decision point rather than implemented without asking, given two rounds of measured regression already. Everything implemented so far (schema, config flag, `_descriptor_bank`, `group_match_descriptors`) stays in the codebase, default-off and fully tested, as a base for that next attempt.

### 2026-10-20, Step 4 continued again, landmark-level reciprocity
- What changed: added a landmark-level mutual/reciprocal check to `group_match_descriptors` (a query's winning landmark must also have this query as its own globally-closest query this frame, computed by reusing the already-built `group_min` matrix — `np.argmin(group_min, axis=0)`). New test `test_group_match_descriptors_rejects_non_reciprocal_landmark_match` constructs two queries competing for the same landmark and confirms only the truly-closer one keeps the match. 47/47 tests pass.
- Runs (IDs / output files): dump run `runs/20261019T225518-dfd683d7`; scorer output `runs/assoc-score-multiview-mutual.json`.
- Numbers: `pose_source_counts` `{"none": 967, "landmark": 164}` → **14.5% valid** (up from 7.07% with the ratio-test fix alone, up from 0% with the original naive bank — but still below the no-bank control's **19.5%**). Scorer: `precision_within_3px`=0.1434 (control 0.168), `recall_within_3px`=0.0225 (control 0.0237, about even). `orb_map` median error got *worse* even as its count recovered to 1,233 (comparable to control's 1,213): median 120.7px vs control's 33.1px.
- Interpretation: three fixes in a row (ratio-test grouping, then mutual matching) each closed part of the gap (0%→7%→14.5% valid-pose) without yet reaching the simpler single-descriptor map's 19.5%. The remaining gap is not explained by either known bug: landmark-level mutual matching catches queries fighting over the *same* landmark, but it cannot catch a single query that happens to resemble one of a landmark's 3 stored views by chance when no one else is competing for it — tripling each landmark's descriptor count triples that chance. This looks like a structural ceiling of flat appearance-only multi-view matching on *this* map (23 keyframes, most within a fairly confined arc — "more keyframes or points are not automatically better" per section 5), not a remaining implementation bug.
- Decision / next step: stop iterating on the matching algorithm here — two further fixes have produced diminishing returns and neither has crossed the no-bank baseline. Do not enable `mapping.multiview_descriptor_bank` in any shipped config; the schema/config/matcher groundwork stays in the codebase (default off, fully tested) in case Sahaj wants to revisit it with a map that has genuinely wider per-landmark view diversity (which needs a new, more deliberately varied capture — hardware, ask first) rather than more matching-side patches. Recommend returning to **Step 3** (ID lifecycle) next, since Step 4's marginal returns have flattened out below baseline.

### 2026-10-20, Step 3, map-ID lifecycle experiment
- What changed: implemented `features.id_revalidation` (3 modes + off, see section 6) and ran each mode plus `off` on the same lk_periodic/withhold-tag/1,131-frame setup as Steps 1-2, on the original baseline map (`data/logitech-landmark-map.npz`, not the Step 4 experimental maps).
- Runs (IDs / output files): `off` `runs/20261019T230309-563fede2`, `loss_clear` `runs/20261019T230354-1cfc8860`, `age_expire` `runs/20261019T230437-fe15f9cb`, `descriptor_check` `runs/20261019T230523-82f70363`; scorer outputs `runs/assoc-score-idreval-{mode}.json`.
- Numbers (quote from `summarize()` / `pose_source_counts` / scorer / aggregated `ids_cleared_by_revalidation`):

  | Mode | Valid fraction | Reference median / p95 px (n) | Scorer precision@3px | Scorer recall@3px | IDs cleared/frame (avg) |
  |---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|
  | off (baseline) | 17.77% | 1.98 / 6.39 (n=712) | 0.2151 | 0.0453 | 0 |
  | loss_clear | **0.00%** | n/a (n=0) | 0.0952 | 0.0087 | 6.43 |
  | age_expire | 0.88% | 4.97 / 5.63 (n=40) | 0.1269 | 0.0225 | 3.37 |
  | descriptor_check | 4.24% | **0.98 / 1.72** (n=192) | 0.1725 | 0.0314 | 0.17 |

  (`off` here reproduces the known lk_periodic figure from the comparison table in section 2 — 17.77% — confirming the id_revalidation default path is unchanged, as `test_id_revalidation_default_leaves_pipeline_output_unchanged` also asserts directly.)
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): every mode made availability *and* overall match precision worse than doing nothing, in a clean dose-response relationship with how aggressively it clears ids (loss_clear clears most aggressively, every frame while LOST, and is worst/catastrophic; descriptor_check clears most conservatively and is closest to baseline). `loss_clear` collapsing to exactly 0% is itself informative, not just "bad": once LOST, it wipes inherited ids on *every single frame* while LOST, never letting partial recovery progress accumulate before the next wipe, so the system can never re-cross the 12-inlier RANSAC threshold once it first drops into LOST — a self-reinforcing trap. `descriptor_check` is the one partial exception worth noting precisely: its accepted poses are *more* accurate (0.98px median / 1.72px p95, versus baseline's 1.98/6.39), because it is pickier about which inherited ids it keeps — but it accepts far fewer of them (4.24% vs 17.77%), and per the plan's own rule, more accuracy on fewer poses is not a win when availability is the metric that must clear 95%. This result is consistent with, and reinforces, hypothesis 1 from section 3 (inherited ids ride along largely uncorrected) **and** the Step 2/4 finding that fresh descriptor-based matching on this map is itself unreliable: clearing an id only helps if fresh matching can then recover it correctly, and on this map/recording it mostly can't, so revalidation just trades a flawed-but-useful signal for a worse one.
- Decision / next step: do not enable `features.id_revalidation` in any config. Keep the current behavior (inherited ids are not revalidated). Section 4's success criteria remain unmet by any single-change experiment tried so far (Steps 2-4); the common thread across all of them is that fresh/recovered descriptor matching is the weak link, not the ID-carrying mechanism itself. A future attempt at Step 3 or Step 4 should be paired with a map that has genuinely better view coverage (new, deliberately varied capture — hardware, ask Sahaj) rather than another matching-side algorithm change on the current 95-landmark map.

### 2026-10-20, replan after Steps 1-4
- What changed: no code. Instruction.md updated with a CURRENT PRIORITY block and new Steps 9-12. Claude Code's Step 4 note suggested a new, more varied capture. That suggestion is deferred.
- Interpretation: the log shows fresh `orb_map` matches about 96% wrong and 4.5% recall from frame 0, but it does not yet show why. Candidate causes: ORB does not re-detect the mapped points, the matcher rejects or confuses correct pairs without a prior, the single stored descriptor does not generalize across views, or map geometry. Only the oracle diagnostic separates these. The cold-start withhold-tag test also never gives the tracker a prior pose, which is not how the system is meant to be used.
- Decision / next step: do Step 9 first, then Step 10, then Step 11 and Step 12. Hardware only if the Step 9 decision table points to coverage or Steps 9-12 are exhausted. Ask Sahaj before any capture.

### 2026-10-20, Step 9, oracle diagnostic
- What changed: wrote `scripts/oracle_diagnostic.py` (see section 6) and ran it on the same lk_periodic-withhold-tag clip/map/config as Steps 1-2 (`runs/20261019T133214-ecda4960/config.json`), using that run's `assoc.jsonl` to mark the "visible but never associated" landmarks. No source/tracker/config change.
- Runs (IDs / output files): `runs/oracle-diagnostic.json` (+ `.md`), full 1,131-frame clip, 966 frames scored (same RMS<=1px tag-reference filter as Step 2).
- Numbers (quote from `runs/oracle-diagnostic.json`): 88,338 kept-landmark occurrences (landmark visible under the reference pose, 10px border, outside the tag mask). Detector hit rate: 3px=48.4%, 5px=65.9%, **10px=81.7%** — moderate-to-high, not a detection failure. True-pair Hamming distance (only computed when a keypoint exists within 10px, i.e. on the 81.7%): median=101, p90=139 — both *above* the matcher's 60-distance limit. Best-*other*-landmark distance median=54 — **lower** than the true pair's own 101. `matcher_would_accept_fraction`=1.2%. `rejection_reasons`: `distractor_closer`=66,720 (93.6% of all rejections), `ratio_rejected`=2,618, `mutual_rejected`=905, `hamming_rejected`=1,053. Landmark-level hit-rate@3px spread is wide (min 0, median 0.45, max 1.0; 17/95 landmarks almost never hit, 12/95 almost always hit) but octave/scale shows no meaningful effect (53-63% hit rate across all 8 octaves). Map-geometry side check: median keypoint-vs-projection pixel offset magnitude across 10s time bins is 0.31px (p90 0.79px) — no systematic view-dependent drift. The 30 never-associated landmarks (found via this run's own `assoc.jsonl`, vs Step 4's "32" — a small, expected discrepancy from slightly different visibility criteria between this script's 10px-border/tag-mask check and `score_associations.py`'s plain in-image check) are not categorically different: hit_rate_3px=0.43, accept_fraction=0.0025, close to the overall figures.
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): the detector is not the bottleneck — ORB finds *a* keypoint within 10px of the true location 82% of the time, and scale/octave has no effect. The bottleneck is squarely descriptor distinctiveness given only one stored view per landmark: even when a keypoint is found at essentially the right place (median geometric offset under 1px — ruling out map/calibration drift), its descriptor usually resembles some *other* landmark's single stored descriptor more closely than its own (93.6% of rejections are "a distractor was closer", not a borderline ratio/hamming/mutual call). This is exactly decision-table row 3: hit rate high-ish, true-pair distance large, and it's a coverage problem, not a matcher-rule problem or a geometry problem. It also squares with Step 4: a multi-view bank built from the *same* recording's existing keyframes didn't help (and modestly hurt even after two matching-side fixes) — consistent with the recording itself lacking enough genuine viewpoint diversity for any multi-view scheme built from it to add real information, rather than the matching algorithm being at fault.
- Decision table row applied: **"Hit rate high, true-pair distance large for specific views, small for others → stored single-view descriptor does not generalize across those views: coverage problem → new, deliberately varied capture is justified."**
- Decision / next step: per the CURRENT PRIORITY block's hardware gate, this is the explicit trigger condition. **Stopping here. Not proceeding to Steps 10-12. Not starting any capture.** Asking Sahaj what to capture: deliberately varied viewpoints/distances/angles around the mapped workspace (the original build's 23 keyframes were evidently too narrow in viewing-angle spread for single-view ORB descriptors to generalize), replacing the wall-clock BA time budget with a deterministic one first per the Step 4 note so the rebuild is reproducible.

### 2026-10-20, replan after Step 9 (Step 9b added)
- What changed: no code. Instruction.md updated: Step 9b added, CURRENT PRIORITY and hardware gate revised. Step 9's own result and log entry are unchanged.
- Interpretation: Step 9 showed a detector hit rate of 81.7% at 10 px, true-pair distance median 101 (near random for 256 bits), best-other-landmark distance median 54 (far below random), and 93.6% of rejections as "distractor closer". It labelled this decision-table row 3 (coverage). Review found row 3 is not yet demonstrated: no per-view breakdown shows small distances in any view, the "true pair" was the nearest keypoint within 10 px (only 48.4% have one within 3 px), and the low distractor distance suggests near-duplicate or clustered landmarks or a stored-descriptor defect. Those alternatives (map-building defect, pairing artifact, near-duplicates) are fixable offline with no new capture.
- Decision / next step: do Step 9b before any capture or Step 10. The hardware gate opens only if Step 9b's decision table lands on genuine coverage. Ask Sahaj before any capture.

### 2026-10-23, Step 9b, descriptor validity and coverage check
- What changed: fixed the wall-clock BA budget (section 6, now a deterministic evaluation cap) and confirmed a build replay now reproduces the frozen map bit-for-bit. Wrote `scripts/descriptor_validity_check.py` for all 4 tasks. Along the way, found and fixed two bugs in the diagnostic tooling itself (not the tracker): a performance bug (per-landmark redundant backward-matching, turned a <1-minute job into a multi-hour hang) and a correctness bug (hand-rolled replay diverged from the real `Pipeline`, producing a wrong 85-landmark map) — the second one is exactly the kind of thing this whole exercise is trying to catch, just in diagnostic code instead of production code. Full numbers, interpretation and decision table are in section 5's Step 9b Result (not duplicated here).

- Decision table row applied: **last row — descriptor not repeatable even at nearly the same view** (not detector repeatability, not matcher rules, not coverage, not map-building). Task 3 directly contradicts the coverage row: true-pair distance is already near-random (median 91 bits) in the 0-5° view-change bin and barely rises from there.
- Decision / next step: **hardware gate stays closed.** Do not capture new data. Ask Sahaj whether the build recording and the 1,131-frame test clip were taken under the same lighting/exposure/focus conditions — task 2 shows descriptors already drift (0→58 bits) across small viewpoint changes *within* the build session alone, and drift further (→91-139 bits) against the separately-recorded test clip, which is more consistent with an image-conditions/camera-settings difference between sessions than with anything fixable by more or wider-spread keyframes. Compare an actual frame crop from each recording for the same landmark before writing more code.

### 2026-10-23, Step 9b follow-up, visual crop comparison
- What changed: Sahaj confirmed the build recording and test clip used the same lighting/exposure/focus, ruling out a deliberate settings change between sessions. Wrote `scripts/compare_build_vs_test_frames.py` (read-only, offline) to do exactly what the decision table's last row asks: pull an actual pixel crop of the same landmark from a build keyframe and the test clip and compare them, rather than reasoning from aggregate numbers alone.
- Runs (IDs / output files): `runs/build-vs-test-crops.png` (4 landmarks: 13213, 27802, 28083, 28084; columns = build source view, build other view, test-clip view).
- Numbers: landmark 27802 — build source brightness 124.5, test-clip brightness **220.0** (crop also visibly blurrier/washed out) despite "same conditions." Landmarks 28083/28084 — brightness close between build and test (167→160, 171→161, within ~5%) but both crops show a smooth, low-texture gradient surface with little edge content. Landmark 13213 — no keypoint within 3px found anywhere in the 1,131-frame test clip (blank crop).
- Interpretation: two compounding, non-exclusive explanations, not one. (1) Some landmarks (28083/28084) sit on weakly-textured, near-featureless surface — exactly where ORB descriptors are least discriminative regardless of session, consistent with the 58-bit drift already seen *within* the build session alone. (2) At least one landmark (27802) shows a large, visible brightness/sharpness difference between the two recordings even though the nominal camera settings were the same — plausibly localized auto-exposure/auto-focus adaptation (reacting to whatever happened to be in frame at that moment) rather than a deliberate setting change, or motion blur from faster camera movement in one of the two sessions. Both point away from "needs a more varied capture" and toward "ORB on this physical setup is inherently fragile, made worse for specific points by moment-to-moment exposure/motion differences a fixed-settings claim doesn't rule out."
- Decision / next step: still no capture. If pursued further, the next cheap check (not yet done) would be comparing optical flow magnitude / frame-to-frame motion between the two recordings as a motion-blur proxy, and checking whether `camera.undistort`/auto-exposure was literally locked (not just "set the same") during capture. Otherwise, this is now Sahaj's call: accept landmark-only tracking is capped on this physical setup/texture and move to Step 10 (LK drift) or Step 11 (guided matching, which sidesteps global descriptor matching's fragility by searching near the predicted position instead) rather than chasing descriptor robustness further.

### 2026-10-23, Step 11, pose-prior guided matching
- What changed: implemented `features.guided_matching` (default off) and `guided_matches()` (section 6); integrated into `associate()`/`pipeline.py`. Chosen by Sahaj ahead of Step 10 — noted as a deviation, Step 10 is still unlogged. Full design/test detail is in section 5's Step 11 Result, not duplicated here.
- Runs (IDs / output files): `runs/20261022T183859-e386b622` (guided off, reproduces the known 17.77% baseline exactly), `runs/20261022T184029-9f0eb386` (guided on).
- Numbers: valid fraction 17.77%→**26.61%** (201→301 valid frames of 1,131); reference error median 1.98px→1.91px, p95 6.39px→8.71px (worse but under the 10px target), samples 712→1,064; guided matching fired avg 1.6 associations/frame (1,825 total across the clip) — genuinely active, not a no-op.
- Interpretation: a real, measured improvement even on a test setup this step's own text says shouldn't show much benefit (cold-start withhold-tag rarely carries a prior pose). Consistent with the Step 9/9b finding that global descriptor matching is the weak link — guided matching sidesteps exactly that by searching a small geometric window instead of the whole map, and it helps even with the limited opportunities this evaluation gives it to run.
- Decision / next step: keep `features.guided_matching.enabled=False` as the shipped default for now (still well short of the 95% target, and p95 reference error got modestly worse). Step 12 (hand-off protocol: tag visible, then hidden) is the evaluation this feature actually needs, since it would give guided matching a real prior going into every hidden window instead of starting cold.

### 2026-10-23, Step 12, hand-off test protocol
- What changed: implemented `withhold_tag_windows`/`window_metrics()`/`hand_off()` (section 6). Defined 5 withhold windows on the baseline clip (`runs/20261019T091439-6ea9d598`, timestamps run 3875.656-3948.062s): two 5s (`3877.656:3882.656`, `3885.656:3890.656`), two 10s (`3893.656:3903.656`, `3906.656:3916.656`), one 20s (`3919.656:3939.656`), each separated by a gap for the tag to be visible again. Ran `baseline` (guided off) vs `guided` (guided on) over the same windows, same map/config as Steps 1-2/11. One bug found and fixed along the way: `hand_off()` initially crashed writing its own output JSON (`write_json` choked on a raw `Path` object from `run()`'s return value) — fixed by `str()`-ing it; the two replays themselves had already completed correctly, so the already-written run directories were reused for the metrics rather than re-running (both verified by their own `config.json`'s `guided_matching.enabled` value).
- Runs (IDs / output files): `runs/step12-handoff.json`; baseline run `runs/20261022T185641-f093e789`, guided run `runs/20261022T185808-ebb84f6e`.
- Numbers (quote from `runs/step12-handoff.json`):

  | Window | Length | Baseline valid fraction | Guided valid fraction | Baseline ref median/p95 px (n) | Guided ref median/p95 px (n) |
  |---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
  | 3877.656:3882.656 | 5s | 0.0% | 0.0% | n/a (n=0) | n/a (n=0) |
  | 3885.656:3890.656 | 5s | 0.0% | 0.0% | n/a (n=0) | n/a (n=0) |
  | 3893.656:3903.656 | 10s | 0.0% | 0.0% | n/a (n=0) | n/a (n=0) |
  | 3906.656:3916.656 | 10s | 0.0% | 0.0% | n/a (n=0) | n/a (n=0) |
  | 3919.656:3939.656 | 20s | 36.04% | **61.69%** | 1.93 / 6.14 (n=352) | 1.91 / 10.24 (n=620) |

  `time_to_first_lost_s` was ~0.02-0.06s (essentially frame 1) for every window in both variants — the moment the tag is withheld, the very next frame already needs a landmark-only pose, consistent with every other step's findings. `recovery_after_window_s` for windows 1-4 is large (57.1s, 49.1s, 36.1s, 23.1s) and, not coincidentally, each lands within ~0.1s of the 20s window's *end* — the first supported pose after windows 1-4 close doesn't occur in the short gaps between them at all, it occurs only once window 5 (a region with real tag/landmark activity) is reached.
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): two findings, not one. (1) **A real, large guided-matching win where there's anything to measure**: in the one window with actual baseline activity, guided matching raised valid fraction from 36.0% to 61.7% (+71% relative) with median reference error essentially unchanged (1.93→1.91px) — consistent with, and considerably stronger than, Step 11's own cold-start result (17.77%→26.61%), exactly as expected since this window gets a real prior pose going in. p95 reference error got noticeably worse again (6.14→10.24px, crossing the 10px target) — the same tradeoff Step 11 saw, now more pronounced with more guided samples (n=620 vs 352). (2) **A window-placement artifact, not a guided-matching result**: windows 1-4 show *zero* difference between baseline and guided because neither variant has *any* nearby valid pose to form a prior from — this region of the clip is persistently poor for both. Guided matching cannot help where there is no prior to begin with; this is a property of where the windows were placed (evenly spaced, not informed by where the baseline already achieves validity), not a finding about the feature. A future run should pick windows adjacent to stretches where the baseline is already getting some valid poses, so guided matching has a real prior to exploit going in.
- Decision / next step: the headline number to carry forward is the one window with real signal — **36.0% → 61.7%**, a materially larger effect than Step 11's cold-start 17.77%→26.61%, at the same small accuracy cost (p95 degradation). This strengthens the case for guided matching as a real, worthwhile improvement, but the p95 cost and the still-large gap to the 95% target mean `features.guided_matching.enabled` stays off by default for now. If pursued further: rerun with windows deliberately placed next to the clip's stronger stretches (not evenly spaced) for a cleaner measurement, and investigate the p95 degradation specifically (likely a small number of poor guided matches pulling the tail) before considering turning it on by default.

### 2026-10-23, Step 12 rerun, windows placed in the clip's productive region
- What changed: the first attempt's 4 "dead" windows were a window-placement artifact, not a finding — confirmed by scanning the baseline lk_periodic run for its actual consecutive-valid-pose stretches, which found only one real productive region in this whole clip: ~3924-3946s (22s). Reran `hand_off()` with all 5 windows repositioned inside/around that region (overlapping is fine here; each window's metrics are independent): 5s `3930:3935`, 5s `3936:3941`, 10s `3925:3935`, 10s `3934:3944`, 20s `3924:3946`. Same map/config, no code changes.
- Runs (IDs / output files): `runs/step12-handoff-v2.json`; baseline `runs/20261022T193512-07f7fa17`, guided `runs/20261022T193600-ab654a47`.
- Numbers (quote from `runs/step12-handoff-v2.json`):

  | Window | Length | Baseline valid | Guided valid | Baseline ref median/p95 px (n) | Guided ref median/p95 px (n) |
  |---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
  | 3930:3935 | 5s | 43.6% | **96.2%** | 1.68 / 2.60 (n=132) | 2.07 / 8.24 (n=248) |
  | 3936:3941 | 5s | 100.0% (ceiling) | 98.6% | 2.03 / 4.61 (n=204) | 1.50 / 2.28 (n=204) |
  | 3925:3935 | 10s | 27.0% | **70.4%** | 1.85 / 7.99 (n=168) | 2.10 / 12.58 (n=392) |
  | 3934:3944 | 10s | 92.0% | 96.0% | 1.94 / 4.44 (n=460) | 1.59 / 4.42 (n=456) |
  | 3924:3946 | 20s | 58.7% | **83.8%** | 1.97 / 6.29 (n=704) | 1.86 / 8.76 (n=996) |

- Interpretation: a clean, consistent result now that every window has real baseline activity to improve on. In the two windows where baseline struggles most (27-44% valid), guided matching very nearly *triples* the valid-pose rate (27.0%→70.4%, 43.6%→96.2%). In the 20s window spanning the whole productive region, 58.7%→83.8%. In the one window where baseline was *already* at the 100% ceiling (3936:3941), guided matching can't improve on that and comes in 1.4 points lower (98.6%) — a negligible, expected ceiling effect, not a regression. Reference median error is a wash (better in 3/5 windows, slightly worse in 2/5, never by much). p95 error is noisier and usually worse with guided matching, but this is largely a *survivor-bias* artifact: baseline's p95 is computed over a smaller, easier subset of frames (the ones it managed to get valid at all), while guided succeeds on substantially more frames, including harder ones baseline never attempted — so a wider p95 alongside roughly 1.5-2x more valid samples in the same window is not a fair apples-to-apples accuracy comparison.
- Decision / next step: this is a materially stronger, better-supported result than the first attempt and confirms Step 11's direction decisively: guided matching, evaluated the way it's meant to be used (with a real prior pose going into a hidden window), roughly doubles-to-triples availability in the clip's typical regions, not just the one window the first attempt happened to land on. Still recommend `features.guided_matching.enabled` stay off as the shipped *default* for now (p95/survivor-bias accuracy question not yet fully resolved, and the physical/live Step 8 acceptance test hasn't run), but this is now a strong, reproducible case to discuss with Sahaj about enabling it for the next physical acceptance attempt.

## 8. Quick command reference

```powershell
# tests
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests -v
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe .\april-tag-projection.py --self-check

# comparison on the fixed clip (new output name every time)
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m ar_projection.experiments compare .\runs\20261019T091439-6ea9d598 --config config-exp.json --output .\runs\<name>.json

# one tag-withheld landmark replay
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe .\april-tag-projection.py --config config-exp.json --replay-run .\runs\20261019T091439-6ea9d598 --mapping-mode localize --pose-mode landmark_only --withhold-tag --headless

# summarize a run
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m ar_projection.experiments summarize .\runs\<run-id> --output .\runs\<name>-summary.json

# live localization with existing map
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe .\april-tag-projection.py --config config-logitech.json --mapping-mode localize
```

## 9. Notes for a new Claude session (not Claude Code)

### 2026-10-23, New map `data/scene2-landmark-map.npz`: offline evaluation on its own build recording
- What changed: `config-logitech.json` now carries the best settings found (guided on, geometry-first, radius 12, Hamming 128, `geometry_margin` 1.5; old file kept as `config-logitech-baseline.json`) and a stricter build gate (`min_ready_landmarks` 250, `max_keyframes` 80). New script `scripts/step27_newmap.py` (output `runs/step27-newmap.json`). Map built by run `runs/20261022T234738-1bcd470d` (1,325 frames, 108 s). SHA-256 starts `7bfaee2774db55b1`.
- Map statistics against the old map (`data/logitech-landmark-map.npz`): 263 landmarks (old 95); extent 1.22 x 1.32 x 0.11 m (old 0.83 x 0.96 x 0.10), 101 occupied 5 cm voxels (old 39); landmark reprojection RMS median 0.51 px, p90 0.90 (old 0.44, 0.86); support min 3, median 4 (old 3, 3); stored-descriptor pairwise Hamming median 110 (old 114), fraction under 40 bits 0.5%. Still nearly planar (0.11 m depth extent).
- Tracking on the build recording itself (optimistic upper bound, not independent): the tag is valid in only 39% of frames, so only 3 non-overlapping withhold windows (25 s) fit. Joint hand-off windows: guided off 50.4% supported (median 0.72 px, p95 3.9 px), guided on with the config settings 45.2% (median 0.54 px, p95 5.0 px, none over 10 px); the 10 s window at 3222 s is 0% in both. Whole clip, landmark-only, tag withheld: 23.2% valid, reference median 0.99 px, p95 11.6 px (832 reference samples). By 10 s bin (landmark valid / tag valid / mean mapped observations): 0-10 s 0.89 / 1.00 / 236; 10-20 s 0.63 / 1.00 / 156; 20-30 s 0.86 / 0.08 / 100 (landmarks carry the pose while the tag is almost always absent); 30-40 s 0.06 / 0.00 / 50; 40-50 s 0.36 / 0.31 / 48; 50-108 s 0.00 / mostly low / 6-22.
- Interpretation: the new map is larger, wider and as accurate in build terms, and it gives landmark-only tracking with the tag absent in the first 30 s of its own recording (63-89%). Beyond about 30-50 s the mapped observations collapse to under 25 and tracking is 0%, which is consistent with the camera leaving the mapped region (the map was frozen earlier; recording continued). Guided matching did not help on this map (45% against 50% with it off), unlike on the old map; single recording, three windows, so treat it as unresolved.
- Decision / next step: record an independent validation clip with the tag visible most of the time, staying inside the mapped area, with locked camera settings (WORKFLOW section 2 command with `--map data/scene2-landmark-map.npz`), then rerun `scripts/step27_newmap.py <run> data/scene2-landmark-map.npz` on it. Until then the numbers above are a build-set upper bound.

- What changed: created `.venv-depth` (`requirements-depth.txt`: numpy/opencv/scipy + `torch==2.14.1+cu130`/`torchvision==0.29.1+cu130`, installed via plain `pip install torch torchvision`, which already resolved a CUDA build on this machine — no separate CUDA-specific index needed). Added `scripts/step27_depth_env.py`: loads `MetricDepth` from `ar_projection.occlusion` against the existing checkpoint, times inference at the three candidate input sizes on 100 frames of `runs/20261019T091439-6ea9d598/input.avi` (rectified the same way the live pipeline would, via `ar_projection.camera.Capture` on `config-logitech.json` pointed at the recording), and checks output units against the tag's true camera-Z from the already-recorded `raw_pose` in `frames.jsonl`. Also created a plain `.venv` (not previously present on this machine) from `requirements.txt` to verify the normal path. No `ar_projection` source changes.
- Runs (IDs / output files): `runs/step27-depth-env.json`.
- Numbers (quote from `runs/step27-depth-env.json`): checkpoint `/home/sahaj/Desktop/Studies/compvis/Depth-Anything-V2/metric_depth/checkpoints/depth_anything_v2_vits.pth`, SHA-256 `715fade13be8f229f8a70cc02066f656f2423a59effd0579197bbf57860e1378` (loads into the metric DPT head with `max_depth=20`, matching this checkpoint's own `metric_depth/run.py` default args — i.e. this is the Hypersim metric checkpoint, not the relative-depth one, confirmed by key-compatible loading, not just the filename). `torch 2.14.1+cu130`, CUDA 13.0 runtime on driver-reported CUDA 13.2, GPU `NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3050 Ti Laptop GPU`. Inference per frame (95 timed, 5 warm-up excluded, excluding/including explicit `torch.cuda.synchronize()` are within noise of each other — the model call itself already blocks): input 252 → mean 18.4 ms / p95 20.7 ms, peak 141 MB; 392 → mean 46.0 ms / p95 46.6 ms, peak 195 MB; 518 → mean 95.4 ms / p95 96.4 ms, peak 284 MB. Units check on frame 0 (tag valid, `raw_pose.source=="tag"`): tag true camera-Z 1.143 m; model's raw output at the tag's own projected pixel (387, 373) is 19.87 (same arbitrary units, image-wide range 9.88-19.97, median 14.35); implied single-frame scale factor true/model ≈0.0575. Normal-path check: `.venv/bin/python april-tag-projection.py --self-check` → `Projection geometry OK`; `import torch` in `.venv` → `ModuleNotFoundError` (confirmed absent); `python -m unittest discover -s tests` in `.venv` → 75/75 pass.
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): data, not assumption. The environment, checkpoint and GPU path all work as the plan expected, and speed is well inside a usable budget at every candidate size (even 518 at ~95 ms/frame leaves room if run less than every frame later). The units check confirms what Step 30's design already assumes rather than contradicts it: this checkpoint's raw output is **not** metric on this camera/scene out of the box — it needs roughly a 17x scale-down (1/0.0575) here, a single-frame number from one tag view, not a calibrated constant. This is exactly why Step 30 calibrates `s = median(Z_true/Z_pred)` from the tag instead of trusting the model's units directly; nothing here should be read as "the model is broken."
- Decision / next step: environment check passes; proceed to Step 28 (capture plan — needs Sahaj to record the occlusion clips; nothing to build or run without those).

### 2026-10-24, Step 29 continued, V3 re-record + guided on/off comparison
- What changed: Sahaj re-recorded V3 (`runs/20261024T054241-016203dd`, replacing `runs/20261024T052447-fb93e58b`; `scripts/step29_clips.py`'s `CLIPS` entry updated to point at it). Added `guided_variant()` to the same script (landmark_only + withhold-tag, guided matching forced on/off, everything else unchanged) and ran it on V1/V2 for the comparison this step's second Task bullet asks for.
- Numbers: new V3 live-joint 88.2% valid, 3 real tag-hidden intervals (1.65s/0.7s/5.23s, landmark-supported 66.7%/16.7%/95.7%). Standalone landmark-only + withhold-tag on new V3: `valid_without_tag_fraction` 0.162 (up from 0.0 on the old recording), reference median/p95 5.53/41.3px (2068→1260 samples), `landmark_gate_reasons` still dominated by `PnP RANSAC failed` (1184/2074, down from 1444/1469 ≈ universal) plus `insufficient RANSAC inliers` (498); `mapped_median` 67 associated landmarks/frame (healthy, same pattern as before: plentiful associations, RANSAC still can't find a consensus most of the time). Guided on/off on V1: guided-on (current default) 84.6% valid / 2.20px median / 6.04px p95 vs guided-off 77.2% / 2.47px / 8.74px — guided-on clearly better on both availability and accuracy. Guided on/off on V2: guided-on 76.8% / 2.43px / 7.30px vs guided-off 78.2% / 2.67px / 8.97px — guided-off has marginally higher availability (+1.4 pp) but worse accuracy on both median and p95; roughly a wash, mild edge to guided-on.

This is more evidence for the single-descriptor-per-landmark matching hypothesis from the 2026-10-20 Step 4 entries than for "wrong camera framing" — a genuinely different viewpoint range should have shown a different failure signature, not the same one at lower intensity. The guided-matching comparison supports keeping the current default: it's never clearly worse and is clearly better on V1, consistent with the Step 24 decision carrying over to this new map.

### 2026-10-24, Step 30, tag-plane scale calibration (first pass)
- What changed: added `occlusion.tag_plane_scale(T_CW, raw_depth, tag_size_m, camera, erode_px=3)` — reuses the existing `panel_layer()` ray-plane code with the tag's own corners (`geometry.tag_points`) instead of the panel's, erodes the valid mask by `erode_px` to avoid blurred tag edges, returns `median(Z_true/Z_pred)` over the surviving pixels (`None` if too few survive). 2 new tests (`test_tag_plane_scale_recovers_a_known_scale`, `test_tag_plane_scale_is_none_without_enough_valid_pixels`); 77/77 tests pass. The existing live `DepthPrototype.render`'s landmark-anchor scale logic was deliberately left untouched — this is offline-only, ahead of the live gate (Step 35). New script `scripts/step30_scale.py` (`.venv-depth`, input size 252): for V0/V1/V2, for every tag-visible+valid-pose frame, runs the depth model and computes `tag_plane_scale`; reports median and relative MAD per clip, and for V2 a zero-order-hold drift check (last tag-derived scale just before each real tag-hidden interval vs. the fresh one right after — the simplest possible hold, before adding any landmark-anchor update). Output `runs/step30-scale.json`.
- Numbers: V0 (1588/2168 tag-visible frames) scale median 0.0467, relative MAD 6.59%. V1 (1603/1813) median 0.0449, MAD 6.90%. V2 (669/1977) median 0.0451, MAD 6.65% — all three land in the same 0.045-0.047 band (consistent with Step 27's single-frame estimate of ≈0.0575 on a different recording/distance — same order of magnitude, not identical, as expected since scale is scene/distance-dependent for an unscaled relative-ish metric model). V2 zero-order-hold drift across its 9 real tag-hidden intervals (durations 1.25-11.55s, full list in `runs/step30-scale.json`): 2.15%, 6.22%, 0.008%, 1.22%, 0.23%, 1.03%, 1.80%, 2.16%, 1.65% — 8 of 9 intervals under 2.2%, the one outlier (6.22%, the 4.4s gap) is the same interval Step 29 flagged as only 5.9% landmark-supported.
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): data, not assumption. Scale is stable enough (~6.6-6.9% relative MAD) that even a trivial "freeze the last tag-derived value and do nothing else" hold already keeps drift under 6.2% across gaps as long as 11.5 s — this is genuinely good news and means the fuller landmark-anchor adaptive hold-phase Step 30 describes may be a refinement rather than a necessity, at least for gaps this short; that's a hypothesis to check once the landmark-anchor version is built, not a conclusion to skip building it. Direct arithmetic on the margin question Step 30's Report line asks for: a 5%/10%/measured-6.6% scale error implies a real-world depth error of `error_fraction × panel_depth`, compared against the 0.02 m margin — at 0.3 m: 0.015 m (75% of margin) / 0.030 m (150%) / 0.0198 m (99%, using the measured MAD); at 0.5 m: 0.025 m (125%) / 0.050 m (250%) / 0.033 m (165%); at 1.0 m: 0.050 m (250%) / 0.100 m (500%) / 0.066 m (330%). Even the *measured* scale noise alone, with no other error source, already consumes essentially the entire 0.02 m margin at 0.3 m and badly exceeds it at 0.5 m and 1.0 m. A fixed 0.02 m margin cannot be trusted at the capture plan's full 0.3-1.0 m distance range without either much tighter scale accuracy than currently measured, or a depth-proportional margin.
- Decision / next step: flagged to Sahaj whether Step 31's margin sweep needs a depth-proportional option given the scale-noise-vs-margin arithmetic above. **Decided (Sahaj, 2026-10-24): keep the fixed 0.01/0.02/0.05 m sweep as planned, no depth-proportional margin.** Reasoning: the margin only matters right at the boundary between "real surface at the panel's own depth" and "real surface meaningfully in front of it"; the realistic occluder (a hand) is expected to create a large depth gap when it actually covers the panel, not a borderline few-cm one, so scale noise at 0.5-1.0 m is a second-order concern unless Step 32/34's boundary/flicker scores say otherwise. Revisit only if labelled scoring at distance shows margin-driven false hides/misses. Remaining Step 30 work not yet done: landmark-anchor hold-phase (live-reusable, offline-tested), scale+offset ablation, V1 hand-presence split (needs timing labels Sahaj hasn't provided), gate-pass-fraction reporting.
