- What changed: added `invalid_occludes` to `panel_layer()` (parameterizes the existing invalid-depth policy instead of hardcoding it: default unchanged — unknown depth stays visible — `invalid_occludes=True` flips it to occluded) and `margin_m`/`invalid_occludes` constructor params on `DepthPrototype`, threaded into its existing `panel_layer()` call; nothing else in the class changed. 1 new test for the policy toggle (both directions); 78/78 tests pass. New `scripts/step31_masks.py` calls `ar_projection.pipeline.run()` directly with `occlusion=DepthPrototype(...)` — this bypasses `cli.py`'s tracking-acceptance gate, which is a live-only (Step 35) requirement; Steps 30-34 are offline and exempt per Step 35's own gate note, and no tracking-acceptance report exists for the new map. Primary pass: V0-V4 at the default margin (0.02 m), input size 252. Secondary (V1 only): margin 0.01/0.05 m, and `invalid_occludes=True`. New `scripts/step31_contact_sheets.py` composes image/mask/virtual-Z-heatmap contact sheets from the exported files (no new rendering logic, reuses the existing export format exactly). One bug hit and fixed: the summary writer tried to `json.dumps()` a `Path` object (`tracking_run`); fixed with `str()`; all 8 variants' GPU export had already completed successfully before the crash, so only the summary needed recomputing, not a rerun.
- Runs/numbers: `runs/step31-masks/{V0,V1,V2,V3,V4,V1-margin0.01,V1-margin0.05,V1-invalid-occludes}/` (1888/1623/1628/1830/1157/1623/1623/1623 frames respectively — matches each clip's live valid-pose fraction) plus `summary.json` and 8 contact sheets. Mean per-frame fraction of panel pixels occluded (active frames only): V0 0.0% (no occluder clip, correctly near-zero), V1 0.171%, V2 0.126%, V3 1.487%, V4 1.256%. Margin sweep on V1: 0.01 m -> 0.288% mean occluded, 0.02 m (default) -> 0.171%, 0.05 m -> 0.009% — monotonic and consistent with the mask rule (`real < panel - margin`: a bigger margin makes the inequality harder to satisfy, so fewer pixels occlude), confirming the margin knob behaves as designed. `invalid_occludes=True` vs default on V1: **0 of 1623 frames differ** — this checkpoint never produces invalid/non-finite depth over the panel region on these clips, so the policy toggle exists and is tested but is currently a no-op on real data (not a bug — nothing to occlude differently).
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): visually confirmed both a correct and an incorrect case, not just aggregate numbers. (1) **Real occlusion works**: V2 frame 72 (`runs/step31-masks/V2/000072-image.png`/`.png`) shows a hand entering frame directly in front of the tag/panel area, and the mask correctly blacks out most of the panel (37% of its pixels) with a small sliver left visible exactly where the hand doesn't reach — this is the mask doing its job. (2) **False occlusion also happens, with nothing in front of the camera at all**: V3 frames ~20-30 (`.../V3/000026-image.png` etc., up to 73% of the panel falsely hidden) and V4 frames ~1270-1280 (up to 55%, `.../V4/001276-image.png`) show a clean, empty scene — table, tag, objects, no hand or occluder anywhere — yet the mask hides most of the panel. V3's case lines up with its already-known broken landmark tracking (Step 29); V4's does not (V4's live/landmark-only numbers were otherwise healthy), so this is a second, independent source of false occlusion beyond V3's tracking problem, and it clusters at the very start (V3) or very end (V4) of its clip, which may point at bootstrap/warm-up instability in the depth or scale pipeline rather than a mid-clip issue — a hypothesis, not confirmed. The clip-mean occlusion fractions (V3/V4 at 1.0-1.5%, roughly 10x V0/V1/V2's) are consistent with this being a real, if currently infrequent, failure mode rather than noise in my measurement.
- Decision / next step: Step 32's labelling needs to specifically cover these false-occlusion windows (V3 early, V4 late), not just the intended occluder passes, so the false-hide rate is actually measured rather than averaged away. Flagging to Sahaj rather than digging further unprompted: diagnosing why these specific frames produce bad per-pixel depth (vs. the surrounding frames in the same clip, which look fine) is a reasonable next investigation if the false-hide rate turns out to matter at Step 32/34, but V1/V2's overall numbers are clean enough to proceed to Step 32 as planned.

### 2026-10-24, Step 31 continued, root cause of the two false-occlusion cases (Sahaj asked to see the actual depth map)
- What changed: new `scripts/step31_depth_inspect.py` re-runs the model on one exported frame and renders it directly (rectified image | real scaled depth heatmap with the panel's own pixel region outlined | virtual panel Z on the same colour scale | mask), rather than only the aggregate numbers from the main pass. No pipeline/occlusion code changed. Ad hoc, not part of the regular export.
- Numbers (V2 frame 72, V3 frame 26, V4 frame 1276, matching the earlier false-occlusion report): V2 real-depth range in frame 0.51-0.97 m, panel median real 0.836 m vs virtual 0.823 m (median close, occlusion driven by the sub-region under the hand, not a uniform offset). V3 real-depth range 0.56-0.94 m, panel median real 0.781 m vs virtual 0.838 m (real reads 5.7 cm nearer across most of the panel). V4 real-depth range 0.56-0.98 m, panel median real 0.654 m vs virtual 0.680 m (2.6 cm nearer).
- Interpretation: visually inspecting the actual depth map (not just the mask) identifies two distinct, position-dependent causes, neither a scale error (both scales were in the normal ~0.047-0.049 band): (1) **V3**: the model's depth over the flat table/background has a smooth top-to-bottom gradient unrelated to the true (flat) geometry there -- a known monocular-depth prior ("lower in frame reads nearer"), and the panel happens to sit on the nearer-reading part of that gradient. (2) **V4**: the panel sits right at the top image border, next to a visible depth "halo" around the headphones' silhouette -- a boundary/edge artifact where the model has the least surrounding context. Both are *where the panel lands on screen*, not a global scale or pose problem -- a more specific finding than the earlier "scale noise" framing in Step 30, and arguably a bigger practical risk for a fixed-margin rule than random per-frame noise would be, since it will recur deterministically whenever the panel's projected position revisits a weak region of the frame (near edges, or over flat low-texture surfaces).
- Decision / next step: flagging to Sahaj, not deciding unilaterally -- this suggests Step 32's labelling (or a quick follow-up check) could usefully record *where on screen* the panel was during false-hide frames, not just when, since the failure looks position-triggered rather than random. No code change proposed yet; this is diagnostic only.

### 2026-10-24, Step 31 correction, V2/72 was NOT a confirmed-correct example, and a real miss found (Sahaj asked for the overlay directly on the photo)
- What changed: **correcting the previous entry.** Sahaj pointed out the virtual-panel-Z heatmap's internal colour gradient looked like it ran the opposite direction from the real-depth heatmap's gradient under the panel outline. Checking the exact numbers at the panel's 4 corners showed the mask *is* self-consistent with the numbers it's given (every corner's visible/occluded state matches `real < virtual - margin` exactly) -- so there was no bug in the comparison logic. But overlaying the mask directly on the rectified photo (rather than comparing two side-by-side heatmaps) showed the earlier "confirmed correct occlusion" claim for V2 frame 72 was wrong: the panel there sits over bare table, the hand is well clear of it, and the 37% occluded region is a **third false positive**, not a validation. Searching inside one of V2's known tag-hidden intervals (Step 29, source_time ~8395s -> frame 441) found a clean counter-example instead: the hand is visibly on top of the tag/panel, and the mask shows **0% occlusion** -- a false negative (miss).
- Numbers (frame 441): virtual panel Z median ~0.72 m. Real (scaled) depth under the hand: ~1.17-1.24 m -- *farther* than the panel, not closer. The model's raw (unscaled) output at the hand's own pixels: 18.6-19.8, essentially pinned near its ceiling (`max_depth=20`), span of only 1.2 units across the whole panel region. A clear background-table pixel in the same frame, just outside the hand: raw 18.76 -- almost the same as the hand's 19.2-19.7, hand even slightly higher (backwards, if larger=farther). `metric_scale` that frame: 0.0628, from 45 landmark anchors agreeing within 8.1% MAD (a trustworthy fit for the background).
- Sahaj asked two follow-ups rather than accepting this at face value, both tested directly: (1) **margin removed** (`margin_m=0.0` instead of 0.02) on the full V2 clip, 1628 frames, via `DepthPrototype(..., margin_m=0.0)` -- mean occluded fraction 0.126%->0.167%, frames-with-any-occlusion 36->37, frame-to-frame pixel flip rate 0.0218%->0.0258%. Negligible change; frame 441 specifically stays at 0.0% occluded even with the margin fully removed. (2) **scale tuned to fix frame 441**: the arithmetic a scale of ~0.0352 would place the hand at ~0.68 m (correctly occluded), but applying that same scale to the frame's background anchors (raw~19, currently placed at ~1.19 m by the trusted 0.0628 scale) would put them at ~0.67 m -- roughly half their real distance. One multiplicative scale cannot be simultaneously right for both; the raw signal itself doesn't separate "hand at ~0.2-0.3 m" from "table at ~1.2 m" in this frame.
- Interpretation: neither proposed fix works, and both results point the same direction -- this is not a margin problem (removing it changed almost nothing) and not a scale-calibration problem (no single scalar can satisfy both the hand and the background anchors in the same frame from the raw values actually produced). The raw model output itself fails to discriminate a large, close, skin-toned occluder from normal room-scale background in this frame. Plausible cause, not confirmed: Hypersim (the training set) is room-scale indoor imagery and likely has few or no close-up hand/macro examples, so the model has no learned prior for "something a few cm from the lens" and defaults toward a generic mid-range value.
- Decision / next step: flagging to Sahaj -- Step 33's planned 392/518 input-size comparison is the next concrete thing to try (more input detail might relieve the saturation), but if this is a training-distribution gap rather than a resolution gap, a bigger input size won't fix it either, and that would need to be treated as a known limitation of this checkpoint rather than something to keep tuning around. Not deciding which to assume; recommend treating "does the hand-occlusion case work at all" as its own explicit question for Step 32's labelling rather than inferring it from aggregate IoU.

### 2026-10-24, Step 30/31 continued, affine scale fit made real (production code), then found to regress, then partially recovered with Huber loss
- What changed (several rounds in one session, logged together): (1) Replaced `DepthPrototype`'s scale-only `median(ratios)` with an affine fit `real_m = (raw-a)/b` fit per-frame from the same landmark anchors (`occlusion.fit_affine_huber`, initially plain `np.polyfit`), still held as a 5-frame median, same `>=12` anchor / `<=0.25` relative-error gate. 2 new tests (invalid-depth-policy toggle, affine-fit exact recovery on synthetic data); 79/79 then 80/80 pass as tests were added. Verified via `scripts/step31_masks.py` end-to-end (not just the exploratory scripts) that production code reproduces the exploratory numbers. (2) Rerunning the full V0-V4 export (not just the 4 hand-picked frames validated earlier) found a severe regression: mean occluded fraction on V0 (the no-occluder baseline, should be exactly 0%) rose from 0.0% to 10.07%, and similarly worsened on V1-V4 -- the plain least-squares fit is not robust to a handful of bad anchors, confirmed on V0 frame 1918 (92% falsely occluded; fit mean residual 0.285 m against its own anchors, vs 0.065 m on a known-good frame). (3) Swapped `np.polyfit` for `scipy.optimize.least_squares(loss="huber")` (`fit_affine_huber`, f_scale set from the initial OLS fit's own MAD, reusing the same robust-loss tool already used for pose estimation in `pose.py`) -- halved the regression in most clips (V0 10.07%->5.29%, V1 7.09%->3.09%, V3 19.57%->12.35%) but did not fix it; V0 was still 5.29% mean / 364 of 1888 frames with >10% false occlusion.
- Numbers (full detail: comparing 45 anchors' individual `depth_fit_a`/`depth_fit_b` across V0-V4, from the Huber-fit export): intercept `a` is stable (2-4% coefficient of variation across each clip). Slope `b` is not: 15-25% CV, and in V3 ranges all the way from -10.24 to **-0.71** (near zero) within one clip. Since `real_m=(raw-a)/b`, a slope near zero makes the conversion arbitrarily sensitive to raw-value noise -- this is the direct mechanism behind the worst false-occlusion frames, not anchor-count or Huber-catchable outliers (the bad V0 frames had a *good* relative-error score, 0.058, well inside the 0.25 gate -- the gate measures fit-at-anchors agreement, not accuracy-at-the-panel's-specific-depth, and those are different things when the anchors and the panel aren't at the same distance).
- Also tested and rejected an inverse-depth hypothesis (`raw = c + s/Z` instead of linear in `Z`) on the two best-understood frames (V0/1918 known-bad, V2/441 known-miss): the straight-line-in-Z fit beat the straight-line-in-1/Z fit on both (R² 0.235 vs 0.020, and 0.905 vs 0.834) -- not an accidental workaround, the data genuinely prefers the linear form over this checkpoint's output.
- Decision / next step: this round is **not shippable as the live default** -- still a net regression against the original proportional model on the one clip (V0) that must read zero. Sahaj proposed and this session is now testing a different hypothesis before further fit/temporal work: that the checkpoint used throughout Steps 27-31 was the wrong file (see the Step 27b entry below).

### 2026-10-24, Step 27b, wrong-checkpoint hypothesis tested: confirmed at the raw-signal level, not confirmed at the mask level
- What changed: Sahaj had downloaded `depth_anything_v2_metric_hypersim_vits.pth` (SHA-256 `b782898d...`) into the same `checkpoints/` directory as the file used throughout Steps 27-31, `depth_anything_v2_vits.pth` (SHA-256 `715fade1...`, confirmed differs, confirmed matches Step 27's recorded hash). Added `DepthPrototype.slope_sign` (default -1, logged above) so a checkpoint with the physically-correct positive slope can be accepted instead of rejected by the sanity gate. Added `--checkpoint` to the 3 relevant scripts (logged above). New `scripts/step27b_checkpoint.py`: runs both checkpoints on the same 100 frames of `runs/20261019T091439-6ea9d598/input.avi` (Step 27's original recording, old map `data/logitech-landmark-map.npz`, replayed through the real `Pipeline` so landmark anchors are available, not just the tag). New `scripts/step27b_fit_diagnostics.py`: per-frame affine fit, in-sample and leave-one-out anchor error, and a scale-only (no intercept) alternative, on a 200-frame sample each of V1/V2 with the new checkpoint. Reused `scripts/step31_masks.py --checkpoint ... --slope-sign 1 --output-root runs/step27b-masks` for the mask re-export, unchanged otherwise (same margin, input size, clips, maps). No tuning performed, per this step's own instruction.
- Numbers -- raw signal (100 frames, `runs/step27b-checkpoint.json`): old checkpoint 45.0% of pixels saturated within 1 unit of the 20 ceiling, tag-plane ratio `Z_true/Z_pred` median 0.057 (17x off), landmark-anchor regression slope -0.98 (disparity-like, raw *falls* with true distance), R²=0.69 (n=3347 anchor samples). New checkpoint: 0.01% saturated, tag-plane ratio median 0.49 (2x off, not the hoped-for ~1.0 but a 34x improvement in gross error), landmark-anchor slope +1.46 (metric-like, raw correctly *rises* with distance), R²=0.91.
- Numbers -- fit diagnostics, new checkpoint, 200-frame samples (`runs/step27b-fit-diagnostics.json`): V1 (61 anchors/frame mean) `a`=0.18±0.16, `b`=1.51±0.16 (10.6% CV -- about half the 15-25% CV measured on the old checkpoint in the prior entry), in-sample mean anchor error 0.055 m, leave-one-out 0.057 m (close agreement, not overfitting), scale-only alternative 0.057 m (statistically the same as the full affine fit -- the intercept buys nothing on V1 with this checkpoint). V2 (41 anchors/frame mean) `a`=-0.12±0.40, `b`=1.71±0.39 (23% CV, noisier), in-sample 0.097 m, leave-one-out 0.106 m, scale-only 0.127 m (here the intercept *does* help, cutting error by about 25%).
- Numbers -- mask re-export, new checkpoint, `slope_sign=1`, same margin (0.02 m)/input size (252)/clips as Step 31 (`runs/step27b-masks/`): mean occluded fraction of the panel, active frames only -- V0 **3.97%** (old-checkpoint-proportional baseline: 0.0%), V1 3.09% (0.17%), V2 6.82% (0.13%), V3 11.01% (1.49%), V4 21.03% (1.26%). V1 margin 0.05 m: 1.10% (was 0.009% with the old checkpoint/proportional fit). The specific named frames: V3 frames 0-60 mean 14.8% occluded (max 86.8% at frame 26 -- the exact same frame flagged as a false hide in Step 31); V4 frames 1240-1300 mean 3.4% (down from the single-frame 55% spike logged in Step 31, the clearest improvement found); V2 frame 72 (hand clear of panel) 0.0%, correct; **V2 frame 441 (hand squarely on the panel) 0.0%, still a complete miss**, visually confirmed with a photo overlay (identical in kind to the Step 31 overlay -- hand fills the frame, panel outline sits over it, nothing tinted).
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): the wrong-checkpoint hypothesis is **confirmed at the level it makes a direct, falsifiable prediction about** -- saturation, slope sign and R² all moved exactly as predicted, by a lot, not a little. It is **not confirmed as a fix for the mask** -- V3 frame 26's false hide and V2 frame 441's miss are both numerically unchanged (not just "still present," the V3 number got *worse* than Step 31's old-checkpoint-Huber-affine figures in the clip means, though the single worst frame is identical). The most informative new data point is the V1 fit-diagnostics result: anchor-level calibration is now good (5-6 cm mean error, stable 10.6% slope CV) on a clip where the mask itself still over-occludes by 3%. That decouples two things this session had been treating as one problem: anchor-fit quality (now demonstrably good, at least on V1) and panel-location accuracy (still bad on the specific adversarial frames). The false hide at V3/26 and the miss at V2/441 both look like the position-dependent, per-pixel model behaviour already diagnosed in the "root cause of the two false-occlusion cases" entry above (flat-region gradient prior; large/close/skin-toned objects not resolved) -- i.e. a correct checkpoint does not fix a problem that was never really about calibration in the first place.
- Decision table rows that apply (both, simultaneously): row 2, "raw is sane but false hides remain" -- the listed next options (patch-median anchors with edge rejection, per-frame margin from leave-one-out error, mask-level gating/hysteresis, spatial plane correction) are the queued candidates, none attempted here. Row 4, "hand at frame 441 still reads as far as the table with a sane model" -- record as a limit of this checkpoint/resolution at close range for a large skin-toned occluder, not a calibration bug; next options per that row are 392/518 input (Step 33), a hand-segmentation mask used only for front/behind, or Video Depth Anything. Not deciding between any of these; flagging to Sahaj. The new checkpoint should still become the default going forward regardless (it is strictly better on every raw-signal measure and at least as good on every mask measure except the clip means, which were already not shippable) -- but switching it does not by itself clear Step 27b's goal of fixing the known false-hide/miss frames, so Step 32 (labelling) should not proceed yet on the assumption that it does.

### 2026-10-24, Step 27b continued, decision-table item (a) tried, and a pattern across three independent fixes
- What changed: new checkpoint (now the default: `CHECKPOINT` constants in `step27_depth_env.py`/`step30_scale.py`/`step31_masks.py` updated; `step31_masks.py`'s `--slope-sign` default flipped to `+1` to match, so checkpoint and sign can't silently mismatch). Implemented decision-table item (a), "patch-median anchors with edge rejection": new `occlusion.anchor_sample(raw, x, y, radius=2, edge_range=.5)` -- a 5x5-patch median instead of a single pixel, returns `None` (reject the anchor) if the patch spans more than `edge_range` raw units (likely straddling a depth edge); `edge_range=.5` is marked `ponytail:` as an uncalibrated first guess. Wired into `DepthPrototype.render()`'s anchor-collection loop, replacing the single-pixel read. "Reprojection-consistent landmarks only" (the other half of item (a)) needed no new code -- `pipeline.py` already clears `f.landmark_id` on any feature whose reprojection exceeds `pose.ransac_reprojection_threshold_px`, before `DepthPrototype.render()` ever sees it, confirmed by reading the existing flow rather than assumed. 2 new tests (`anchor_sample`'s patch-median and edge-rejection behaviour directly; existing affine-fit tests' synthetic stubs updated from single-pixel spikes to small flat patches so they survive the new edge check). 81/81 tests pass. Re-ran the full `step31_masks.py` export with the new defaults to `runs/step27b-patch-median/`.
- Numbers (vs `runs/step27b-masks/`, same checkpoint, before this change): mean occluded fraction V0 3.97%->4.70%, V1 3.09%->3.31%, V2 6.82%->**12.38%** (nearly doubled), V3 11.01%->8.92%, V4 21.03%->21.48%. The two named failure frames specifically: V3 frame 26 86.8%->87.6% (unchanged within noise); V2 frame 441 0.0%->0.0% (exactly unchanged); V2 frame 72 and V3 frames 0-60's clip mean (14.76%->14.75%) also essentially unchanged.
- Interpretation: item (a) is a net negative on V2 and does not move either named failure frame. More importantly, this is now the **third** independent fix tried against the false-hide/miss problem -- Huber-robust fitting (prior entry), the correct metric checkpoint (prior entry), and patch-median anchors with edge rejection (this entry) -- and none of the three has moved V3 frame 26 or V2 frame 441 by a meaningful amount, even though two of the three measurably improved other things (anchor-fit quality, raw-signal sanity). Three different interventions on anchor/calibration quality producing zero effect on the specific failure frames is itself evidence: it says the false-hide and the miss are not calibration problems, consistent with the earlier per-pixel diagnosis (flat-region depth gradient at V3's panel location; large/close/skin-toned object not resolved at V2/441) that none of these three fixes could have touched, since all three only change how anchors *elsewhere* in the frame are turned into a scale/line -- they do not change what the model predicts *at the panel's own pixels*.
- Decision / next step: stopping the row-2 (anchor/fit) line of attack here rather than continuing to items (b)-(d), which share the same assumption the last three results argue against. Flagging to Sahaj rather than picking: the two paths that remain live are row 4's options (392/518 input size, a hand-segmentation-assisted mask, or documenting this as a checkpoint/resolution limit) -- or a fresh, direct diagnosis of why V3's panel location specifically reads close in this checkpoint's output (not yet done; the "root cause" entry above diagnosed the old checkpoint's behaviour at that location, not this one). Keeping the patch-median/edge-rejection code (it's harmless, tested, and the "reprojection-consistent" half is a genuine correctness note) but not treating it as having solved anything.

### 2026-10-25, Step 36, depth-source comparison -- the fresh direct diagnosis, and a decisive answer
- What changed: verified the table-plane assumption before anything else, per the step's own requirement, using the full landmark map rather than only 3 frames: 78% of 502 mapped points have `|world Z| < 0.05 m`, versus 6% for `|Y|` and 12% for `|X|` -- confirms world `Z=0` is the table plane (the tag lies flat, face up). Added `occlusion.plane_depth(T_CW, point_world, normal_world, camera)` (logged in section 6) -- the oracle: analytic camera-Z of the infinite `Z=0` plane at every pixel, reusing `panel_layer`'s ray-plane algebra but not clipped to a small quad. New `scripts/step36_depth_sources.py`: for 79 selected frames across all 5 clips (V3 frame 26 + 20-30; V4 frame 1276 + 1270-1280; V2 frame 441 + 72 + a 431-451 window; V0 worst-5 + random-5; 5 zero-occlusion controls per clip), computed A (model depth, raw and scaled by the *production* fit, reusing the already-exported `(a,b)` from `runs/step27b-patch-median/` rather than refitting), B (the pose the production export actually used, read directly from its `frames.jsonl`'s `render_T_CW` -- no replay needed), C (a tag-only pose refit per frame, and the landmark-only pose, both obtained from a single `--withhold-tag` replay per clip reusing `pipeline.py`'s own existing `tag_reference_T_CW` diagnostic -- no new pose-fitting code written), and D (the oracle). Caught and fixed two real bugs in a V2-only smoke test before the full run: a classic Python `x or default` bug that silently turned every genuine `0.0` occluded-fraction into `1.0`, excluding all zero-occlusion control frames from selection; and a placeholder tag-exclusion mask that excluded nothing. `scripts/step36_figures.py`/`step36_summary.py` run under the system `python3` (has matplotlib; neither project venv does, and installing it there for 2 scripts would be a new dependency for a convenience the system interpreter already provides for free). 82/82 tests pass (1 new test for `plane_depth`, checked against `panel_layer` on the same plane and confirmed *not* clipped to a polygon).
- Numbers (full detail in `runs/step36-depth-sources.json`/`.md`, 79 frames, 79 figures in `runs/step36-depth-sources/`): **false-hide group** (V3/V4, occluded>10%, n=5) -- `oracle_vs_B`=0.0%, `oracle_vs_C`=0.0%, `model_vs_B`=40.8%, `model_vs_C`=42.3% (margin 0.02 m); B-vs-C pose agreement panel-Z median 1.8 cm (close, though 40% of these 5 frames exceed 2 cm); model-vs-oracle bias median -11.6 cm, row-band top -14.0 cm vs bottom -7.9 cm. **V0 control clip** (should always read 0%, n=15): `oracle_vs_B`=0.0%, `oracle_vs_C`=0.0%, `model_vs_B`=20.8%, `model_vs_C`=24.0%; pose agreement tight (panel-Z median 0.26 cm, 0% of frames exceed 2 cm); bias row-band top -29.7 cm vs bottom -1.4 cm. **Zero-occlusion controls across all 5 clips** (n=25, the cleanest baseline): masks all near zero either way (oracle_vs_B 1.1%, others <=2.2%), but the row-dependent bias is still present underneath (top -18.2 cm vs bottom +2.6 cm) even where it isn't yet large enough to misfire. **V2/441 hand window** (n=21): model depth under the hand (simple rectangular polygon, no Sahaj-drawn mask available, logged in the output) median 0.844 m vs the panel/oracle reference (borrowed from the nearest tag-valid neighbour, frame 373, since the tag is covered at 441 by construction) at 0.714/0.708 m -- **hand reads farther, not nearer**. Same window's `oracle_vs_B`=28.2% vs `oracle_vs_C`=0.0% (a real, smaller pose-sensitivity specific to this clip's landmark-only pose, worth noting separately from the model finding).
- Interpretation (observation vs hypothesis): this is the clean confirmation the three prior fixes (Huber loss, correct checkpoint, patch-median anchors) couldn't deliver, because none of them could touch it: comparing against true geometry (the oracle), pose is fine (0% false hides either way, on both V3/V4 *and* V0) and the model's own depth is not (20-42% false hides, same two clips). That is decision-table row "oracle gives no false hides in either, model does: model or fit problem confirmed" on two independent clips, not an isolated frame. The bias additionally has real spatial structure -- top-of-frame rows read 6-28 cm more biased than bottom-of-frame rows, *in every group tested including the clean controls* -- so it is a position-dependent property of the model's output on this scene, not a uniform offset a smarter per-frame fit could remove (matches decision-table row "model against oracle shows a structured bias... plane-like or row-dependent: model bias"). The hand frame gives the same verdict independently: with the *current production* fit (not an exploratory one), the model places the hand farther than the panel, confirming "hand reads far... a model limit at close range" from the decision table. One caveat found by inspection, not initially anticipated: at a few extreme camera angles (V0 frame 1780, where the view grazes the table near-horizontally) the oracle's own ray-plane math approaches infinity as the viewing ray nears parallel to the table, making a direct model-vs-oracle comparison at those specific pixels unreliable -- the group statistics are median-based and robust to this, and the representative examples sent to Sahaj (V3/26, V2/441) were chosen specifically because their oracle values are sane (0.3-1.6 m), but it is a real limitation of the infinite-plane oracle worth remembering before trusting any single grazing-angle frame at face value.
- Decision / next step: per the step's own instruction, no fix attempted here. Flagging to Sahaj with a clearer target than before: Step 36 has now ruled out pose as the cause of the false-hides on two clips, and identified the bias as structured (row-dependent) rather than uniform, which argues against another global per-frame scale/offset fit (already tried three ways) and toward either (a) the dense table-plane calibration the decision table itself names next (fit scale+offset+a low-order plane on table pixels every frame, instead of sparse landmark anchors), or (b) treating the row-dependent bias and the close-range hand blind spot as checkpoint/resolution limits per row 4 and moving to Step 33's 392/518 input-size test to see if either improves. Not picking between these.
